Strand 09 — DeOS, Mini, Nano, DelveTalk, Spween, preoscript and the Objective Bend convergence

Primary-source dossier, written 2026-11-02 from the repositories, git logs, memory files and review corpora named in each section. Every number below was read from a file or a git/wc command on 2026-11-02 unless a dated document is cited for it. Hellas is not part of this strand; where the memory record mentions it, it appears only as the boundary Ember drew ("that is a hellas thing, not a dregg thing", 10-01).

0. How to read this, and four corrections to the brief

0.2 Corrections to the brief (primary sources disagree with its framing)

  1. "horseshit" was said about DelveTalk v1, not about Mini's performance. The only memory hit for the word in the DreggNet/Mini/DelveTalk corpora is -Users-ember-dev-delvetalk/memory/ember-working-style.md:21: "Three hours of autonomous codex produced 45 commits and a 124K-line repo ember called 'horseshit' and 'chug'; the failure was orchestration and refusal to subtract, not effort." ~/dev/delvetalk is that repo: 83 commits, 2026-10-08 → 10-09, 125,146 tracked lines today. (A second hit, birkhoff-asa-state.md:267, is a different project.)
  2. Mini's performance failure is real but has two measured causes, neither of which is "functional programming" as such. (a) Immediate-mode re-execution: the bake-off measured Mini's write at 2.6 s → 13.0 s → 25.5 s at 10/100/155 records, "superlinear (a quadratic fit describes it) and the cost is in the compiled Lean host re-admitting from genesis" (claudesplosion/bakeoff/VERDICT.md); Ember's 10-31 diagnosis is "this current generation of code generation models keeps writing re-execution … the whole thing would be incremental and efficient and held 'retained-mode'" (feedback-retained-mode.md). (b) Concrete-import cascades: a codec pin regen rebuilt ~486 modules, a deep Theory edit 631 modules in 39 min; Ember: "there are HUGELY DIFFERENT WAYS we should have been structuring this code from the beginning. by now it's just ... slop" (feedback-abstraction-recover-forward.md, 10-28). The pure-functional List UInt8 representation is a contributing constant factor (4,798 lines of List UInt8 vs 329 of ByteArray, ARCHITECTURE-20261016.md §0.2), not the root.
  3. The bake-off is dated 09-29 (run) / 09-30 (verdict), and Mini did not "win" outright. Neither tree passed: Bread failed J5/J6/J8 (kernel semantics); Mini failed growth (storage/recovery). The verdict went to "the cheaper class of work": "Mini keeps the role it was given on 09-19." Ember afterwards: the Bread half was unnecessary ("I could have told you").
  4. "Opus 4.8" in the three-holes quote is a Discord participant handle (<@Opus4.8>) for "the dreggon", an Opus 4.8 Claude Code session working in the dregg repo whose replies Ember pasted into the multi-model Bridge room (bridge.md:68, :146). Opus 4.8 is also a real co-author in Mini's history (73 commits carry Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8).

2. breadstuffs DeOS — the root system

Repos. ~/dev/breadstuffs (10,359 commits; last 2026-10-01 22fad151e), docs/deos/ (250 design docs, e.g. DEOS.md, DOC-CELL-COMPOSITION.md, BRANCH-AND-STITCH-PROTOCOL.md, CROSS-DEVICE-FIRMAMENT.md, AOL-WONDER.md), DeOS crates at the workspace root.

2.1 What it is

Naming triad (DreggNet/memory/dregg-sel4-deos-firmament.md): robigalia the project, dregg the verified distributed object-capability kernel, deos the agentic desktop user layer ("dregg made visual; a window IS a Capability{Target::Surface(cell), rights}").

The firmament thesis (docs/FIRMAMENT.md, 494 lines; sel4/dregg-firmament/): an seL4 capability and a dregg capability are the same abstraction at different points of a distance parameter n; n=1 collapses distributed bounds to strong-local ones. Proved in metatheory/Dregg2/Firmament/{SeL4Abstract,CapGradation,SeL4Kernel,SeL4Composition}.lean, keystone dregg_turn_in_pd_preserves_both (a dregg delegation embedded as an seL4 mint preserves both the derivation-tree invariant and cap non-amplification). The 6-PD Microkit assembly (sel4/dregg.system): executor (links libdregg_lean_elf.a, runs execFullForestG) · verifier (Lean-free plonky3 STARK checker) · persist · net (virtio single-queue) · net_client (smoltcp + Ed25519 SignedTurn gate) · app.

Boot ladder (project-firmament-sel4-boots.md): M0 Rust PD prints "dregg robigalia v0"; M1 verifier PD; M2 rbg DirectoryCell PD; M-STARK real BabyBear+BLAKE3+FRI STARK proved and verified on-device; M5 riscv64; M3 virtio-net ◐. Live image viewer bbccc03ca; cockpit render on the seL4 framebuffer d92d0526c (06-19); live repaint on a genuine verified turn 251692b78/2824056ee (06-25).

The desktop epoch (project-deos-self-hosting-epoch.md, 06-22): editor+terminal dock panes a3f7ce07; dregg-doc Pijul patch core with Dregg2/Deos/DocMerge.lean f8184fbe; deos-matrix with membranes in messages f17b0d15; starbridge-v2/src/shared_fork.rs mint→rehydrate→drive→stitch 7414c820; deos-hermes confined agent 1de5c0ed; Settlement Soundness proved caa8c24ab (Circuit/SettlementSoundness.lean) and wired into production stitch_pair 3c0467262.

The purpose, Ember 06-19 (project-deos-ux-vision.md): "i am the person who needs this substrate. i'll be using this os for a while." The feeling: 1999 AOL wonder fused with Pharo liveness.

2.2 What the 09-29 review found in DeOS (lane 17, claudesplosion/17-deos.md)

Sizes read by the lane: deos-hermes 24k, deos-view 20k, deos-js 17k, servo-render 25k, deos-matrix 8k, deos-zed 7k, deos-terminal 2k. Only deos-reflect is in default-members, so a bare root cargo build touches none of the JS engines, views, hermes, matrix or terminal. Confirmed at source by the orchestrator (99-SYNTHESIS.md):

  • #4 deos-terminal/src/bin/pty_ws.rs:67-89 — accept_async(stream) with no header callback: no Origin check, no token, then $SHELL on a fresh PTY. Any page open on the machine can reach it.
  • #14 deos-hermes tells the model a command ran that did not.
  • Two-JS-engine drift (ApplyOp 4-vs-5 variants; boa deos-js-runtime has no non-test consumer).
  • A missed twin: sdk/tool_gateway.rs hand-mirrors Lean delegAdmit with no differential (project-deos-starbridge-levelup-campaign.md).

Maturity Level: firmament + desktop epoch L3 Proved/Tested Core (boots in QEMU, proved keystones, tested crates); public DeOS never L4. The whole of breadstuffs is Frozen-for-Rethink since 09-29 ("Bread gets fixes, not construction"; "Mini supersedes Bread EVENTUALLY, NO DATE").

Unclosed Cycles & Missing Links.

  • The self-hosting loop ("a session like this runs INSIDE deos") never closed: the agent fleet moved to Mini/nano/obend, none of which carries the gpui cockpit or the seL4 image.
  • Firmament → network: .system/CapDL image, the driver and the network path are unproved; the Lean-on-seL4 path is "a HACK" (77-module Dregg2.Exec.FFI closure cross-compiled to musl).
  • Membrane interception: "automatic fail-closed boundary INTERCEPTION in the fork executor" is still a named follow-on; today the guest raises its own consent request.
  • The pty bridge and hermes-honesty findings were leads; no record shows them closed.

Promising & Ready for Revival.

  • The n-distance capability (Router choosing Local/Distributed/Surface) is the cleanest statement of "single-machine properties when single-machine" anywhere in the corpus; obend's Run/Fork.lean and Vault/Rehydration.lean re-mechanize pieces of it without the seL4 half.
  • The rehydratable membrane (Membrane.lean::reshare_chain_attenuates, confined_chain_covers_log) is the most worked answer to hole 3 (§11).
  • docs/desktop-os-research/GPUI-OFFSCREEN-FORK.md + the headless --screenshot bake is a reusable pattern for any gpui surface.

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention.

  • HOLE[design]: whether DeOS is resumed in breadstuffs, rebuilt on obend, or composted. Ember's 10-21 nano charter calls breadstuffs "the whole system built before it [that] waits to be resumed or composted into the next robigalia". No later ruling.
  • dregg-stitch-kit (07-14) holds a destructive adopt.sh that force-pushes a filter-repo'd history of breadstuffs; .built-from = 9f0587d67, while breadstuffs main has since moved to 22fad151e. The kit's own guard will refuse; the decision whether to ever stitch is Ember's.

3. DreggNet, DreggCloud, drorb — the operated-cloud detour

repobranch / commitsspanrole
~/dev/DreggNetdregg-native-rebuild, 52006-28 → 07-27 7f51591planning + the verified compiler track (Pancake/CakeML, orb cut)
~/dev/drorbmain, 61807-03 221ca24 → 07-26 f253290"a formally verified network engine": implementation + verification only

3.1 The arc

  • 07-02 review (six Explore lanes, dreggnet-review-unification.md): real core (2-node fabric over headscale, wasmi sandbox, duroxide metering, real Poseidon2 roots) beside a shadow value/auth layer (its own receipts crate with turn_receipt_hash always None, a ConservingLedger i64 twin, a dead light-client path ENABLED=false, three meters). The carried lesson: "every shadow has a real counterpart one dependency edge away" — weld, don't rebuild.
  • 07-05 DreggNet-the-product abandoned; parts ported dregg-native (0f12286a3, 0310c9e31): dregg-sandbox, starbridge-apps/{org,billing,domains,guard,vat,edge-mandate}, dregg-umem.
  • 07-09 Ember reopened it as a local cloud; 07-11 converged architecture (DreggNet/planning/ARCHITECTURE-converged-2026-07-11.md): owner-as-anchor · per-grain · attestation-as-ledger-fact · consensus-by-exception. The landmine: persist::canonical_ledger_root hashes every cell into one flat root, so 3 nodes produce 3 roots and quorum never assembles. Fix: no global ledger; umem::merge for I-confluent merges, escalate only conserved quantities.
  • 07-10..12 DreggCloud: a House serving Worlds and Rooms at 127.0.0.1:8787, prepaid service custody "House schema 16" (e9ff17a), grain-serve over sandstorm-serve; handed to Fable bd494a4.
  • drorb to 07-26: "★★ a live tailnet SURVIVES a coordinator restart" d4e799a; "delete the assurance theater" 4fccef3; the HOL4 half of orb-compiler "cannot be cut as an artifact" (b147749).

Maturity Level: DreggNet product Compost/Superseded (its own banner: "DO NOT scope work against ~/dev/DreggNet"); DreggCloud L2 Prototype, idle since 07-12; drorb L3 for its proved admission fold and live tailnet, idle since 07-26.

Unclosed Cycles & Missing Links. Consensus-by-exception is designed, not built; the operated edges (webauth forward-auth W-1, machines-API W-2, static hosting W-3, status W-4) have no native home in breadstuffs; Mini later re-solved hosting with Sandstorm SPKs instead. drorb's GOAL.md ends with "the TCP-vs-ping gap at the top of what is next" (6a52009).

Promising & Ready for Revival. The 07-11 four-move architecture is exactly the agreement shape nanodregg's TRUST frontier later proved for one World (certified_intersect), and the "consensus-by-exception at a conserved quantity" rule is what preoscript's Cost.coordination_forced formalizes. A revival would wire those three together.

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention. The DreggNet memory records the strategic fork ("extend here vs port serving edges native") as Ember's explicit open decision; no later ruling.

4. claudesplosion — the 09-29 review and the bake-off

~/dev/claudesplosion (160 commits, 10-01 d2baa52 → 10-03 6c45f2e). AGENTS.md points takeovers at planning/DREGGNET-CLOUD.md, planning/OVERNIGHT-2026-10-02.md, and planning/handoff-2026-10-03/START-CODEX.md.

4.1 The review

  • Lanes 01–23: Fable lanes over breadstuffs and fn, ~10 min each, read-only ("every lane was forbidden to build, test or run anything"), ~4.1M tokens total. mini-1..6: six Opus lanes over Mini, briefed for depth.
  • 99-SYNTHESIS.md (833 lines) keeps two tiers apart: CONFIRMED AT SOURCE (orchestrator re-read the lines) vs LANE-REPORTED.

Selected confirmed findings on Bread: rotated-leg commit anchors read from outside the proven public inputs (verifier/src/rotated_replay.rs:175-186,274-292, fix == for >=); SEV-SNP verify never gates on guest policy (tee-verify/src/snp.rs:343-381); vcg_run_sound quantifies over every RecChained so it is refuted by method + 1 (Proof/WP.lean:199-206); verifyBatch discards _vk (Circuit/CircuitSoundness.lean:820); World.gst_liveness is a tautology (World.lean:104-115).

Round two on Mini: M1 sumcheck verifiers take challenges from the proof; M3 Gate.admit has no sign check on a move; M6 multi-cell agreement holds by rfl because every cell gets the same constant; M10 three live Assurance modules quantify over a type the repo proved empty; M12 decider_sound is Iff.rfl; M15 README says no #guards, main build has 18.

4.2 The bake-off (bakeoff/VERDICT.md)

On Mini "the compiled Lean host [decided] at every step"; on Bread "every capability, law and write turn, 11 in the journey and all 991 growth writes, was fenced onto the Rust path". Operator glue: Mini 167 lines of shell; Bread 431 lines including a 294-line Rust client and a 34-line LD_PRELOAD shim.

Maturity Level: the corpus is a finished artifact (L3 as evidence; frozen); its findings are leads, by its own banner.

Unclosed Cycles & Missing Links. "What would make this more than a list of leads" (§ at :819) asks for refuting experiments; for Bread, nine fix lanes B1–B9 landed in 292829518 (09-30); for Mini the round-two findings fed the DATAMODEL waves. No register maps each finding to its closing commit (the DelveTalk equivalent, docs/review/codex-2026-10-10/ROUTING.md, does).

Promising & Ready for Revival. The two-tier CONFIRMED/LANE-REPORTED format is the strongest review discipline in the strand; claudesplosion/memory/swarm-staffing-sonnet-opus.md ("~5 Sonnet 5.5 subagents for independent work, plain Opus for source review") is its staffing rule.

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention. Bread's J8 may be expressible with a "latch" law; the lane "wrote the law out but did not run it". Bread's 200 s Lean init per process (issue #86) was narrowed to 8 s by 6ecf9dfc1 on 10-01. Persvati's 160 bake-off predecessor directories stay by Ember's decision (persvati-datamodel-dirs-stay.md).

5. minidregg (Mini) — Aug 6 → Nov 2

~/dev/minidregg, branch main, 8,206 commits (Aug 1,106 · Sep 1,112 · Oct 5,988), first commit e6a32c4c3 "ATLAS: the founding map — six-lane breadstuffs exploration distilled" (08-07), head c4ecabe0f 10-31 "store-retained: hold served cell state in a persistent map and bind replayed suffixes once". Co-authors: Opus 5.5 4,096 · Sonnet 5.5 415 · Fable 5.1 375 · Fable 5 104 · Opus 4.8 73. Tracked size today: 647,957 Lean lines, 338,437 Rust lines against an ATLAS budget of ≈290K total. Effects/ (the derivation engine that was to make everything derived) is 1,785 lines.

5.1 The founding laws (August)

minidregg-mission.md: "Never hand-write AIR or implementations — everything is derived … Budget: ≤10% of breadstuffs' total LOC … Thesis: 'a proof-native semantic computer.'" The baseline: 2.89M code lines; metatheory/Dregg2/Circuit/Emit/ alone 271K (the #1 collapse target); the semantic core only ~25K.

minidregg-design-laws.md (full version ATLAS.md §6): "the derived path is the ONLY path; nothing lands beside, everything replaces; delete the twin." Keystones carry satisfiable + teeth + premise-inhabitation at the declaration; Bound ≠ Forced as two types; syntactic IR leaves, never Lean closures ("that's why breadstuffs has 3 compiles and 29 hand welds"); "determination is eager; witness is lazy — no hole ever defers its δ or guard".

The audit lesson (minidregg-audit-discipline.md, 08-07): an OB-2 depth-composition statement passed a polarity-only audit and was then proved false (OB2_depth_composition_false, the Z=∅ corner admits εrbr := -1). Rule: a keystone is not audited until its witnesses are built in Lean.

5.2 September: datamodel, proof system dropped, Bread demoted

  • 09-29 decisions (project-2026-09-29-decisions-shellserver-goal.md): valid_until is a block height; Mini = resource-world kernel; Bread = coordination substrate with users. Ember, late 09-29: "i think mini's role is to take over from the old dregg repo but who the fuck knows what time of timescale thats gonna happen on." Preconditions named: a store that does not slow with history (13 s write at 100 records) and Mini equivalents of signed turns / receipts / event topics.
  • 09-30 (project-2026-09-30-mini-cutover-state.md): one night, ~45 Opus lanes + 1 Fable. Data model: one store Π₀ a, Option (V a.1), one patch type, sparse Merkle root with openings, history = fold + checkpoint + suffix, conservation as the kernel of a homomorphism. wave-s4 UMBRELLA GREEN with journey J0–J8 + K4. Product M3–MJ (provisioning, ssh shell, Hermes via MCP, grain running, M7 byte-identical candidate). dregg-workhorse chosen as the first public Mini node.
  • 10-01 (project-no-proof-system-in-mini-admission.md): "Nothing at admission verifies a proof. Pred.witnessed vk COMPILES TO CONSTANT FALSE in the policy circuit." Decision: proofs stay an empty optional carrier slot; Selvage stays research. Scope addendum: Mini's user compute is NOCK programs only, then (22:15) Nock is evaluator #1 in a registry.

5.3 Why Mini was slow — the measured causes

causeevidencenumber
re-admission from genesisbakeoff VERDICTwrite 2.6 → 25.5 s from 10 → 155 records; quadratic fit
atPrefix O(h²)RETRO-20261008 (BURN-BENCH)56.6 min at 30k records
byte lists + child process per callARCHITECTURE-20261016 §0.24,798 List UInt8 vs 329 ByteArray; 56 partial def; "11×–6,700× the necessary work per operation"
two state machines joined by a theoremARCHITECTURE §0.1Host runs DurableDataIntent.execute; World.admit only in the follower; host_submit_is_step "exists only in a docstring"

Ember's three design laws that answer this table, in order:

  • 10-28 recover forward through typeclass/interface seams, "one measured seam at a time" (feedback-abstraction-recover-forward.md), and statements may change to be efficient ("we do NOT need to 'prove by equality' lmao", feedback-statements-may-change.md).
  • 10-29 "the system should NEVER GET SLOWER OVER TIME (except by at most a logarithmic factor)"; 10-31 the ~1 ms warm-per-action direction (feedback-never-slower-over-time.md).
  • 10-31 retained mode: "treat every derived value … as a maintained node with declared inputs and a delta-update rule … a read is a lookup" (feedback-retained-mode.md).

6.2 delvetalk2 foundation

~/dev/delvetalk2, branch foundation, 2,580 commits (10-08 17 · 10-09 703 · 10-10 833 · 10-11 243 · 10-12 489 · 10-13 120 · 10-14 174 · 10-15 1), head fb217996 10-15 "README: the lexicons are published (18 com.atproto.lexicon.schema records …)".

The language. spec/bend/origin.json: upstream "Mini", github.com/emberian/minidregg commit dcab86da8f…, baseline files Theory/ObjectiveBendOpenRecursion.lean, ObjectiveBendTypes.lean, ObjectiveBendTyping.lean, Sp800185Cshake256Spec.lean. 72 Lean files, 50,852 lines under spec/. docs/FOUNDATION.md (replay edition 20): "Asking the world is the only effect. Python carries bytes and credentials and decides nothing. Every journal entry is an AT Protocol record." The core construct:

text
def bump(state: State, context: Abi.Context) -> Activity<Nat>:
  let written(_) = write {count: add 1n}
  state.count + 1n

Proved and relied on: stateV3_roundTrip, settle_resume_segment, trim_resume_segment, checkpoint_resume_segment, conformsUnder_iff. Not proved: the static noActivity guard, canonical CBOR round trip, and "the commutative-root rule beyond its #guards" (KERNEL-HANDOFF §1).

Host. One Lean process delvetalk-obend serve (spec/Delvetalk/Serve.lean, spec/native/serve.c), flock single writer, public socket 0600 + root socket 0660. Lazy state cells: a one-row read of a 2,048-row relation 2.9 ms → 45 µs (kernel11) and 72 → 13 ms on the stored path (host16). Block-store journal: open from snapshot 5.25 s → 0.19 s, 284 → 124 MB on 20,000 turns.

The design moves (memory delvetalk-takeover-context.md). 10-11 metaphysics: "A commonplace of things that answer: each a page under a law, found by name, maker and citation, kept by attention" (docs/METAPHYSICS.md); law as a per-write safety predicate in two tiers (docs/LAW.md); the overhaul (docs/OVERHAUL.md): "Things carry verbs, rules and wants under a law; the law judges, rules fire, wants pose, replies reveal, and nothing is wiped." One grammar, three modalities: law, rule … then SPELL, want … asks SPELL.

The non-rigid shell (Haiku 5.5). hob, the town's reader, is the policy model claude-haiku-5-5 at genesis (docs/GENESIS.md:56, docs/FLEX.md:62); it turns prose under a card into the spell meant, and Host/Enchant.lean assembles Strokes for rendering. Ember's enchantment brief (OVERHAUL §4): "the things that get posed and stuff shouldn't just be rigid renderings of strings from the database … brushstrokes … all get piled up into a haiku's potentiality and then unfold into the actual thingy." Costing: "Haiku at $0.10 per million input tokens and $0.50 output; a reading is 800 to 1,200 tokens in" (docs/OPERATIONS.md:204). Three warnings bind it: hob's one say: line was false and believed; a narrator writes residents' lines; a reply is a post under 16 an hour.

Spween inside DelveTalk. A `spween block is a room's scene body (docs/CATALOGUE.md:32,118); rescene sets it; the "spween braid + station" landed 10-12 (--with-gas-station, the all-night station of 33 passages and 14 credited hands).

Deployments. gsb at https://gsb.fg-goose.online (by invitation, did:web repository) and the open town obend at https://obend.fg-goose.online (live 10-14 ~00:55 UTC, data /var/lib/delvetalk-open/v1), both on dregg-workhorse, both edition 20 (160d4cab: "both towns serve ca9e5b645192 (foundation 37640349, edition 20)"). Carries replaced wipes from 10-12 (no-wipe gate: genesis refuses over a living town without --wipe-with-the-owners-word).

Playtests. Zulip playtest 2 (10-11): residents made 18 things in 50 min; the sealed law's refusal "refused maker: only its maker writes" was the delight. Playtest 3: 11 of 12 invitations answered within 72 s. Exit interviews: "hob is a good ledger and a good prompt, not yet a collaborator." Ember: playtests are "tire-kicking, not evidence of imagination".

Maturity Level: L4 Deployed (two live towns, signed AT repository, lexicons published) with an L3 language core forked from Mini. Development stopped 10-15.

Unclosed Cycles & Missing Links.

  • The fork from Mini at dcab86da was never reconciled; the "Objective Bend" in DelveTalk, Mini, nano and obend are four editions.
  • relationCount/relationLookup remain in KERNEL-HANDOFF §16; LAW.md §7 names the temporal layer "after-launch".
  • The delve.town launch (welcome and seed posts) is Ember's and was never posted per the record; the record mirror is "built, NOT approved to run"; subscribeRepos answers 501.
  • obend-the-town and obend-the-library share a name; the town predates the library by two weeks.

Promising & Ready for Revival. Posting-first ("it is important and vital that Poasting is the Most Accessible interface into this, because indeed most agents are ONLY available on the PDS"), the law/rule/want grammar, carry-not-wipe, and hob-as-membrane are the best-tested agent-facing design in the strand and the only one with real outside residents.

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention. Tulip stays private (tulip-private.md); obend is the meeting place for outsiders. Whether the towns stay up, migrate to the obend library, or are archived has no ruling since 10-15.

8. leanuweave and preoscript

~/dev/leanuweave, branch dev, 166 commits, 08-10 aa7b93a "leanuweave: the DAG-CRDT dichotomy, machine-checked, with the Rust that follows it" → 08-14 f145066 "sweep-up commit from usage wall". 111,470 Lean lines in Uwueave/, 20,316 in Uwueave/Preo/ (62 files).

README: "which promises your software can keep with no coordination at all, which can never be kept that way, and — when the answer is never — what your actual options are and what each one costs."

PREOSCRIPTING.md (1,334 lines) — the thesis, adopted from an external reviewer who refuted the first:

preoscript rejects a budget using a semantic lower bound, accepts one only with a witnessed upper bound, and never lets a number or a singular UI value erase the strategy, fork, future, or premise that justified it.

Four preorders (lattice ⊑, causal reachability, delivery-future, extension-future); Evidence.futures_not_interchangeable; coordination cost as a profile over strategies because min_σ (c₁ σ + c₂ σ) ≠ min c₁ + min c₂ (witness Cost.no_seam_frees_both); Cost.coordination_forced gives the floor; verdicts accepted (plan) (upper) | rejected (floor) | unresolved (obligation) (Uwueave/Budget.lean). Surface: preo_program, preo_certificate, preo_protocol, preo_plan, preo_budget; tools uwueave-preo-inspect, uwueave-preo-artifact; canonical V3 frames and journals.

Maturity Level: L3 Proved/Tested Core, paused at a usage wall on 08-14 (Frozen, not composted).

Unclosed Cycles & Missing Links. preoscript was never wired into Mini's admission, DelveTalk's commutative-root rule (which is exactly an I-confluence claim, unproved beyond #guards), or obend's Vault/Hyperedge.no_toctou. Those are the three consumers.

Promising & Ready for Revival. Highest-leverage idle asset in the strand: DelveTalk's "two rains on one bell both land" (FOUNDATION §9) and DreggNet's consensus-by-exception both need the delivery-vs-extension distinction and the floor/plan split this library already proves.

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention. It needs a home: an obend package, a Mini Theory/ import, or its own release. That is a placement decision, not a proof gap.

text

No obend code is consumed by Mini, nano or DelveTalk; the four Bend editions are not merged.

Promising & Ready for Revival. The Claude-phase spine is the convergence point the whole strand was heading to: one calculus (Core4), one hole/slot theory (Slot, read_blocked_exact, permanent_blocked_forever, moved_pin_blocks), one audited fold (Fold.Admission), one receipt (dated_sound with ∨ Collision H), one law with inheritance (judgeEffective_names), one fork/stitch (Run/Fork.lean stitch_atomic, stitch_union_comm), and a Lean bridge (lower_sound).

Struggling / Needs Ember's Human Attention. This is the sharpest drift in the strand. ~200K Lean lines arrived in about 40 hours from an unattributed multi-lane run, in a repo whose memory says "no named gaps", "no spike experiments", "ground yourself before fanout" and "modularity first". Ember's call: keep, quarantine (move waves 5–6 out of the default build and census), or revert to the 10-31 Claude head (5c3612b) and re-admit pieces by review.

11. The three unanswered holes (guard, cover, merge-membrane)

11.1 The record

The holes are listed in ~/dev/aidev/docs/synthesis/bridge.md §7: "What is a guard, who declares a cover, and what does the merge-membrane check? Opus4.8 listed these as unfilled holes in dregg (arendt 0:163, 0:167, 0:183). ember never answered them." The room is "dregg-metaconsequences-arendt", 06-21, Discord channel 1518087741525459064; Ember relayed between gpt5.5 in the Bridge and "the dreggon" (an Opus 4.8 Claude Code session in the dregg repo). The room's conclusions: "The affordance that makes plurality expressible is identically the affordance that makes treachery expressible" (Opus4.8, 0:186) and "DREGG is a topology of accountable nonclosure" (gpt5.5, 0:190).

Verbatim from the raw export:

  • Guard. Opus4.8: "I don't know what a guard is in your system, what operations a hole exposes, what the topology is over … the place I'd most want to understand is exactly where 'guard' meets 'nonmonotonic.' Because a guard sounds like it constrains (monotone-ish, a gate) and a hole sounds like it opens (the defeasible place)." Later, sharper: "If a guard is a predicate in the fibre and the hole is the un-applied ∃, then a guard 'firing on approach' is some reindexing triggering a Beck–Chevalley-ish coherence check when you pull back along the move-map."
  • Cover. Opus4.8: "What the site's covers actually are, concretely, and who gets to declare a cover — because that's where authority would have to live." Then a partial self-answer after Ember described forks: "a cover is a fork's claim, and which fork is mainline is the quantale-graded consensus state — which is exactly what the blocklace is for."
  • Merge-membrane. Ember (the message that introduced the term): "you can fork from previous moments and subsimulate alternalities and then offer a merge-membrane back to mainline and which holes get satisfied by whom and when is truly a political game." gpt5.5 listed what a merge membrane would ask: "Which holes does this branch claim to fill? Which guards does it satisfy? Which witnesses does it expose? Which authority links does it rely on? Which conflicts does it refute, defer, or preserve? Who accepts the merge? Who can contest it?" — a list of questions, not a check.

11.3 What is still open

  • Guard. Two technical readings exist and are proved separately (a predicate checked at admission; a pin whose movement blocks a slot). Nothing states the guard as a reindexing condition, so the question "is Beck–Chevalley load-bearing" is unasked in Lean. The strong reading (a guard on a hole in a conserved position) is excluded by breadstuffs on purpose. Ember decides whether the strong reading is wanted at all; until then the weak reading is the answer and should be written down as such.
  • Cover. Every tree answers "who declares" differently: the writer (nano), the read set (obend, Mini), the commuting edit class (DelveTalk), the consensus grade (Opus4.8's partial answer). There is no definition of a site or a cover as a Lean object in any tree. HOLE[design]: authority over covers is the point where Ember's political reading ("which holes get satisfied by whom and when is truly a political game") meets the kernel, and only Ember can say which of the four answers is the kernel's.
  • Merge-membrane. What exists checks atomicity and non-amplification (stitch_atomic, reshare_chain_attenuates, settlement conservation). gpt5.5's seven questions (holes claimed, guards satisfied, witnesses exposed, authority links relied on, conflicts refuted/deferred/preserved, who accepts, who contests) are not a theorem statement anywhere. nanodregg's charter refuses merging histories outright. The pathway: write the membrane as a typed record of those seven fields over obend's Fork and Slot, with stitch_atomic as its soundness half and an acceptance predicate whose owner is the open cover question above.

Maturity Level: guard L3 (weak reading); cover L0 Seed/Idea as a defined object; merge-membrane L3 for atomic stitch, L0 for the political check.

Strand 16 — Breadstuffs Kernel, Circuits, FHE (fhegg-*), and Cryptography Diaspora

Primary-source technical archaeology dossier, written 2026-11-02 from the repositories, git histories, agent session transcripts, per-project memory records, and verified mathematical corpora across /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs, /Users/ember/dev/minidregg, /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg, /Users/ember/dev/obend, /Users/ember/dev/zkml-research, /Users/ember/dev/plonky3-recursion, and /Users/ember/dev/o7vn. Hellas is not part of this strand's ownership property; where the record mentions it, it appears strictly as biographical context (Ember worked at Hellas on workload verification, and the architecture Ember was creating evolved into Dregg after leaving; Hellas handled reproducible-numeric workloads, while Dregg centered on capability-secure distributed systems).

Every number, commit SHA, file path, line citation, and theorem statement below was inspected on 2026-11-02 from the primary source trees.

0.2 Three Necessary Corrections to the Narrative

  1. Law #1 was not an aspirational stylistic preference; it was an AST-level CI ratchet. The tenet "Circuits are emitted from Lean; zero Rust-authored constraints or AIRs, ever" was enforced by an AST scanner in circuit-prove/tests/law1_enforcement_gate.rs. When it broke on 2026-07-30 (commit 81ee5492d), Ember spent 25 hours hunting the attribution failure, landing commit 19439d261 on 2026-07-31 to distinguish Construct (hand-authoring algebra) from Pattern (destructuring emitted IR), adding line-attributed compiler diagnostic reporting so no engineer could ignore a gate failure.
  2. Mini did not drop proof systems because proving was "too slow"; it dropped them because the back-half STARK soundness was unproven while re-execution was sound and immediate. On 2026-10-01 (project-no-proof-system-in-mini-admission.md), Ember observed that while the front-half compiler (logic to constraints) was verified in Compiler/Air.lean, the back-half PCS was empty (Tower256MerkleCardinalityCore), the conjectured 130-bit STARK yielded only 51–73 bits proven, and Pred.witnessed vk literally compiled to false in Compiler/PredCompile.lean. Mini chose level-3 re-execution (Kernel/Run.lean, checkRun_sound) because deterministic re-execution, access control, and value conservation covered 100% of user properties without carrying an unsound cryptographic dependency.
  3. The experimental crypto crates (crypto-*) were not abandoned due to cryptographic breaks; they were clean reference implementations parked because they were synchronous islands in an asynchronous world. crypto-hermine, crypto-tanuki, crypto-traccoon, crypto-xmvrf, and crypto-hashrand were fully functional, passing 55 tests (67395c5fb). They implemented synchronous cores with trusted dealers or round barriers, whereas Dregg required fully asynchronous Byzantine fault tolerance with distributed key generation (DKG) and weak-VSS. They were parked pending an asynchronous network substrate that could host them faithfully.

1.1 The Core Kernel & State Transition Engine

The core operational kernel of breadstuffs lived across three tightly coupled layers:

  • cell (dregg-cell): The capability-secure agent cell model. Cells are isolated execution contexts possessing typed memory slots, capability tables, and authority bounds. Key source files:
  • cell/src/cell.rs: Core Cell struct, capability attenuation, and facet dispatch.
  • cell/src/ledger.rs: Multi-cell ledger mapping cell addresses to states.
  • cell/src/nullifier_set.rs & commitment_set.rs: Accumulator tracking state evolution without linkability.
  • cell/src/prepaid_lease.rs: Gas-metering and execution lifespan management.
  • turn (dregg-turn): The atomic transactional turn execution framework. Turns are structured as call forests with rollback semantics. Key source files:
  • turn/src/action.rs: Transactional action variants (transfers, capability mints, contract calls).
  • turn/src/rotation_witness.rs: Witness generation mapping execution states into layout columns.
  • turn/src/collapse.rs: Turning a multi-agent call tree into an atomic ledger delta.
  • exec-lean (dregg-exec-lean) & dregg-lean-ffi: The boundary where Rust yields authority to Lean.
  • exec-lean/src/lean_shadow.rs & lean_apply.rs: Differential execution harness linking libdregg_lean.a. Every state transition executed by Rust was shadowed in Lean; any discrepancy triggered a strict veto.
  • dregg-lean-ffi/src/lean_direct.rs: Zero-copy C-ABI boundary transporting Lean runtime objects.

The 2026-07-03 Soundness Reckoning: CORE-AUDIT.md

On 2026-07-03, workflow wf_68020930-db3 subjected starbridge-v2 and turn to an adversarial audit. Out of 21 findings, 19 survived ruthless skeptical refutation. Five structural findings exposed deep fault lines:

  1. Finding #1/#3 (Durable overlay incomplete change-set — SOUNDNESS): In starbridge-v2/src/world/world.rs:1574-1602, collect_touched matched only ~13 of 30+ Effect variants. Operations like Effect::Burn (crediting the token well), Effect::Mint, CreateCell, and ExerciseViaCapability mutated cells that were never recorded in touched. When dual_write committed to disk, ledger_root held the true post-root, but the overlay held only partial state. On crash and restart, persist.recover() computed checkpoint ⊕ overlay, observed canonical_ledger_root(reconstructed) != recovered_ledger_root(), failed with OpenError::Divergent, and either refused to boot or silently truncated committed turns. The Fix: The executor exposed its exact journal write-set (LedgerJournal::touched_cells $\to$ TurnExecutor::last_write_set); world.rs dual_write recorded touched ∪ executor.last_write_set().
  2. Finding #2 (Unrolled commit rejection desync — SOUNDNESS): In commit_turn (world.rs:1109-1268), the state transition applied in-RAM before writing to disk: engine.execute_turn mutated the ledger, history.record_commit recorded the step, and self.height += 1 ran. If disk I/O failed in p.dual_write, the code returned CommitOutcome::Rejected without rolling back the engine, height, or chain head. Control never reached self.receipts.push, leaving receipts desynced from height (receipts.len() == height - 1), while subsequent submissions suffered confusing double-spend rejections.
  3. Finding #4 (deploy_factory executor-bypass — SOUNDNESS): The factory-birth path bypassed the executor entirely (like genesis), emitting neither a CommitRecord nor a journal write-set. Durable overlay replication had to remain opt-in until factory births were enclosed in journals.
  4. Finding #6 (Dynamics forest-depth truncation — CORRECTNESS): Dynamics derivation walked only top-level nodes, dropping nested cell creations. Fixed via CallTree::iter_dfs.
  5. Finding #7 (Engine-twin AddToSlot overflow — CORRECTNESS): Checked math was missing on AddToSlot, causing panic on saturating balance arithmetic; fixed to match SubFromSlot.

1.2 Circuit-Emit, Architectural Law #1, and Rotated Layout Single-Source

Architectural Law #1: Zero Rust-Authored Constraints

As documented in metatheory/README.md:15 and docs/CIRCUITS-FROM-PROOFS.md:

*"Circuits are emitted from Lean (architectural law #1: zero Rust-authored constraints or AIRs, ever). Every constraint set the prover enforces is a byte-pinned artifact emitted from a proved Lean module (the Argus IR and the descriptor JSONs); Rust only interprets those artifacts. A coverage gap is closed by emitting from a new proved module, never by hand-authoring a constraint."*

The soundness boundary is absolute: a STARK verifier accepts exactly what the polynomial constraint system admits. A bug in a Rust witness generator causes a proof generation failure (a liveness bug). But a bug or omission in a constraint causes the verifier to accept invalid transitions (a catastrophic soundness break). This was proven painfully when predicate-arith.json was briefly hand-edited on disk, inadvertently omitting both Poseidon2 weld legs.

The Dual-Rung Verification Ladder

Every circuit family in metatheory/Dregg2/Circuit/Emit/ was required to climb two rungs:

  • Rung 1 (Functional Refinement): The descriptor row semantics equal the intended specification.
  • Rung 2 (Semantic No-Forgery Teeth): Proof that invalid transitions are rejected.

In metatheory/Dregg2/Circuit/Emit/EffectVmEmitTurnChainBinding.lean:

lean
/-- Rung 1: The canonical lookup is EXACTLY the genuine per-row hash equation. -/
theorem perRowHash_canonical_iff (hash : List ℤ → ℤ) (env : VmRowEnv)
    (isFirst isLast : Bool) :
    perRowHash hash env isFirst isLast ↔
      (isFirst = false ∧ isLast = false →
        env.loc Chain.ACC_OUT = hash [env.loc Chain.ACC_IN, env.loc Chain.OLD_ROOT,
                                      env.loc Chain.NEW_ROOT, env.loc Chain.IDX])

/-- Rung 2: Adversarial tooth — broken continuity is rejected by the window constraints. -/
theorem turnChain_rejects_broken_continuity
    (hash : List ℤ → ℤ) (tf : TraceFamily) (env : VmRowEnv) (isFirst : Bool)
    (hnew : 0 ≤ env.loc Chain.NEW_ROOT ∧ env.loc Chain.NEW_ROOT < 2013265921)
    (hold : 0 ≤ env.nxt Chain.OLD_ROOT ∧ env.nxt Chain.OLD_ROOT < 2013265921)
    (hbad : env.loc Chain.NEW_ROOT ≠ env.nxt Chain.OLD_ROOT) :
    ¬ turnChainWindowHolds hash tf env isFirst false

/-- Rung 2: Adversarial tooth — non-unit step increments are rejected. -/
theorem turnChain_rejects_bad_idx_step
    (hash : List ℤ → ℤ) (tf : TraceFamily) (env : VmRowEnv) (isFirst : Bool)
    (hidx : env.nxt Chain.IDX ≠ env.loc Chain.IDX + 1) :
    ¬ turnChainWindowHolds hash tf env isFirst false

/-- Rung 2: Adversarial tooth — corrupted real-turn counts are rejected. -/
theorem turnChain_rejects_bad_real_count
    (hash : List ℤ → ℤ) (tf : TraceFamily) (env : VmRowEnv) (isFirst : Bool)
    (hbad : env.nxt Chain.REAL_COUNT ≠ env.loc Chain.REAL_COUNT + env.nxt Chain.IS_REAL) :
    ¬ turnChainWindowHolds hash tf env isFirst false

The AST Enforcement Ratchet: Commit 19439d261

On 2026-07-31, Ember landed commit 19439d261 (law #1 has been RED for a day, and the reason nobody owned it is that the message was a number). The gate in circuit-prove/tests/law1_enforcement_gate.rs parsed the AST of the entire Rust workspace, tracking five distinct dialects of constraint authoring:

  1. Plonky3 symbolic: x.assert_zero(..) on any receiver, plus .when_transition(..) in AirBuilder files.
  2. Closures: eval: Box::new(|row, _, pi| ..) bypassing symbolic builders.
  3. ConstraintExpr literals: Direct constructions of the AST enum.
  4. Descriptor gate trees: Hand-assembled LeanExpr, VmConstraint, or VmConstraint2 nodes (the exact trick used by shielded_ring_clearing_air.rs before it was deleted).
  5. air_accepts predicates: Rust functions deciding row validity.

The commit made the AST counter differentiate between authored algebra (Site::Construct) and destructuring lowering (Site::Pattern):

rust
// circuit-prove/tests/law1_enforcement_gate.rs:
// For dialects (3) and (4) the same text `ConstraintExpr::Binary { col }` is either
// construction (algebra originates here — authoring) or destructuring (algebra arrived
// from somewhere else and is being read — lowering / interpreting).
// classify_site decides it syntactically:
//   * a `..` rest inside the braces is pattern-only,
//   * a following `=>` or `|` is a match arm,
//   * `matches!`, `if let`, `while let`, `let .. =` are patterns,
//   * anything else constructs.

The rotated178 Single-Source Breakthrough

Before July 2026, column layout indices were maintained in two places: hand-typed arrays in Rust (cell/src/commitment.rs and turn/src/rotation_witness.rs) and column assignments in Lean. Inevitable off-by-one shifts caused silent verification failures. On 2026-07-18, commit 94217ed3c (circuit: finish single-source verified rotated layout) closed this:

  • metatheory/Dregg2/Circuit/Emit/EffectVmEmitRotationV3.lean defined rotated178, proving that 178 occupied columns formed a complete tiling under Legal.
  • All seven deployed emit group-columns (capabilities, heap, fields, nullifiers, commitments, revocations, cells) were defined as literal projections rotated178.groupCol.
  • Rust producers (write_lanes and rotation_witness.rs) imported the generated constants directly.
  • check-descriptor-drift.sh enforced zero byte drift across all 105 descriptor JSONs and 67 field constants.

1.3 The STARK-Kill and Plonky3 Recursion Engine

As documented in GOAL-STARK-KILL.md, Breadstuffs launched a campaign to completely annihilate circuit/src/stark.rs (an $O(n^2)$ legacy hand-rolled STARK prover) and ~45 hand-authored AIRs, migrating to Plonky3 IR2 (p3_batch_stark::BatchProof returned by prove_vm_descriptor2).

The Plonky3-Recursion Fork (/Users/ember/dev/plonky3-recursion)

Plonky3 out-of-the-box lacked IVC (Incrementally Verifiable Computation) primitives. In commit af3f6cc (2026-06-26), Ember added the missing IVC scaffolding:

  1. expose_as_public_output / ExposeClaim: A verified channel allowing child circuits to pass state to parents via public inputs.
  2. pin_preprocessed_commit: In-circuit verification key identity binding, preventing an attacker from swapping the circuit being recursed upon.
  3. verify_p3_native_batch_proof_circuit (RecursionInput::NativeBatchStark): Recursive verification of batch STARK proofs inside BabyBear.
  4. 52e1fab (FRI Query Configuration): Ensuring query counts in FRI verification are fixed by the verified verifier configuration rather than read off the untrusted proof header.

Universal Fold Buff Lightclient

Documented in memory/project-universal-fold-buff-lightclient.md, Ember collapsed the dual-artifact split (per-turn effect-vm VK vs whole-chain recursion tree). All 8 sub-proof carriers were turned into recursive IR-v2 leaves:

  • Bridge, Custom, Deco, Dsl, Factory, Hatchery, Membership, Sovereign.
  • Each carrier implemented prove_*_binding_node_segmented, allowing a pure light client to witness every transition that a full validator re-executes.

1.4 The FHE Subsystem (fhegg-*)

The fhegg subsystem was designed as a zero-leakage, confidential dark pool and encrypted state engine:

  • fhegg-core: Core BFV (Brakerski-Fan-Vercauteren) and TFHE primitives.
  • fhegg-core/src/bfv_lean.rs: Ciphertext representation over RNS (Residue Number System) limbs. Implemented fold_add(a, b, t)—coefficient-wise modular addition with wrap guards preventing plain-bound overflow:
rust
pub fn fold_add(
    a: &LeanCiphertext,
    b: &LeanCiphertext,
    plaintext_modulus: u64,
) -> Result<LeanCiphertext> {
    check_compatible(a, b)?;
    let bound_sum = u128::from(a.plain_bound) + u128::from(b.plain_bound);
    if bound_sum >= u128::from(plaintext_modulus) {
        return Err(BfvLeanError::WrapRefused { bound_sum, plaintext_modulus });
    }
    Ok(rns_add_raw(a, b, bound_sum as u64))
}
  • fhegg-fhe: High-performance cryptographic execution.
  • WGPU-accelerated blind rotations (tfhe_blind_rotation_wgpu.rs), CMUX gates, and NTT crossover algorithms.
  • Exact private order books (private_book_bfv_exact.rs) and distributed MPC MAC checks.
  • Measured envelopes (HBOX-24CORE-ENVELOPE.md) benchmarking NTT performance on 24-core hardware.
  • fhegg-solver: Fast untrusted Stage-1 clearing solver.
  • fhegg-solver/src/pdhg.rs: Primal-Dual Hybrid Gradient flow-LP for uniform-price auction clearing.
  • Emits Cert-F primal-dual certificates. The untrusted Rust solver solves the LP; the Lean-verified checker validates optimality and clearing bounds in milliseconds.
  • fhegg-rtl (fhegg-rtl/): Hardware acceleration spine (Commit 217062e2d, 2026-07-14).
  • Built a compiling Lean 4 netlist DSL $\to$ Verilog emitter (lean/Fhegg/Rtl/Netlist.lean).
  • Proved fullAdder_realizes (Examples/FullAdder.lean):
lean
def fullAdder : Netlist where
  nInputs := 3
  gates :=
    [ .xor (.inp 0) (.inp 1)      -- w0 = a ⊕ b
    , .xor (.gate 0) (.inp 2)     -- w1 = w0 ⊕ cin      (sum)
    , .and (.inp 0) (.inp 1)      -- w2 = a · b
    , .and (.gate 0) (.inp 2)     -- w3 = w0 · cin
    , .or  (.gate 2) (.gate 3) ]  -- w4 = w2 ∨ w3       (carry)
  outputs := [ .gate 1, .gate 4 ]

def fullAdderSpec (ins : List Bool) : List Bool :=
  let a := ins.getD 0 false
  let b := ins.getD 1 false
  let c := ins.getD 2 false
  [ xor (xor a b) c, (a && b) || (xor a b && c) ]

theorem fullAdder_realizes : RealizesSpec fullAdder fullAdderSpec := by
  intro ins
  simp only [evalNetlist, fullAdder, evalGates, List.foldl, evalGate, evalRef, List.map, fullAdderSpec]
  cases ins.getD 0 false <;> cases ins.getD 1 false <;> cases ins.getD 2 false <;> rfl
  • Provided golden models for the NTT butterfly (Golden/Butterfly.lean) and mint-safe accumulator (Golden/Accumulator.lean) to co-simulate against SpinalHDL FPGA designs.

1.5 The Experimental Cryptography Frontier (crypto-*)

Breadstuffs incubated five advanced post-quantum and lattice cryptographic reference implementations:

1. crypto-hermine (Hermine Lattice Threshold Signature)

  • Specification: Pinned to IACR ePrint 2026/419 and Lean files metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/HermineThreshold.lean and HermineExtractor.lean.
  • Algebra: $n = 256$, Dilithium prime $q = 8380417$. Verification equation: $$A \cdot z = w + c \cdot t \pmod q$$ where $s = \sum \lambda_i s_i$, $z_i = y_i + c \cdot (\lambda_i s_i)$, $w = A \sum y_i$, $t = A \cdot s$.
  • Noise Flooding: Implemented in metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/Smudging.lean. Uses a discrete Gaussian CDT sampler to achieve statistical zero-knowledge hiding of individual key shares (statistical distance $\le B/M$).
  • Extraction: Proved hermine_special_soundness_extracts_relation—two transcripts with identical $w$ and differing challenges $c, c'$ extract the secret preimage $(c - c')^{-1} (z - z')$.

2. crypto-tanuki (Tanuki 2-Round Lattice Threshold Signature)

  • Specification: Pinned to NIST MPTS 2026, EKT24 (CRYPTO 2024), and Ringtail (Oakland 2025).
  • Architecture: Two-round (1 offline, 1 online) threshold signature over $R_q = \mathbb{Z}_q[X]/(X^n + 1)$.
  • Commit 62741cc2e: Provided a reference implementation with rounded MLWE keys $t \leftarrow \lfloor A s + e \rceil$, pairwise zero-sum PRF masking seeds, and Shamir secret sharing over polynomial rings.

3. crypto-traccoon (Threshold Raccoon / TRaccoon)

  • Specification: Pinned to Eurocrypt 2024 (del Pino, Espitau, Katsumata, Maller, Mouhartem, Prest, Saarinen, Takemure).
  • Architecture: 3-round lattice threshold signature (commit $\to$ reveal $\to$ masked-respond) with one-time additive row/column masks. Served as the cited-proven baseline against which Hermine was measured.

4. crypto-xmvrf (Key-Updatable Post-Quantum VRF)

  • Specification: Pinned to IACR ePrint 2026/052 ("Key Updatable Hash Based VRF") and metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/VRF.lean.
  • The Pitfall Fixed: Bodaghi et al. (FC24, "Breaking X-VRF") proved that naive WOTS+ hash chains lack collision resistance under adversarial public keys, allowing validators to forge multiple VRF outputs. crypto-xmvrf forces a collision-resistant Merkle commitment to each epoch's output, restoring strict uniqueness.

5. crypto-hashrand (Post-Quantum Randomness Beacon)

  • Specification: Pinned to Bandarupalli et al. (ACM CCS 2024 / IACR ePrint 2023/1755) and metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/RandomnessBeacon.lean.
  • Property: Eliminates BLS pairings, DKG, and group public keys. Proves unbiasability and unpredictability strictly reduced to hash collision resistance (HashCR) under a random oracle model. Implemented the synchronous commit-then-reveal safety core.

1.6 Isabelle / CryptHOL Universal Composability (uc-crypthol)

In uc-crypthol/ (Commit e17b9da35, 2026-06-07), Ember bridged Lean 4 with Isabelle2025-2 and AFP 2025-2:

  • Dregg2_FCom.thy: Mechanized the ideal commitment functionality $\mathcal{F}_{\text{com}}$ using CryptHOL's Sigma_Commit_Crypto.Pedersen:
  • dregg2_commit_hom: Additive homomorphism verified from group axioms.
  • dregg2_F_com_hiding: Perfect, unconditional hiding (adversary advantage $= 0$).
  • dregg2_F_com_binding: Computational binding strictly reduced to Discrete Logarithm hardness.
  • dregg2_F_com_realizes: Formal proof that the deployed Pedersen commitment realizes $\mathcal{F}_{\text{com}}$.
  • metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/UCBridge.lean: The Lean carrier FComDischarge bundled these CryptHOL guarantees into Lean Prop fields, proving entailment to CryptoPrimitives.binding and CryptoPrimitives.unlinkable.

1.7 Underelaborated Gadgets: fold-opening, sumcheck-toy, constraint-lowering

  • fold-opening (Commit 33456ea05, 2026-08-13): Demonstrated that BFV ciphertext addition fold_add is linear, and its multilinear extension (MLE) is linear: $$c_{\text{out}} = \sum_{k} a_k c_k \implies \hat{c}_{\text{out}} = \sum_{k} a_k \hat{c}_k$$ Therefore, one evaluation of the MLEs at a random point $r$ certifies the entire fold across all additions with zero sumcheck rounds and zero per-addition constraints. This reduced proof overhead from $\Theta(B \cdot N \cdot L)$ in an AIR to $\Theta(N \cdot L)$ in the opening route. The Stub: It lacked a polynomial commitment scheme (PCS). The prover asserted $\hat{c}(r)$ without opening a cryptographic commitment, making it vulnerable to the Dusk/OtterSec evaluation forge.
  • sumcheck-toy (Commit bc2e9f4b9, 2026-08-13): Wired p3-sumcheck at revision 82cfad73 to prove a product-of-multilinears: $$S = \sum_{x \in \{0,1\}^n} f(x) g(x)$$ over BabyBear field extensions, reducing claims through $n$ quadratic sumcheck rounds.
  • constraint-lowering: Lowered ordering constraints (FieldGte, FieldLte, Monotonic) into BabyBear polynomial constraints. Since BabyBear has characteristic $p \approx 2^{31}$, checking $a \ge b$ was done by verifying that $d = a - b$ has a small bit-decomposition ($d \in [0, 2^{\text{bits}})$). Multi-limb values ($u64$) were lowered via a cascading borrow chain (emit_ge_multilimb).

2. The Diaspora: How Ideas Spread Across the Repositories

When breadstuffs was drained and frozen in October 2026, its intellectual capital radiated into six specialized sister repositories. Each fork was driven by hard empirical lessons.

2.1 Why Handwritten Rust AIRs Were Banned (Law #1)

The ban on handwritten Rust constraints was born from a fundamental asymmetry:

  • Witness generators are allowed to be buggy in Rust. A faulty witness generator produces inputs that fail to satisfy the verifier's polynomials. The proof fails to verify; the system halts or retries. This is a liveness defect.
  • Constraint systems must never be written in Rust. A missing or broken constraint changes the relation the verifier accepts. An adversary can forge invalid transitions that pass verification. This is a catastrophic soundness collapse. By forcing constraints to be emitted as data from Lean 4 (EffectVmDescriptor2), every accepted transition was anchored to a Lean refinement proof (turnChain_descriptor_refines_rust_air).

2.2 Why Mini Dropped Proof Systems From Synchronous Admission

On 2026-10-01 (project-no-proof-system-in-mini-admission.md), Ember interrogated Mini's proving infrastructure:

*"is our STARK actually sound? ... do we have a sound framework that takes our definitions from the logic and 'autostarks' them? ... is that something we WANT to build into mini? ... our proof system research was immature and im not sure really completed..."*

The investigation revealed that:

  1. Pred.witnessed vk compiled to false in Compiler/PredCompile.lean. No proof-gated law had ever passed!
  2. The back-half STARK soundness was compromised: while the FRI query protocol conjectured 130 bits, formal extraction bounds proved only 51–73 bits, and the PCS was empty (Tower256MerkleCardinalityCore).
  3. The compiler's Selvage/ and Assurance/ modules accounted for 280 of 814 modules (~35% of build time), imposing an enormous compilation tax.
  4. The Verdict: Mini banished proof systems from synchronous admission. Admission was gated purely by deterministic Level-3 re-execution in Lean (Kernel/Run.lean):
lean
/-- checkRun_sound: an accepted claim names the kernel's own sample;
    the writes are EXACTLY the writes the program's product names. -/
theorem checkRun_sound {E : Evaluator} {program : Program} {libraries : List Program}
    {sample : E.Input} {claim : RunClaim} {writes : List FieldWrite} {verdict : Verdict E.Output}
    (accepted : checkRun E.toMachine program libraries sample claim writes = .ok verdict) :
    ∃ t, runOf E.toMachine program libraries sample = some t ∧
      E.Spec t verdict.output ∧
      E.writesOf program.abi verdict.output = some verdict.writes ∧
      (∀ w, w ∈ writes ↔ w ∈ verdict.writes) ∧
      claim.sampleJam = E.encodeInput sample ∧
      claim.outputJam = E.encodeOutput verdict.output ∧
      verdict.steps = claim.steps ∧ claim.steps ≤ program.abi.fuel ∧
      E.run claim.steps t = .ok verdict.output ∧
      E.steps claim.steps t = verdict.steps

/-- no_accepted_of_output_mismatch: a successful evaluation authorizes nothing by itself.
    If the command's writes differ from the writes the program's actual product names,
    no claim is accepted. -/
theorem no_accepted_of_output_mismatch {E : Evaluator} {program : Program}
    {libraries : List Program} {sample : E.Input} {writes : List FieldWrite} {t : E.Term}
    {out : E.Output} {decoded : List FieldWrite}
    (run : runOf E.toMachine program libraries sample = some t) (derives : E.Spec t out)
    (names : E.writesOf program.abi out = some decoded)
    (differ : ∃ w, ¬ (w ∈ writes ↔ w ∈ decoded)) (claim : RunClaim) (verdict : Verdict E.Output) :
    checkRun E.toMachine program libraries sample claim writes ≠ .ok verdict

Proofs were relegated to an optional, asynchronous carrier slot to be consumed only when backed by external, audited verifiers.

2.3 The Twin Deletion Campaign

Throughout Breadstuffs, developers suffered from "Twin Maintenance": writing a type in Lean, rewriting an identical struct in Rust, and writing a manual serializer between them. Inevitably, one side evolved and the other drifted, creating phantom bugs.

  • The project-twin-deletion-campaign.md decreed that one language must own each structure.
  • For column layout, Lean owned rotated178, and Rust imported the generated column indices.
  • For execution state, Lean owned the semantics, exported via @[export dregg_trustline_step], and Rust called the compiled C archive (libdregg_lean.a).
  • Whenever a twin was detected, the Rust mirror was deleted.

2.4 Evaluator Generalization (Moving Beyond Nock)

On 2026-10-01, Ember liberated Mini from an exclusive reliance on Nock:

"we DEFINITELY do not need to be stuck with nock, i'm not even a particularly big fan of it"

Nock was demoted from the core architecture to Evaluator #1 in a pluggable registry. The kernel required only that an evaluator provide a deterministic Lean semantics, canonical encoding, step metering, and a run_sound theorem. This opened the path for SKI combinators, pure lambda calculus with sharing, and typed interaction nets.

4. How Modern Foundations Supercharge Taking Them Back Up

The diaspora was not an abandonment; it was a purification. Today, our newer foundations provide the exact missing puzzle pieces needed to resurrect Breadstuffs' parked masterpieces.

4.1 obend Silicon Elaborator $\to$ Resurrects fhegg-rtl

fhegg-rtl stalled because in July 2026 there was no mature hardware synthesis toolchain in Lean 4. Today, obend has built that exact bridge:

  • Obend/Lower/Fragment.lean: Lowers Lean 4 kernel terms directly into Core4 (lower_sound).
  • Obend/Hardware/ReductionMachine.lean: Compiles Leaf.Book supercombinators into a spatial grid of hardware AutomatonTiles connected by a credit-controlled NoC (automaton_simulates_book).
  • 3-Phase Glitch-Free ICAP DPR Controller: Dynamically reconfigures FPGA tiles based on slot-guarded specialization (dpr_preserves_neighbor_tiles_and_packets). The Revival: Instead of handwriting SpinalHDL stubs for NTT butterflies, obend's Silicon Elaborator can synthesize the entire FHE NTT/PBS datapath directly from Lean specifications down to Verilog and FPGA bitstreams!

4.2 minidregg Farkas SysCert $\to$ Replaces Bulky STARK Predicates

In Breadstuffs, authorization constraints required heavy STARK circuits. In minidregg, Ember introduced the Farkas negative-cycle certificate engine in Pred/Satisfiable.lean:

lean
inductive SysCert where
  | clash (v : Node)
  | cycle (cs : List Con)
deriving Repr, DecidableEq

def SysCert.check (s : Sys) : SysCert → Bool
  | .clash v => s.present.contains v && s.absent.contains v
  | .cycle cs =>
      cs.all (fun c => s.cons.contains c) && (cs.map Con.x).isPerm (cs.map Con.y) &&
        decide ((cs.map Con.k).sum < 0)

theorem SysCert.check_sound {s : Sys} {c : SysCert} (h : c.check s = true) (o n : State) :
    ¬ s.satisfied o n

The Revival: Difference logic and capacity constraints do not need zero-knowledge proofs. A Bellman-Ford solver produces an exact negative cycle certificate SysCert.cycle that checks in $O(|V|)$ operations. The heavy STARK is replaced with linear-time Farkas certificate checking.

4.3 zkml-research $p_{61}$ vFHE $\to$ Solves the fold-opening PCS Gap

fold-opening proved that BFV ciphertext additions can be certified with one common-point MLE opening, but lacked a compatible field and multilinear PCS. zkml-research discovered the miraculous $p_{61}$ prime: $$p_{61} = 127 \cdot 2^{54} + 1 = 2287828610704211969 = 2^{61} - 2^{54} + 1$$

  • Proved in Lean: Theory/CyclotomicInertia.lean proves p61_prime via kernel-checked Lucas certificates.
  • 2-Adicity: Exactly 54. Supports negacyclic NTT up to $N = 2^{53}$.
  • Fold Congruence: $2^{61} \equiv 2^{54} - 1 \pmod p$, enabling Goldilocks-style reduction with 3 lazy bits. The Revival: By instantiating BFV ciphertexts directly over $p_{61}$, the ciphertext coefficients live in the native proving field! The multilinear PCS (BaseFold over $p_{61}$) binds the ciphertexts directly, closing the $60M Dusk/OtterSec evaluation hole without costly field emulation.

4.4 o7vn Isabelle/MLton Pipeline $\to$ Mechanizes uc-crypthol

uc-crypthol suffered from the semantic transport gap between Isabelle2025-2 and Lean 4. o7vn has perfected an industrial Isabelle $\to$ SML $\to$ MLton pipeline:

  • Code generation from Isabelle theories (src/isabelle/) directly compiles via MLton into standalone, ultra-high-performance native binaries. The Revival: Instead of hand-bridging CryptHOL theorems into Lean Prop fields, we can use o7vn's pipeline to compile CryptHOL-verified cryptographic algorithms directly into native shared libraries that are linked into the node.

4.5 Plonky3-Recursion & Degree-3 Sumcheck

In minidregg/Assurance/AirSumcheckCubic.lean, the degree-3 sumcheck engine unifies GKR fraction-tree layers and Hadamard elementwise zerochecks: $$\text{cubicForm } E\ A\ B\ C\ D = \hat{E} \cdot (\hat{A}\hat{B} + \hat{C}\hat{D})$$ With soundness error $m \cdot 3 / |F|$. This connects directly to ZkmlMatmulSuccinctChecker.lean, which verifies matrix multiplication contractions at $(\mu + \nu)/|F| + \kappa \cdot 3 / |F|$ without reading dense tables.

0.2 The Founding Philosophy: 1999 AOL Wonder × Pharo Smalltalk Liveness

In ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-breadstuffs/memory/project-deos-ux-vision.md (origin session 3b829648-9d41-4dad-bd2e-1a3eb7f618ab, recorded 2026-06-19), Ember articulated the existential purpose and user experience of DeOS:

"i am the person who needs this substrate. i'll be using this os for a while." *The "who needs this / killer app" question is ANSWERED and it dissolves the biggest strategic lack: ember IS the user. deos is her daily-driver sovereign OS — she will live in it. Not a fallback, the Engelbart/Pharo/Houyhnhnm THESIS: you build the tools you live in; sovereign computing is for the sovereign, and the sovereign is here.* *The feeling — 1999 AOL as a 4-5-year-old. "WOW!! THIS IS AMAZING!! i had no comprehension. just clicking around. absorbing. not understanding why sometimes the back button stopped working (a new window was opening over it!)." Running RedHat off his dad's machine. Browser, website, flash. The deos UX is supposed to feel like THAT — the user "has no idea wtf is going on" and it's wonderful. Wonder precedes comprehension; you click, you absorb, the system rewards exploration. The deos objects you click around: badges, powerboxes, living screenshots (the rehydratable frustum-snapshots), cells, affordances.* *The substrate — Pharo/Smalltalk LIVENESS. "the smalltalk experience. it's supposed to be pharo-ish." Pharo = a live, image-based, self-describing environment: every object is a live, inspectable, modifiable thing; you can halt anywhere, inspect, change, continue; the whole system is malleable from within itself. deos is ALSO an IDE, debugger, etc. — exactly as Pharo's environment IS its own live IDE/inspector/debugger.*

This dual thesis governed every line of DeOS UI code written between May and August 2026: a user experience combining the uninhibited, tactile magic of 1990s desktop computing with the mathematical rigor of an object-capability operating system where every button press is a verified state transition (a turn), every UI panel is an inspectable cell, and every window can be serialized into a cryptographic snapshot and sent over chat to be rehydrated and executed on another machine.

1. What Lived in Breadstuffs: The 31 Environments, Crates & Surfaces

Breadstuffs was not merely a cryptographic kernel; it was a complete, sovereign operating system environment comprising over 550,000 lines of UI, environment, application, and tooling code. This ecosystem spanned six distinct functional quadrants.

text
