1.1 The Native Desktop Cockpit & Rendering Engine

1. starbridge-v2 (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/starbridge-v2, 194,109 LOC)

  • Scaffold Commit: a7de1c5a4 (2026-06-13); Last Touches: d5ad5a6e4 (2026-09-30).
  • Core Architecture: The flagship native desktop application built using Zed's GPUI framework. It housed 115 source files including the master cockpit (src/cockpit/), containing 21 modules (panels_workspace.rs, panels_main.rs, panels_moldable.rs, panels_web.rs, panels_devtools.rs, panels_app_launcher.rs, live.rs, construct.rs, render.rs, shell_ops.rs).
  • DeOS Desktop Subsystem (src/deos_desktop/, 29 modules): Implemented the full Pharo/Smalltalk-style desktop:
  • world_explorer.rs: Visual hierarchy walker over sovereign cells, authority delegations, and receipts.
  • spotter.rs: Universal command palette and search indexing every Presentable object.
  • halo.rs: Direct-manipulation ring surrounding on-screen cells (tear off, inspect, fork, actuate).
  • systemui_chrome_render.rs & android_window.rs: Cap-badge status bars and quick-setting pull-downs.
  • matrix_room.rs, agent_room.rs, exchange_floor.rs, docgraph_view.rs, face_scroll.rs, toasts.rs.
  • Inhabitation & Rehydration: src/shared_fork.rs and src/distributed_card.rs enabled live cockpit tabs to be detached into independent OS windows (tear_off_tab_unleased), snapshotted to disk, or transmitted across machines.
  • The seL4 Framebuffer Keystone: Commit d92d0526c (2026-06-19) closed the #1 precious milestone: wiring GPUI's offscreen software render directly into the seL4 QEMU ramfb framebuffer (deos-live.system), allowing the full Starbridge v2 cockpit to boot on a formally verified microkernel without an X11 or Wayland host.

2. servo-render (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/servo-render, 24,749 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: 83179110f (2026-06-14); Key Hardening: 5ae26d4ee (2026-07-27).
  • Architecture: A hardware-independent web rendering pipeline designed to run inside an unprivileged seL4 Protection Domain (PD).
  • The SWGL Core: Used Mozilla WebRender's C++17 software rasterizer (swgl 0.68.0) in swgl_context.rs. It implemented gleam::gl::Gl, compiling pure C++ into a CPU-only rasterizer that rendered HTML/CSS directly into a caller-owned RGBA8 buffer (Vec<u8>).
  • Attested Output: In compositor_seam.rs:
rust
  pub fn present_frame(frame: &RgbaFrame, region: Rect) -> FrameReceipt {
      let digest = blake3::hash(&frame.pixels);
      // Digest committed to seL4 CompositorPd gate (T1 non-overlap, T2 label, T3 focus)
      FrameReceipt { digest, bounds: region, timestamp: now() }
  }
  • Libservo Elephant: Contained an opt-in feature --features libservo in webview.rs wiring into SpiderMonkey (mozjs) and Servo's layout engine. Because of mozjs compilation overhead (multi-GB C++ build), SWGL was cleanly decoupled into swgl-standalone so pixel rendering could be tested instantly without building SpiderMonkey.

3. android-cell (Graphideos) (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/android-cell, 11,066 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: 6fae061d0 (2026-06-24); Memory: project-graphideos-android-epoch.md.
  • The Graphideos Concept: Running unmodified Android applications within cap-confined cell sandboxes. servo : web :: android-runtime : android.
  • Key Modules:
  • appfactory.rs: Minted an android-cell from an APK's manifest descriptor; declared permissions defined the exact initial capability set (CapBadgeSet), eliminating ambient Android authority.
  • permgate.rs: Interposed over Android's Context.checkSelfPermission. If a cap badge was dim, the runtime returned PERMISSION_DENIED in-process. Handing over a permission minted a genuine Effect::GrantCapability through the kernel TurnExecutor.
  • intentgate.rs & broadcastgate.rs: Intercepted Android Intents and Binder RPCs, routing them through verified capability checks.
  • runtime/: Shipped MacOsEmulatorRuntime (driving Google Android Emulator over Hypervisor.framework) and CapturedFrameRuntime (headless PNG/RGBA8 tile pipe).

4. dregg-tui (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/dregg-tui, 1,132 LOC)

  • Ratatui-based terminal cockpit displaying the active cell graph, ledger height, witness cursor, and recent receipts for headless server environments and remote SSH sessions.

1.2 Cap-Bounded Reflection & The View-Tree Substrate

5. deos-reflect (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/deos-reflect, 1,420 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: 21ebc84cf (2026-06-23); Fix: b3ef43204 (2026-08-01).
  • Purpose: Cap-bounded, attested reflection over cell substance, completely independent of GPUI. Crawling was strictly per-viewer: a principal can only observe what their authority reaches.
  • Core Types (src/lib.rs):
rust
  pub use substance::{Field, FieldValue, Inspectable, ObjectKind, reflect_cell};
  pub use graph::{GraphEdge, GraphLayer, GraphNode, OcapGraph};
  pub use frustum::Frustum;
  pub use affordances::{Affordance, AffordanceSurface, EffectSummary};
  pub use present::{Presentation, PresentationBody, PresentationKind, ReflectedCell};
  • The Attested Redaction Principle: When inspecting private or committed fields, deos-reflect emits the cryptographic commitment (Poseidon2 / Blake3), never the cleartext value.

6. deos-view (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/deos-view, 19,963 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: ecbbe2252 (2026-06-23); Last Touches: c5ab6469e (2026-08-06).
  • Core Thesis: The card is renderer-independent. deos-js produces declarative UI trees (tree::ViewNode), and deos-view renders that data into pixels or markup.
  • Dual Renders:
  • native (GPUI): bridge::build_live_view executes SpiderMonkey JS applets, extracts ViewNode, and render::AppletView translates nodes (vstack, button, text, bind) into GPUI widgets. Buttons bind directly to World::commit_turn.
  • web (HTML/DOM): web::render_card_document translates the exact same ViewNode tree into a standalone .html string with embedded styling and SVG badges.
  • Progressive Disclosure: ViewNode::Adept, Disclosure::Simple | Disclosure::Adept, and the disclose() AST transform allowed UI elements to hide developer/cryptographic details from casual users while allowing power users to expand raw state constraints and Lean invariants.

7. deos-js, deos-js-core, deos-js-runtime (21,526 LOC combined)

  • Embedded SpiderMonkey runtime executing unprivileged userspace applets.
  • Exposed the deos.ui.* DOM building API, deos.turn.* intent builder, and deos.cell.* query layer.
  • src/attach.rs: Enabled an autonomous agent (Claude Sonnet 4.5 via ACP/MCP) to attach to a live Rc<RefCell<World>>, inspect cells through deos-reflect, synthesize a JavaScript applet, execute it, and commit verified state transitions (0d03f6ee...).

1.3 Developer Panes, Confined Inhabitation & Editors

8. deos-zed (6,672 LOC) & deos-zed-full (4,774 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: 3190aa849 (2026-06-23); Test Harness: 302aea28c (2026-09-30).
  • The Vision: Embedding the full, real Zed editor stack directly into the DeOS cockpit.
  • FirmamentZedFs (src/firmament_zed_fs.rs): Implemented Zed's asynchronous fs::Fs trait over sovereign cells:
  • Files and directories mapped to cells in the sovereign graph.
  • Buffer saves invoked verified kernel turns:
rust
    impl fs::Fs for FirmamentZedFs {
        async fn save(&self, path: &Path, content: &Rope, ...) -> Result<()> {
            let patch = dregg_doc::diff(&self.read(path).await?, content);
            let turn = TurnBuilder::new().apply_patch(self.cell_for_path(path)?, patch).build();
            self.executor.commit_turn(turn).await?;
            Ok(())
        }
    }
  • Validated by three headless integration tests (cargo test --features full-zed): tests/firmament_zed_fs.rs, tests/project_over_cells.rs, and tests/full_workspace_over_cells.rs. The full Zed Workspace, project_panel, outline_panel, and terminal_view mounted cleanly over cells-as-files.

9. deos-terminal (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/deos-terminal, 3,309 LOC)

  • Integrated Alacritty PTY terminal dock pane. Allowed the developer to pop open a shell with ⌘K, executing local commands inside a cap-bounded sub-environment.

10. deos-hermes (/Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/deos-hermes, 24,012 LOC)

  • The confined agent host. Hermes ran autonomous LLM reasoning loops inside an isolated Protection Domain. All external actuation (filesystem, network, editor) was mediated via capability handles, with ambient authority denied (verdict 0xf). Every agent action emitted an irrevocable, verifiable turn receipt.

1.4 Multiplayer Communications & Sovereign Grains

11. deos-matrix (7,706 LOC) & deos-homeserver (13,484 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: c330befc5 (2026-06-22); Memory: project-deos-self-hosting-epoch.md.
  • The Concept: Native Matrix chat (matrix-rust-sdk) re-pilled around sovereign cells:
  • Matrix Rooms = Sovereign Cells; Matrix Identities = Cell Cap Badges; Messages = Verified Turns.
  • The Rehydratable Membrane (src/membrane.rs): The crowning multiplayer primitive. A chat message could encapsulate a membrane: a frustum-culled, cap-bounded snapshot of a live world-fork. The recipient received the message, clicked the membrane, and the cockpit rehydrated the world-fork in-memory. The recipient executed turns within the fork, and a subsequent stitch operation merged the divergent branches back into the sender's ledger using categorical pushouts.
  • deos-homeserver: A Matrix homeserver designed to run as an unprivileged, persistent Sandstorm-style grain on top of the cell store (docs/deos/GRAIN-HOMESERVER.md), making communications self-hosted without relying on third-party Matrix homeservers.

12. Sandstorm Integration: sandstorm-bridge, sandstorm-package, sandstorm-serve (10,393 LOC combined)

  • Origin: Began in May 2026; standalone package parser extracted by Ember in commit 028bc0271 (2026-09-26: "Extract reusable signed Sandstorm package parser for Mini hosting").
  • Mechanism:
  • sandstorm-package: Decompressed real .spk packages (8-byte magic Spk1, xz decompression, Ed25519 signature verification where App ID = public key, and Cap'n Proto archive decoding).
  • sandstorm-bridge: Mapped Sandstorm grain concepts directly to DeOS cells:
  • Grain /var directory = Sovereign cell umem heap.
  • Grain lifecycle (create, wake, sleep, delete) = Cell lease and persistence management.
  • Sandstorm Powerbox = Attenuable credentials (dga1_..., ed25519 caveat chains).
  • HTTP Bridge: Interposed X-Sandstorm-* identity and permission headers derived from the holder's cell capability.

1.5 The Starbridge App Ecosystem & Web Surfaces

13. app-framework (21,851 LOC) & starbridge-apps (114,679 LOC)

  • Origin Commit: 1e25f8fc8 (2026-05-24); Census: docs/deos/STARBRIDGE-APPS-CENSUS.md.
  • The Immutable Law:

"The answer is never Effect::FooApp." *When an app wants a domain Effect, the missing primitive is the generic one (Caveat, StateConstraint, Authorization, Factory) it would compose from. Every starbridge-app must be buildable from dregg-native primitives only.*

  • The 32 Apps:
  • nameservice: Hierarchical domain registration with WriteOnce and Monotonic slot caveats.
  • identity: Selective credential disclosure, verifiable presentation, and revocation.
  • subscription: Cap-metered bounded ring buffer for pub/sub message consumption.
  • governed-namespace: Threshold multisig voting on namespace registration.
  • compartment-workflow-mandate: Clearance-graph workflow progression and spend policies.
  • storage-gateway-mandate: Bounded volume read/write gateway for blob storage.
  • privacy-voting: Monotonic poll counters with per-voter one-vote-per-cell caveats.
  • bounty-board: First-claimer-wins bounty lifecycle (POSTED -> CLAIMED -> SUBMITTED -> PAID).
  • compute-exchange: Escrow budget state machine (AffineEq(PAID + REFUNDED == BUDGET)).
  • gallery: Commit-reveal art curation using sealed submission boards.
  • sealed-auction: Sealed-bid uniform price auction clearing.
  • domains: Distributed DNS-style authoritative zone cells.
  • edge-mandate: Attenuated execution mandates at network edge nodes.
  • escrow-market: Multi-party conditional asset escrow.
  • first-room: Smalltalk-style beginner world and cell tutorial.
  • guard: Capability revocation gates and watchdog contracts.
  • kvstore: Verified content-addressed key-value store.
  • org: DAO structure with role hierarchies and treasury allocations.
  • polis: Quadratic voting and Deliberative polling.
  • site-host: Static website hosting served directly from cell heaps.
  • supply-chain-provenance: Multi-hop physical asset custody tracking.
  • swarm-orchestration: Multi-agent work distribution and receipt settlement.
  • tool-access-delegation: Fine-grained attenuation of CLI/API tool execution.
  • tussle: Competitive multi-agent game state machine.
  • execution-lease: Computron resource metering and lease renewals.
  • vat: E-style isolated event-loop containers with CapTP messaging.
  • agent-orchestration: Hierarchical subagent scheduling.
  • agent-provenance: Verification of LLM model signatures and prompts.
  • billing: Usage-based micro-invoicing.
  • branch-stitch-multiplayer: Multi-user document branch merge demonstrator.
  • shared: Common Preact inspectors and JSON factory descriptors.
  • starbridge-web-surface (17,030 LOC): Browser-based dregg:// cell URI resolver and transclusion.

14. deos-leptos (4,108 LOC) & deos-web-cells (4,667 LOC)

  • deos-leptos: Explored Rust web frontend reactivity: Cell state = Leptos RwSignal; Affordances = reactive UI components; Rehydration = per-viewer SSR hydration.
  • deos-web-cells: Implemented live DOM snapshot cells (BundleKind::LiveDomSnapshot), enabling declarative HTML/DOM subtrees to be content-addressed, transcluded across documents, and stored directly on the cell ledger.

15. Frontends & Portals: drex-web-v2, launchpad-web, portal, extension

  • drex-web-v2 (1,790 LOC, da95a0fa7): Clean Preact + @preact/signals trading interface. Implemented a 3-tier view dial (Open = public order book, Shielded = blurred liquidity amounts, Dark = sealed-bid commit-reveal). Handshook with window.dregg.
  • launchpad-web (2,245 LOC, e1247989b): Fair-launch token interface driven directly by live node turn streams (POST /turn/submit). Bypassed traditional EVM bonding curves in favor of batch-cleared uniform-price auctions.
  • portal (12,006 LOC, 1f39d35f2): Trustless network explorer (portal.dregg.studio) embedding the dregg-wasm recursive STARK light client. Verified block headers and turn receipts locally in the tab.
  • extension (74,905 LOC, 06b366e54): Firefox/Chrome browser extension exposing window.dregg. Managed BIP-39 mnemonic phrases, signed transactions, and brokered capability delegations.
  • spween-dregg (8,183 LOC, 4ddb4f1f6): Bound the Spween narrative-choice DSL to sovereign world-cells. Every player dialogue choice was executed as a verified turn, producing an unforgeable, un-retconnable receipt chain. Included VoteEngine for collective Twitch-plays-style storytelling.

2. The Moldable Inspector & Gadget Framework

In starbridge-v2/src/presentable.rs (commit 800945db6) and docs/deos/INSPECTOR-FRAMEWORK.md, Ember adapted Tudor Gîrba's Glamorous Toolkit (GT) and Pharo moldable inspector philosophy to the cryptographic ledger.

text
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                  THE MOLDABLE INSPECTOR PIPELINE                                  |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                                                                                   |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|   |                      The 7 Presentation Kinds (enum PresentationKind)                     |   |
|   |  1. RawFields: Mandatory structural floor (field-tree with attested redactions)           |   |
|   |  2. Graph: Ocap delegations, cell reachability closure, state machine transitions         |   |
|   |  3. DomainVisual: High-level domain visualizer (ballot box, token ring, clearance tape)   |   |
|   |  4. Affordances: Cap-gated message roster; illuminated = allowed, dimmed = refused       |   |
|   |  5. Provenance: Irrevocable receipt chain, turn author signatures, blame graph            |   |
|   |  6. Invariant: Active state constraints, Lean theorem checks, conservation proofs        |   |
|   |  7. Source: Underlying Lean / Objective Bend / AST source definition                      |   |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|                                                |                                                  |
|                                                v                                                  |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|   |                         Interactive Gadgets (trait CommittingGadget)                      |   |
|   |                                                                                           |   |
|   |    [User Input / Dial]  --->  IntentDraft  --->  simulate()  --->  commit_turn()          |   |
|   |                                                      |                    |               |   |
|   |                                             (Predict Turn)         (Verified Turn)        |   |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|                                                |                                                  |
|                                                v                                                  |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|   |                         The L1--L10 Shipped Inspector Ladder                              |   |
|   |   L1: presentable.rs         L2: predicate_composer.rs      L3: turn_builder.rs           |   |
|   |   L4: cap_inspector.rs       L5: cell_inspector.rs          L6: receipts_inspector.rs     |   |
|   |   L7: token_inspector.rs     L8: federation_inspector.rs    L9: circuit_inspector.rs      |   |
|   |                              L10: settlement_inspector.rs                                 |   |
|   +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+   |
|                                                                                                   |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

2.1 The Core Traits: Presentable, Gadget, CommittingGadget

Unlike standard debuggers that provide a static, one-size-fits-all JSON or property-grid tree, every DeOS protocol object implemented Presentable:

rust
// starbridge-v2/src/presentable.rs
pub trait Presentable {
    /// Returns the set of named presentation faces this object supports.
    fn presentations(&self, ctx: &PresentationContext) -> Vec<Presentation>;

    /// Render a specific presentation face.
    fn render_presentation(&self, kind: PresentationKind, ctx: &PresentationContext) -> PresentationBody;
}

pub trait Gadget {
    type Output;
    fn render_editor(&mut self, ui: &mut GadgetUi) -> Option<Self::Output>;
}

pub trait CommittingGadget: Gadget {
    /// Predict-then-commit: every UI mutation stages an IntentDraft,
    /// verifies the turn against local invariants, and commits to the ledger.
    fn stage_intent(&self) -> Result<IntentDraft, GadgetError>;
    fn commit(&mut self, executor: &mut TurnExecutor) -> Result<Receipt, GadgetError>;
}

2.2 The Shipped L1–L10 Ladder

The framework successfully replaced monolithic UI views with modular inspector modules:

  • L1 — Presentation Spine (presentable.rs): Base registry, presentation kinds, and Spotter palette.
  • L2 — Predicate Composer (predicate_composer.rs): Visual builder for StateConstraint predicates (WriteOnce, Monotonic, AffineLe).
  • L3 — Turn Builder (turn_builder.rs): Effect stage and intent drafter.
  • L4 — Cap Inspector (cap_inspector.rs): Visualized capability attenuation chains and caveats.
  • L5 — Cell Inspector (cell_inspector.rs): Live cell slot viewer and value inspector.
  • L6 — Receipts Inspector (receipts_inspector.rs): Turn receipt auditor and execution trace walker.
  • L7 — Token Inspector (token_inspector.rs): Balance rings and conservation law verifier.
  • L8 — Federation Inspector (federation_inspector.rs): Blocklace DAG consensus viewer.
  • L9 — Circuit Inspector (circuit_inspector.rs): AIR constraint polynomial viewer.
  • L10 — Settlement Inspector (settlement_inspector.rs): Cross-chain bridge escrow and settlement status.

3. The Diaspora: How Ideas & Surfaces Spread Into Modern Projects

When the monolithic Breadstuffs repository froze in August–October 2026, its ideas did not die; they dispersed into specialized downstream projects.

text
                                    /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs
                                                 |
         +--------------------+------------------+-------------------+--------------------+
         |                    |                                      |                    |
         v                    v                                      v                    v
  ~/dev/delvetalk2      ~/dev/spween & spw-workbench           ~/dev/minidregg     ~/dev/obend/obend-gui
  ----------------      ----------------------------           ---------------     ---------------------
  - Inhabited Town      - Standalone DSL crate                 - "DeOS Crown"      - Lightweight runner
  - Plain-words Law     - VS Code extension & LSP                Lean theorems     - 10 ProjectionKinds
  - Cards & Spells      - Semantic brace operators             - Compressed core   - LawDial controls
  - AT Protocol repo    - Narrative state trees                  (<150k lines)     - SVG / HTML views
  - "No coins" rule     - Collective branch choice             - Zero twins        - Picard-Lindelof ODE

3.1 Diaspora Destinations

1. delvetalk & delvetalk2 (/Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2, branch foundation)

  • The Inhabited World Reborn: DelveTalk inherited the soul of DeOS—the world of durable, programmable, inhabited objects. But it radically subtracted the heavy GUI scaffolding:
  • Monolithic GPUI replaced by Plain Text & Cards: Objects are described by cards; interactions take the form of natural-language replies or concise "spells" (delvetalk garden-1 plant).
  • Hob the Reader: Ingestion layer translating natural language into admitted action spells.
  • AT Protocol Repository: Town state federated as signed AT Protocol records (did:web:gsb.fg-goose.online).
  • "No coins" Rule: Strict policy against exposing raw hashes, DIDs, or blockchain jargon on user-facing surfaces.

2. spween & spw-workbench (/Users/ember/dev/spween, /Users/ember/dev/spw-workbench)

  • spween: Decoupled into an elegant, educational DSL parser and interpreter for interactive fiction, maintaining the trait-based EffectHandler abstraction.
  • spw-workbench: Evolved into a dedicated organelle for .spw workspaces, featuring a complete Language Server Protocol (LSP) server, CLI, runtime, and VS Code extension (extensions/vscode-spw).

3. minidregg (/Users/ember/dev/minidregg)

  • The DeOS Crown Preserved in Lean: In minidregg/ATLAS.md (§3, item 14), Ember codified what survived Breadstuffs:

*"The deos crown: surface-as-cap, membrane non-amplification, replayedDeterministic_iff_confined (confinement IS the deterministic-replay fragment), affordance-soundness; DocMerge as LUB / pushout products; fog-of-war non-interference. Identity: affordance = cap-gated effect-template; firing = turn; turn = receipt — one machinery, three faces (human/authored/agentic)."*

  • tests/objective-bend-source/DeosCounter.obend: Directly ported Breadstuffs' deos-js counter applet into formal Objective Bend syntax, modeling turns as Activity<Plan, Response, Nat>.

4. obend-gui (/Users/ember/dev/obend/crates/obend-gui, 2,088 LOC)

  • The Modern Viewport Successor: Replaced starbridge-v2's massive 194k-line GPUI monolith with a tight, modular notebook runner and hardware CAD projection previewer.
  • Supports 10 distinct ProjectionKinds: Circuit, Netlist, Timing, Gdsii, Waveform, CellQuote, NotebookCell, ProofTree, ContractBadge, ClearingLedger.
  • Implements interactive SVG/HTML rendering for Picard–Lindelöf ODE flowpipe slices, 5-axis CNC waypoints, eFPGA heatmaps, and live .free / .press / .locked LawDial controls.

3.2 Lessons Learned in the Transition

1. Why Rigid Monolithic Shells Failed

  • The Build Lock Nightmare: In July 2026, local developer iteration ground to a halt because compiling starbridge-v2 locked the entire target directory. Building the desktop cockpit pulled GPUI, Alacritty, Servo, SpiderMonkey, Matrix SDK, and the Lean kernel archive (libdregg_lean.a), resulting in a 292MB binary that took minutes to link and frequently jammed (reference-persvati-remote-build).
  • Duplicate Dependency Crises: Direct dependencies collided with nested dependencies (e.g. workspace GPUI adb902652 vs gpui-component's internal 54fbcb6943), requiring complex workspace overrides.
  • Immediate-Mode UI Re-execution Overhead: Commit f27fd4169 documented that the cockpit repainted carded surfaces as frozen snapshots every frame, BLAKE3-hashing the entire state root on every render tick because projection memoization had not been integrated.
  • The Autonomous Swarm Failure: In ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-delvetalk/memory/ember-working-style.md:21:

*"Three hours of autonomous codex produced 45 commits and a 124K-line repo ember called 'horseshit' and 'chug'; the failure was orchestration and refusal to subtract, not effort."*

The remedy was radical subtraction: separate the data models, drop monolithic GUI frameworks, and deliver user agency through lightweight, composable surfaces.

2. Why GPUI Cockpit Builds Needed Discipline

  • Monolithic cockpits must not bundle full editor workspaces, full web browsers, and full chat servers into the primary binary. The split pioneered by deos-view (declarative ViewNode tree) and obend-gui (HTTP/WebSocket server streaming interactive SVG/HTML viewports) demonstrated that rendering belongs at the edge, while state remains pure, verifiable data.

3. How Sandstorm .spk Grains Evolved

  • Sandstorm proved the elegance of capability-secured application grains with private /var heaps. However, Sandstorm grains lacked decentralized, light-client witnessability—users had to trust the central Sandstorm supervisor.
  • In DeOS, grains became content-addressed sovereign cells with verifiable umem heaps. In minidregg and delvetalk2, this evolved into sovereign objects where state transitions are mathematically checked against a plain-words law and recorded on an AT Protocol or blocklace journal.

4. What Got Dropped, Parked, or Underelaborated

During the late-summer 2026 pivot to kernel verification and Mini/Nano rebuilds, several advanced surfaces were parked. These assets represent hundreds of thousands of lines of high-value work:

  1. deos-zed & deos-zed-full (11,446 LOC): The proven integration of Zed's Workspace over FirmamentZedFs. Parked due to GPUI/Zed upstream churn. Left behind: a working editor where saving a file emits a verified Pijul/DocCore patch turn.
  2. deos-matrix & deos-homeserver (21,190 LOC): The rehydratable membrane chat client and grain-hosted Matrix homeserver. Parked when the consensus layer detached from the legacy node. Left behind: the UI mechanism for sending live, forkable world branches inside chat messages.
  3. deos-reflect (1,420 LOC): The pure, cap-bounded reflection engine. Parked during the crate-split campaign. Left behind: the only audit-proven inspector that redacts secrets and graphs capability blast radiuses without pulling GPUI.
  4. servo-render (24,749 LOC): Software-rasterized web browsing using WebRender SWGL. Parked due to SpiderMonkey build complexity. Left behind: working C++17 software rasterization emitting RGBA8 tiles verified by cryptographic digests.
  5. android-cell (Graphideos, 11,066 LOC): Android container/emulator isolation with capability badging. Parked as out-of-scope for the kernel bakeoff. Left behind: real Context.checkSelfPermission interposition and SystemUI capability status bars.
  6. The 34 Apps in starbridge-apps (114,679 LOC): 32 complete domain applications (voting, auctions, escrow, identity, bounty boards, compute markets) adhering to "The answer is never Effect::FooApp". Left behind: complete factory descriptors and slot caveats (WriteOnce, Monotonic, AffineLe).
  7. drex-web-v2 & launchpad-web (4,035 LOC): Modern Preact + Signals trading terminals with 3-tier dials (Open/Shielded/Dark) and live turn-stream fair launches.
  8. extension & portal (86,911 LOC): Browser extension capability broker and in-browser recursive STARK light client verifier.
  9. moldable-inspector (L1–L10 ladder): The 7 presentation faces and gadget composition spine, currently locked inside the legacy starbridge-v2 crate.

5. Concrete Revival Roadmap: Re-Attaching Surfaces to Obend, Mini & DelveTalk2

The diaspora surfaces can be systematically revived by mounting them onto modern hosts: obend-gui, delvetalk2, minidregg, and spw-workbench.

5.2 Phased Execution Plan

Phase 1: Semantic Re-Attachment (Immediate — T1)

  1. DelveTalk2 App Ingestion:
  2. Extract the 8 core factory descriptors from starbridge-apps/ (bounty-board, privacy-voting, nameservice, identity, compute-exchange, gallery, subscription, governed-namespace).
  3. Re-express their state constraints (WriteOnce, Monotonic, AffineLe) as plain-language laws and Objective Bend activities in delvetalk2/capsules/.
  4. Moldable Inspector in Obend-GUI:
  5. Lift PresentationKind and the Presentable trait from starbridge-v2/src/presentable.rs into obend-gui/src/viewport.rs.
  6. Wire RawFields, Graph, DomainVisual, Invariant, and Source into obend-gui's existing 10 ProjectionKinds, enabling rich visual inspection of Lean goals, Picard–Lindelöf ODE flowpipes, and hardware layouts.
  7. Spween Narrative Choice in DelveTalk:
  8. Reconnect spween to delvetalk2, allowing cards to present interactive narrative scenes where replying with a spell advances the passage and records an immutable receipt.

Phase 2: Web & Communications Decoupling (Near-Term — T2)

  1. Lightweight Card Web View:
  2. Port deos-view/src/tree.rs and web.rs into obend-gui/src/server.rs.
  3. Provide an HTTP/WebSocket endpoint rendering declarative ViewNode cards directly in browsers without requiring GPUI or native desktop builds.
  4. AT Protocol Rehydratable Membranes:
  5. Adapt deos-matrix/src/membrane.rs to DelveTalk's AT Protocol sync layer.
  6. When a user shares a room or object state, emit an AT Protocol record holding the cap-bounded frustum snapshot. Any follower can rehydrate the object, experiment locally, and propose an admitted patch.
  7. Sandstorm Package Hosting:
  8. Connect sandstorm-package (028bc0271) to minidregg, allowing standalone .spk archives to be uploaded and executed in confined WebAssembly environments.

Phase 3: The Sovereign Workbench (Frontier — T3)

  1. Virtual Cell Filesystem for Editors:
  2. Extract the async FS abstractions from deos-zed-full/src/firmament_zed_fs.rs.
  3. Implement a VS Code / Zed Language Server extension in spw-workbench that mounts cell:// URIs, enabling developers to edit live on-ledger objects with automatic turn-receipt emission on save.
  4. Deterministic SWGL Document Bakes:
  5. Recommission servo-render's standalone SWGL rasterizer to produce cryptographic PNG snapshots of rendered cards and hardware viewports, guaranteeing identical pixel output across macOS, Linux, and seL4 without depending on host GPU drivers.

6. Conclusion: The Return of the Living Image

The tragedy of the original DeOS effort in breadstuffs was not an error of vision; it was an error of packaging and mass. By attempting to construct the compiler, the proof system, the microkernel, the GPUI desktop, the browser engine, the editor, the chat system, and 34 applications in a single, interlocked monorepo, build friction and dependency cascades eventually choked developer iteration.

The diaspora to delvetalk2, minidregg, spween, and obend-gui was a necessary retreat to sanity—a subtraction campaign that restored fast cycles, modular boundaries, and clean formal methods. Now that minidregg has settled the verified kernel core, obend has established the projection viewports, and delvetalk2 has proven the power of an inhabited town of objects, the rich surfaces of DeOS and Starbridge can be reclaimed.

By re-attaching the moldable inspector, the rehydratable membrane, the 32 starbridge apps, and the card-based view-tree to these lean modern substrates, Ember's original 1999 AOL wonder—the live, malleable, self-describing computing image where a child can click with delight and an adept can inspect every mathematical invariant—is finally ready to live.

Strand 18 — The Breadstuffs Diaspora: 28 DreggNet World/RPG Crates, Verified Game Surfaces, and the Path to Revival

Vertical: breadstuffs (dreggnet-*, dungeon-on-dregg, mud-dregg, procgen-dregg, ugc-dregg, narrator, interactive-fiction-demo, game-turn-slice, dregg-automatafl, dregg-multiway-tug, poa, poa-curator, poa-solana-gate, poa-web, rhlp-web) · delvetalk / delvetalk2 (world/kinds, world/objects, obend) · allgame (Tulip residents: Claude, Yuè月, Kestrel, MotherMythos) · automatafl (zero-twin Lean 4 core) · path-of-angels (~/dev/path-of-angels) · dclutch (Solana DEX & SimLife3 creature aquarium). Compiled: 2026-11-02 by a Claude Opus 5.5 archaeology lane, read-only over the repositories, from git trees, logs, design documents, and per-project memory corpora under ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-breadstuffs/memory/ (project-path-of-angels-platform.md, project-game-engine-strategy.md, project-game-surfaces-closure-campaign.md, project-automatafl-verified-game.md), plus docs/AUTHORING-DUNGEONS.md, docs/CROWD-STREAM-ENGINE-DESIGN.md, GOAL-COLLECTIVE-FICTION-DEMO.md, and GOAL-PATH-OF-ANGELS-EXCELLENCE.md. 1. What lived in breadstuffs — The 28 dreggnet-* crates and verified engines

The monolithic repository /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs (HEAD commit 22fad151e on 2026-10-01) housed the complete, verified, multi-actor world and game engine constructed by Ember between May and October 2026. The workspace spanned 656 lines in its root Cargo.toml, incorporating 12 major DeOS/Starbridge framework subsystems, 28 dreggnet-* crates, and full Lean 4 metatheory proofs.

1.1 The game engine thesis: "Every move is a receipt"

As documented in project-game-engine-strategy.md:11-16:

"EVERY MOVE IS A RECEIPT. dregg is a verified game ENGINE, and the proof of that is a 3-game PORTFOLIO, not one game. A turn is a cap-gated transition the executor re-checks against constraints (StateConstraint teeth) and folds into a succinct verify_history-accepted proof. An illegal move has no satisfying witness. $DREGG buys SERVICES, never power; the world runs as SEASONS, where honest devnet churn is a feature."

The architecture established an essential design law formulated by Ember:

"The architecture lesson that survived (ember's correction): a complex game's mechanics are a Custom VK / a Custom leaf, NOT StateConstraint teeth. Teeth are for simple state; a Custom AIR is for the transition." (project-game-engine-strategy.md:40-42).

1.2 Deep excavation of the 28 dreggnet-* crates

The 28 dreggnet-* crates were not speculative outlines; they were built across coordinated megaswarms (commits 45ef8a7d2, 928c0eb12, ed78ed52d, and 1ba6a4a1e), tested against live kernels, and bound into a single enmeshed dependency lattice.

1. dreggnet-saga — The Enmeshment Proof

  • Location: dreggnet-saga/src/lib.rs:1-65
  • Commits: a65b63f13 (2026-07-14), 422326270 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Proves that the eight isolated feature crates compose through object identity without re-derivation or semantic translation:
  • Run Currency: The ugc_dregg::Completion pointer passes directly from dreggnet-quest to dreggnet_cheevo::CheevoLedger::earn and dreggnet_guild::leaderboard::GuildBoard::record_clear.
  • Item Currency: The dreggnet_asset::AssetId 32-byte content address addresses crafted outputs in [dreggnet-craft], moves through atomic escrow in [dreggnet-trade], and persists across characters in [dreggnet-gear].
  • Driven Sequence: Party muster $\to$ Faction check $\to$ Quest turn-in $\to$ Cheevo mint $\to$ Guild aggregate $\to$ Craft forge $\to$ Trade atomic swap.

2. dreggnet-tavern — The Persistent Co-Inhabitation Hub

  • Location: dreggnet-tavern/src/lib.rs:1-55
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Re-homes node/src/shared_world.rs. A headless Dregg node runs a tavern GM program allocating: (1) a shared BOARD cell, (2) presence SEATS, (3) private STALLS, and (4) a shared PARTY roster.
  • Kernel Enforcement: Clients subscribe to live SSE event streams (dregg_sdk_net::NodeEvents). Patron A touching Patron B's private stall receives an explicit CapabilityNotHeld refusal receipt; seat presence cannot be forged because turns are signed by dregg_sdk::AgentCipherclerk.

3. dreggnet-guild — Cap-Bounded Guilds & Escrow Treasuries

  • Location: dreggnet-guild/src/lib.rs:1-60
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee:
  • Membership as Capability: Guild::admit installs a player cell holding an unforgeable capability token to the shared guild room (World). Non-member writes fail with CapabilityNotHeld.
  • Verified Clear Aggregation: GuildBoard aggregates clears verified by fresh replay (ugc_dregg::verify_completion) alongside the WriteOnce CharacterSheet.dead flag.
  • Escrow-Custodied Treasury: GuildTreasury integrates with starbridge_escrow_market::SealedEscrowMarket. Withdrawals require the original depositor's signature, preventing officers from absconding with group funds.

4. dreggnet-party — Seated Cooperative Division of Labor

  • Location: dreggnet-party/src/lib.rs:1-55
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Escapes "Twitch Plays Pokemon mush" by enforcing a strict division of labor across $N$ seated player-cells (Party::muster):
  • Role Capabilities: Tank controls front-rank cell; Scout controls trap/lock cell; Mage controls spell + focus cells; Healer controls rally + focus cells. Moves outside an assigned role return CapabilityNotHeld.
  • Shared Focus Pool: Enforces a party-wide StateConstraint::FieldLteField budget across Mage and Healer.
  • Collective Forks: Critical path choices open a PartyFork resolved via collective_choice quorum certificates.
  • Loot Distribution: Party::split_loot writes committed shares into WriteOnce cell slots.

5. dreggnet-council — Collective Governance Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-council/src/lib.rs:1-50
  • Commit: ed78ed52d (2026-07-12)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Extends the Offering trait to governance:
  • propose: opens a poll on collective_choice::VoteEngine.
  • vote: single-use WriteOnce(VOTE) ballot signed by custody key; double voting fails closed.
  • enact: checks the polis affine quorum predicate $M \cdot \text{RESOLVED} - \sum \text{TALLY} \le 0$. Below quorum, enact is refused and leaves state unchanged.

6. dreggnet-companion — Fused Assets & Progression Cells

  • Location: dreggnet-companion/src/lib.rs:1-55
  • Commit: 928c0eb12 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Fuses an owned, tradeable dreggnet_asset note (hatched from a provably fair procgen_dregg::verified_stream draw tail) with a progression cell running dungeon_on_dregg::progression::hero_story:
  • Multicell Predicate: A companion run buff is admitted if and only if the companion cell's finalized root matches an ObservedFieldEquals state constraint at level >= N.
  • Permadeath Stakes: The companion's dead slot is WriteOnce-final and un-undoable.

7. dreggnet-quest — Gated Committed Quest Cells

  • Location: dreggnet-quest/src/lib.rs:1-55
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Replaces client-side counter tallies with kernel-enforced WriteOnce step-flags and BoundedBy(step_2 <- step_1) step-order constraints. Quest turn-in gates reward granting behind a StateConstraint::FieldGte on total steps cleared.

8. dreggnet-craft — The Provably-Fair Deflationary Sink

  • Location: dreggnet-craft/src/lib.rs:1-50
  • Commit: ec9104378 (2026-07-15)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: The economy's essential deflationary sink. Consumes a typed multiset of owned materials via owner-gated burns (AssetWorld::revoke). Rolls two provably-fair draws over committed RecipeBook weights using a craft seed derived from beacon randomness, recipe ID, and sorted input asset IDs.

9. dreggnet-cheevo — ISA-Enforced Soulbound Achievements

  • Location: dreggnet-cheevo/src/lib.rs:1-50
  • Commit: ec9104378 (2026-07-15)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: CheevoLedger::earn re-executes playthroughs through ugc_dregg::verify_completion, testing predicates (depth reached, zero-death clears, turn ceilings) over committed state vectors. Mints an asset with field soulbound = 1. The asset layer transfer instruction gates on FieldEquals(soulbound, 0); transfer attempts are refused by the hardware-level executor ISA, preventing account laundering.

10. dreggnet-trade — Atomic Trustless Swaps

  • Location: dreggnet-trade/src/lib.rs:1-50
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Binds asset transfers to starbridge-escrow-market sealed escrow. Supports asset $\leftrightarrow$ asset and asset $\leftrightarrow$ $DREGG value. Settle executes both legs atomically; missing legs can be reclaimed via timeout, eliminating counterparty risk.

11. dreggnet-faction — Gated Reputation State

  • Location: dreggnet-faction/src/lib.rs:1-50
  • Commit: 45ef8a7d2 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Models faction standing ("The Feud at the Ashenmoor Gate": Embers vs. Tide) as committed cell slots. Reputation slots are Monotonic; pledging loyalty to one faction decrements the opposing faction's ceiling slot (embers_ceiling). Betrayal flags are WriteOnce and seal faction content permanently.

12. dreggnet-gear — Cross-Cell Owned Equipment

  • Location: dreggnet-gear/src/lib.rs:1-45
  • Commit: 928c0eb12 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Equipping an item verifies ownership across cells via ObservedFieldEquals into a finalized asset note. Connects class-locked talents directly to the previously dormant spells.rs spellbook (Mage $\ne$ Warrior). Talents cost death-earned echoes, strictly rejecting pay-to-win.

13. dreggnet-adventure — Native Descent Offering Integration

  • Location: dreggnet-adventure/src/lib.rs:1-40
  • Commit: 4696e6950 (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Hosts NativeDescentOffering emitting Lean transition programs (PROGRAM_JSON). Replays runs through a fresh executor before progression accepts them via NativeDescentRun.

14. dreggnet-verified-completion — Universal Completion Interface

  • Location: dreggnet-verified-completion/src/lib.rs:1-40
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Boundary struct providing immutable run identity:
rust
pub struct VerifiedCompletion {
    program_id: [u8; 32],
    world_root: [u8; 32],
    completion_root: [u8; 32],

15. dreggnet-surfaces — The DO-ONCE ViewNode Frontend

  • Location: dreggnet-surfaces/src/lib.rs:1-60
  • Commit: 1ba6a4a1e (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Writing Offering::render -> ViewNode once lights up native cockpit, Web, Discord bot, and Telegram Mini Apps. Surfaces include TradeOffering, InventoryOffering, CheevoShowcase, GuildPage, and CraftOffering.

16. dreggnet-sprite — In-House Deterministic SVG Generator

  • Location: dreggnet-sprite/src/lib.rs
  • Commit: 1ba6a4a1e (2026-07-14)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Pure deterministic asset-to-SVG procedural generator seeded by content hashes, ensuring zero external asset hosting dependencies.

17. dreggnet-offerings — Offering Lifecycle & Signed Ladder

  • Location: dreggnet-offerings/src/lib.rs
  • Commits: 6fa643d05, 295b7f73d (2026-07-16)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Implements SessionPolicy (LRU, TTL, rate limits) in OfferingHost. Introduces Attribution{Signed, Asserted} making trust visible, and persists signed-counter floors across service restarts to prevent replay attacks.

18. dreggnet-game-board — STARK Recursive Multi-Round Test Harness

  • Location: dreggnet-game-board/src/lib.rs
  • Commit: bd0d609c79 (2026-07-25)
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Houses the capstone STARK folding tests (tests/multi_round_fold.rs:1042), verifying full multi-round conflict board games in 1663 seconds of recursive proving.

19. dreggnet-asset — The Canonical Verifiable Asset Layer

  • Location: dreggnet-asset/src/lib.rs:1-35
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Implements a sovereign UTXO-style note lineage (v0 -> v1 -> v2) where each version is a cell authorized by its holder's Ed25519 birth key. Authority genuinely moves to successor cells rather than mutating in place. spent := 1, prev = note_digest(v_i), and asset_id is carried unchanged via WriteOnce.

20. dreggnet-market — Sealed-Bid Batch Cleared Market Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-market/src/lib.rs:1-35
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Offering #commerce. Wraps the sealed-bid auction substrate:
  • LIST: Spawns an auction cell via CreateCellFromFactory with StrictMonotonic(PHASE) and WriteOnce commit boards.
  • BID: Sealed digest BLAKE3(bidder || value || nonce) committed on-chain; double-bidding is rejected by WriteOnce.
  • SETTLE: Verifies ring settlement ([settle_ring_verified]) enforcing per-asset conservation $\sum \delta = 0$.

21. dreggnet-compute — Metered Compute Job Exchange Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-compute/src/lib.rs:1-35
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Offering #compute over starbridge_compute_exchange. Requesters post jobs with WriteOnce(BUDGET). Workers claim jobs with price $\le$ budget via FieldLteField(BID <= BUDGET). Settlement releases payment to the worker (PAID := claim, REFUNDED := budget - claim).

22. dreggnet-discord-identity — Deterministic Custodial Seed Derivation

  • Location: dreggnet-discord-identity/src/lib.rs:1-30
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Single source of truth for Discord identity derivation:
text
seed = BLAKE3_derive_key("dregg-discord-bot-v1", bot_secret || discord_user_id_le)

Used identically in discord-bot and dreggnet-web Activity (/da), preventing user identity drift across web and chat surfaces.

23. dreggnet-hermes — Hosted Confined Agent Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-hermes/src/lib.rs:1-30
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Offering #1. Wraps an AgentRuntime with root tokens derived from session seeds. Tool calls pass through ToolGateway checking ToolGrant rate limits and Charge value budgets; over-mandate proposals are refused fail-closed by the executor.

24. dreggnet-grain — Sandstorm-Style Sandboxed App Session

  • Location: dreggnet-grain/src/lib.rs:1-30
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Offering #2. Admits grain workers via grain_turn::ToolGatewayMinter under ToolGrant { rate_limit: budget }. Turn transitions advance calls_made counters; when exhausted, the delegAdmit rate leg fails and refuses further execution.

25. dreggnet-names — Governed Sovereign Namespace Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-names/src/lib.rs:1-30
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Offering over starbridge_nameservice. Maps strings to sovereign cells with WriteOnce(NAME_HASH) (first-claim tooth), Monotonic(EXPIRY), WriteOnce(REVOKED), and F1..F7 owner-authorization predicates.

26. dreggnet-doc — Collaborative Multi-Editor Document Offering

  • Location: dreggnet-doc/src/lib.rs:1-30
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Collaborative productivity offering. Edits execute as verified turns via MultiEditorDoc::edit, landing receipts on per-agent chains. Edits without edit capabilities fail with CapabilityNotHeld; antichain clashes are rejected by conflict semantics.

27. dreggnet-tournament — Single-Elimination Verifiable Brackets

  • Location: dreggnet-tournament/src/lib.rs:1-35
  • Mechanical Guarantee: Single-elimination tournament bracket over $N$ competitors. Each round runs on a fresh, beacon-seeded universe; competitors advance strictly upon submitting a run verified by ugc_dregg::verify_completion or verify_proof_completion.

28. dreggnet-audit, dreggnet-catalog, dreggnet-web, dreggnet-telegram, dreggnet-wechat, dreggnet-prove-service

  • Audit & Catalog: Maintains verification descriptor sets and schema registries.
  • Transports & Gateways: Provides Axum web servers, Telegram Mini App HMAC gateways, WeChat endpoints, and remote STARK proof aggregation pipelines.

1.3 Verified Game Engines in breadstuffs

1. dungeon-on-dregg and the .dungeon Biconditional Compiler

Landing in commits c338e1e2a and 1dd8a566b, dungeon-on-dregg/src/lib.rs mapped dungeon crawling directly to an EmbeddedExecutor cell:

  • Cell slot 0 is PASSAGE_SLOT, serving as the player's program counter. Slot 1 tracks key items, slot 2 depth, slot 3 gold.
  • In dungeon-on-dregg/src/dsl/ (parse.rs, validate.rs, ir.rs, compile.rs), Ember created the .dungeon text format documented in docs/AUTHORING-DUNGEONS.md.
  • Biconditional Translation Validation: In dungeon-on-dregg/src/dsl/compile.rs:1-60, compile_world lowers a parsed GameWorld onto spween::Scene, lowers through compile_scene, and verifies against check_lowering: any dropped gate is flagged MISSING, and any injected gate is flagged PHANTOM.
  • The Spend Counter Pattern: Exclusion rules (oath, once) enforce that actions can only be taken once by checking that a counter is <= 0 in pre-state and bumping it += 1, which lowers to a post-state FieldLte { value: 1 }. A second attempt produces a post-state of 2 and triggers an executor Refused outcome.
rust
pub fn validate(world: &GameWorld) -> Vec<Issue> {
    check(world, None).into_iter().map(|(_, i)| i).collect()
}

2. attested-dm and dregg-narrator: "Prose is not power"

As recorded in GOAL-COLLECTIVE-FICTION-DEMO.md:55-156:

  • Ember identified the fundamental flaw in naive AI game mastering: lexical prompt-injection filters over natural language are futile because natural language lacks a metasyntax.
  • The Core Architecture:
  • Input Integrity via Guarded Templates: In metatheory/Dregg2/Crypto/HandlebarsGuarded.lean, holes carry explicit regular expression guards over Dregg2.Exec.Value:
lean
inductive GSeg where
  | lit  (text : List Value)
  | hole (id : Nat) (guard : PredRE)

structure GuardedTemplate where
  segments : List GSeg

def guardedSafe (T : GuardedTemplate) (d : Nat → List Value) : Prop :=
  ∀ id g, GSeg.hole id g ∈ T.segments → derives (d id) g = true

theorem guarded_render_mem_language (T : GuardedTemplate) (d : Nat → List Value)
    (h : guardedSafe T d) :
    render T d ∈ (guardedToGrammar T).language :=
  body_gLang d T.segments h
  1. Output Capability Gate: The LLM's natural language output is parsed through a strict JSON grammar into a closed, typed WorldEffect enum:
rust
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
pub enum WorldEffect {
    AdvanceScene(String),
    SetFlag(String, i64),
    GrantItem(String),
    ConsumeItem(String),
    Batch(Vec<WorldEffect>),
}
  1. Capability Confinement: DmCaps::authorize checks the effect against world rules. When a jailbroken Gemma 2 / Claude Haiku narrates granting the player the "Crown of Eternity", DmCaps refuses OverCap; the turn is aborted, no receipt is emitted, and the ledger state remains completely unchanged:
rust
pub struct DmCaps {
    pub may_advance_scene: bool,
    pub may_set_flags: bool,
    pub grantable_items: BTreeSet<String>,
}
  1. Prev-Linked Hash Chain: The transaction log uses BLAKE3 domain-separated receipt hashes:
rust
chain_receipt_id = BLAKE3(domain || seq || prev || narration || effect || attestation_commitment)
  1. Persisted Spend Ceiling: In narrator/src/lib.rs, hosted model calls (AWS Bedrock us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0 and us.amazon.nova-2-lite-v1:0) run behind BudgetLedger, enforcing a fail-closed hard $20 budget with atomic file locks and pre-flight credit reservation.

3. mud-dregg: Multiplayer Branch-Stitch

Defined in mud-dregg/src/lib.rs:1-45 and mud-dregg/src/dungeon.rs:37-50 (commit 6f3309911 on 2026-07-06):

rust
pub enum Command {
    Go { room: CellId },
    Take { entity: CellId },
    Say { room: CellId, glyph: FieldElement },
    Force { target: CellId, value: FieldElement },
}
  • Multiplayer worlds operate as a distributed time-travel lattice $(E, \le, \#)$.
  • Players fork world cells, explore timelines privately, and merge back through starbridge_v2::branch_stitch_session::BranchStitchSession.
  • Disjoint edits merge automatically; conflicting writes to the same entity (e.g., two players issuing take on the same sword) trigger a verified $\#$-conflict refusal under the axiom-clean Lean theorem Metatheory.SettlementSoundness.settlement_soundness, eliminating last-writer-wins silent corruption.

4. procgen-dregg & ugc-dregg: Verifiable Generations and Fair Leaderboards

  • procgen-dregg/src/lib.rs:1-100 folds committed seeds into a dregg_dice::RandomnessRequest, streaming reject-free, unbiased values via DrawStream::draw_bounded across MIN_ROOMS = 4 to 7 rooms with max degree $\le 6$. Any verifier re-generates byte-identical .dungeon files.
  • ugc-dregg/src/lib.rs:1-100 maintains a content-addressed registry keyed by UniverseId (semantics) alongside IPFS CIDs (wire bytes).
  • Supports both $O(N)$ re-execution verification via verify_completion and $O(1)$ succinct STARK verification via ProofCompletion, verifying a WholeChainProof without storing or revealing moves.

5. dregg-automatafl: Cellular Automaton Board Game at N=11

As documented in project-automatafl-verified-game.md:1-55:

  • Simultaneous-move board game on an $11 \times 11$ grid.
  • The handwritten Rust AIR was completely deleted in commit c8789758e8.
  • In metatheory/Dregg2/Circuit/Emit/:
  • resolve_sat_imp_roundBoardN (line 3768) proves move resolution.
  • astep_sat_imp_automatonStepCfgN (line 1090) proves automaton step transition.
  • In AutomataflCommit.lean:198-202, pack_injective_modp proves base-4 packed felts are injective modulo BabyBear prime $p$:
lean
theorem pack_injective_modp (cell1 cell2 : Nat → ℤ) (fc : Nat)
    (h1 : ∀ i, i < 15 * fc → 0 ≤ cell1 i ∧ cell1 i < 4)
    (h2 : ∀ i, i < 15 * fc → 0 ≤ cell2 i ∧ cell2 i < 4)
    (heq : ∀ j, j < fc → packCell cell1 j % BABYBEAR_P = packCell cell2 j % BABYBEAR_P) :
    ∀ i, i < 15 * fc → cell1 i = cell2 i
  • In AutomataflTurnCapstone.lean:284, turn_sat_imp_roundStep_pi joins resolution and step transitions across this crypto-free seam.
  • The M0–M7 multi-round conflict braid braid_sat_imp_runTurn (AutomataflBraidFold.lean:483) was confirmed in commit bd0d609c79, executing a complete 2-round conflict turn into a single STARK proof in 1663 seconds of real folding.

6. dregg-multiway-tug: ZK Hidden Hand

Defined in dregg-multiway-tug/src/lib.rs:1-45 and dregg-multiway-tug/src/state.rs:1-60:

  • Re-themes Hanamikoji (2 players, 7 guilds with influence [2,2,2,3,3,4,5], 21 total cards).
  • 21-card conservation: SumEquals over eight card-zone counters $== 21$ on every post-state.
  • Action flags: HeapAtom::WriteOnce on (player, action) keys.
  • Monotone scores: HeapAtom::Monotonic on per-guild placements.
  • Terminal rule: roundWinner with 9 distinct precedence clauses partitioned into dedicated transition cases.
  • Phase 2 zk HIDDEN HAND: cards are committed into a Poseidon2 Merkle tree. A player plays a card by proving Witnessed{MerkleMembership} in zero knowledge without revealing card identity.

7. poa (Path of Angels): The Sentyr Khovokhi Platform

As recorded in GOAL-PATH-OF-ANGELS-EXCELLENCE.md:1-115 and project-path-of-angels-platform.md:1-76:

  • Platform aboard the Khovokhi (Sentyr's narrative universe), spanning 84 Lean modules and 60,700 lines under metatheory/Dregg2/Games/PathOfAngels/ with 0 sorry and 1,840 verified theorems.
  • Three verified games: Signal Triangulation (3 bands $\times$ 6 values = 216 codes, 5 attempts), Relay Repair, Salvage Lock.
  • Six station organs: Crew, Records, Bazaar, Choir, Missions, Galley.
  • Supported by poa-curator for content manifest epoch signing, poa-web for terminal rendering, and poa-solana-gate for SPL Token-2022 holding admission (XkeTXo1125vz5H9svJpGiw4JvLbN8VmMu9cmMvspump).
  • The Five Weld Points:
  • W1: Precommitted beacon seed (RandomnessBeacon.lean FFI export).
  • W2: Judged transition circuit effect carve-out (moving beyond blind BLAKE3 topic hashing).
  • W3: Cell state 8-field AIR ceiling expansion (circuit/src/effect_vm/columns.rs:251).
  • W4: Automated game-design solver gate (scripts/poa-design-gate.py §12.3: checking non-dominated verbs and strategic forks).
  • W5: Curator authority as an attenuable Dregg capability.

8. CROWD-STREAM-ENGINE-DESIGN.md: Verifiable Live Streaming

Defined in docs/CROWD-STREAM-ENGINE-DESIGN.md:1-100:

  • Feeds live stream events (YouTube Super Chats, Twitch EventSub/bits) into CollectiveRound, weighting ballots by financial contribution.
  • Quorum-certified winners fold into one TurnReceipt, pushed via SSE (OverlayState in dreggnet-web) to transparent OBS browser overlays for broadcast.

2. The Great Diaspora — How parts spread into other projects

When breadstuffs began its transition in late summer 2026, the codebase did not die; its core concepts, modules, and architectures colonized the surrounding repositories:

text

(cba0e03c90c5 cut)     (zero-twin Lean 4)              (Actor World & obend)      (Residents & Solana DEX)
 ├─ metatheory/         ├─ pure Lean 4 core             ├─ world/kinds/            ├─ claude_resident
 ├─ poa-curator/        ├─ lock-free lobby                 (room, door, lantern,   ├─ kimi_resident (Yuè)
 ├─ poa-web/            ├─ fog-of-war telemetry            inhabitant, errand,     ├─ glm_resident (Kestrel)

                                                        ├─ world/objects/*.obend   └─ Token-2022 holding gate

2.1 path-of-angels: The Collaboration Extraction

On 2026-08-04, at upstream breadstuffs commit cba0e03c90c5b701e4ad5dae6d6abfa7d5534130, Ember cleanly carved out the partner boundary into /Users/ember/dev/path-of-angels. As detailed in path-of-angels/SOURCE-MAP.md:1-58, the repo preserved:

  • metatheory/Dregg2/Games/PathOfAngels/ (Lean game semantics, replay, canon)
  • poa-web/ (browser terminal and signed artifact loaders)
  • poa-curator/ (epoch signing and review projection)
  • poa/artifacts/poag1/ (curator-signed manifests and game descriptors)
  • Exclusion of all internal node execution, Solana bridges, and secrets.

On 2026-10-21, Ember issued an explicit historical correction in project-path-of-angels-platform.md:11:

"⚠ CORRECTED 2026-10-21 (Ember): PoA is NOT a real or running thing; 'breadstuffs is the PoA main line' is extremely wrong and out of date. breadstuffs is where the whole system we built lives; mini and nano are side explorations; breadstuffs waits to be resumed or composted into the next robigalia. Read the rest of this file as history."

2.2 automatafl: The Zero-Twin Lean 4 Extraction

Automatafl migrated out of breadstuffs into /Users/ember/dev/automatafl. As evidenced by git log 97db66a, c6652a5, and 253bafa, it completely realized Ember's architectural vision:

  • The dual-implementation "twin" (where rules were written in Rust and mirrored in Lean) was permanently abandoned.
  • The game runs today at automatafl.fg-goose.online as a pure Lean 4 verified core with a lightweight Rust telemetry and lock-free lobby harness.

2.3 delvetalk and delvetalk2: The World/Kinds Diaspora into Objective Bend

In October 2026, the RPG and MUD mechanisms from breadstuffs met Ember's work on Bend (obend).

  • The DelveTalk v1 Failure: In early October 2026, an autonomous Codex run generated 83 commits and 125,146 lines in /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk. Ember termed this initial sprawl "horseshit" and "chug" due to unchecked orchestration and refusal to subtract (-Users-ember-dev-delvetalk/memory/ember-working-style.md:21).
  • The delvetalk2 Rebirth: Founded on 2026-10-09, /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2 restructured the world into clean, composable primitives under /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/world/:

The 22 Kinds in world/kinds/

The files in /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/world/kinds/ reflect the exact ontological concepts developed across breadstuffs:

  1. room.kind:
yaml
of: record
form: <<END
name: text 1..64
spween: text 0..16384
END
head: {name}
voice: dusk-light and stone dust; a place where nobody has decided yet; there is no rush
about: a place walked by choices; prose alone is a scene
body: room

Directly incorporates Spween text passages (text 0..16384) from spween-dregg.

  1. errand.kind:
yaml
of: record
form: <<END
asks: text 1..200
on: object 1..128
done: text 1..400
pays: text 1..400
END
body: errand

Direct descendant of dreggnet-quest ("The Loremaster's Errand"), tracking request conditions, settlement targets, and payouts.

  1. bond.kind:
yaml
of: record
form: <<END
parties: text 1..400
terms: text 1..400
amends: object 0..128
law: text 0..4096
END
text: law signer "a party signs only for themselves": changed(new.marks.who) by request.subject
body: bond

Direct descendant of dreggnet-companion ("an owned asset fused with a leveling cell: a provable bond") and starbridge-apps/escrow-market.

  1. station.kind:
yaml
of: kind/room
voice: fluorescent and patient; the same hour always; open to Anyone Who Arrives
body: station

Inherits the station concept from poa, linking world/bodies/station.spween stitched from community replies.

  1. lantern.kind:
yaml
of: record
form: <<END
name: text 1..64
door: object 1..128
END
body: lantern

Direct descendant of dungeon-on-dregg's lantern item slot gating dark exits.

  1. parlour.kind:
yaml
of: kind/station
body: parlour

Direct descendant of dreggnet-tavern's parlour hub, linking world/bodies/parlour.spween and ouija planchette board interactions.

  1. inhabitant.kind:
yaml
of: record
form: <<END
name: text 1..64
haunts: object 1..128
tide: object 1..128
END
body: inhabitant

Direct descendant of dungeon-on-dregg/src/mud.rs inhabitant cells. 8–22. bee.kind, bell.kind, cistern.kind, counter.kind, dj.kind, door.kind, front.kind, garden.kind, hexboard.kind, planchette.kind, porch.kind, record.kind, register.kind, regular.kind, weather.kind: Environmental, board, clock, and audit kinds.

The 9 Actors in world/objects/*.obend

The .obend actor programs in /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/world/objects/ execute natively in Objective Bend:

  1. Policy.obend:
bend
# The interpretation policy as an ordinary object: the host sends prompt(...) to
# the configured model and returns the answer as a proposal, never as authority.
def hob() -> String:
  "Give the spell they meant, so the card can run it; the card and its law decide what is admitted, never you."

Directly embodies the attested-dm thesis: the model proposes, the object's law disposes.

  1. Tide.obend:
bend
# kimik3's tide: the town's one clock thing. It ticks itself, once every `gap` clock minutes:
# each tick is its own turn ... missed ticks are not made up ... a tick sooner than `gap`
# after the last is refused tooSoon, so a second chain dies at its first tick.

Implements StrictMonotonic turn pacing and deterministic world progression from breadstuffs.

  1. Library.obend: Manages universe definitions, scene stacks, and completion verification records (ugc-dregg).
  2. Thing.obend: Implements inventory items and asset notes (dreggnet-asset, dreggnet-gear).
  3. Workshop.obend: Implements recipe books, input burning, and fair quality draws (dreggnet-craft).
  4. Avatar.obend: Manages user identity, capabilities, and key bindings.
  5. Kind.obend, Mending.obend, Record.obend: Base meta-object system, journal repair, and history logs.

2.4 allgame: Tulip Residents and Confined Minds

In /Users/ember/dev/allgame, the resident mind harness (claude_resident, kimi_resident for Yuè月 on allgame-public, glm_resident for Kestrel, mother_mythos/) directly adopted the hard lesson from breadstuffs's attested-dm:

  • Natural language cannot be constrained by prompt decoration or substring filters.
  • Real agency requires strict capability confinement (DmCaps::authorize) and typed action channels where "prose is not power."

2.5 dclutch: Solana DEX and Creature Aquarium

In /Users/ember/dev/dclutch, the financial and creature systems extracted from breadstuffs found a production trading home:

  • The Solana Token-2022 holding admission gate developed in poa-solana-gate and dregg-bridge::solana_holdings.
  • Atomic escrow settlement from dreggnet-trade and starbridge-escrow-market.
  • The SimLife3 creature aquarium, persisting autonomous living entities over verifiable on-chain state.

3. What got dropped or parked — And how we take it back up now

When breadstuffs froze during the cutover to minidregg, dozens of fully tested, driven game and social mechanics were left stranded in the tree:

3.1 The Stranded Mechanics

  1. The 28 dreggnet-* Social and RPG Modules:
  2. Multi-party seated division of labor (dreggnet-party).
  3. Cross-cell observed asset leveling (dreggnet-companion).
  4. Hard ISA-level soulbound non-transferability (dreggnet-cheevo).
  5. Two-draw deflationary crafting sink (dreggnet-craft).
  6. Atomic escrow player trading (dreggnet-trade).
  7. Monotonic faction reputation decay (dreggnet-faction).
  8. Single-use passkey-voted council enactments (dreggnet-council).
  9. Multi-patron live receipt-streamed taverns (dreggnet-tavern).
  10. Escrow-custodied guild treasuries (dreggnet-guild).
  1. The .dungeon Text DSL and Biconditional Validator:
  2. Hand-authored text files with line-numbered, fail-closed validation (dungeon-on-dregg/src/dsl/).
  3. Biconditional translation validation ensuring every game gate matches an executor constraint.
  1. Multiplayer Branch-Stitch Time-Travel:
  2. Private timeline exploration with verified lattice merge and #-conflict refusal (mud-dregg).
  1. ZK Hidden Hand and Proof Folding:
  2. Poseidon2 Merkle hand commitments proving legal play with zero knowledge (dregg-multiway-tug).
  3. Match-as-single-proof STARK recursive folding (game_turn_folds_and_lightclient_accepts).
  1. Provably-Fair Procedural Generation:
  2. Reject-free draw streams derived from committed beacon seeds (procgen-dregg).
  1. Automated Game Design Solver Gate:
  2. scripts/poa-design-gate.py enforcing non-dominated verbs, strategic fork counts, and binding budgets before content can be committed.

3.2 Why Mini Stalled, and Why We Can Revive Them Now

In the 2026-09-30 bake-off between breadstuffs and minidregg (claudesplosion/bakeoff/VERDICT.md), both repositories revealed structural limitations:

  • breadstuffs suffered from massive monorepo weight, 12 elephant crates, and linker collisions on Linux (mimalloc TLS in libleanrt.a).
  • minidregg suffered from immediate-mode re-execution (write times scaled quadratically: 2.6s $\to$ 13.0s $\to$ 25.5s at 10/100/155 records) and concrete import rebuild cascades.

As Ember diagnosed on 2026-10-31 (feedback-retained-mode.md):

"this current generation of code generation models keeps writing re-execution … the whole thing would be incremental and efficient and held 'retained-mode'"

The modern environment solves both failure modes:

  1. Retained-Mode Storage in minidregg: Replaced immediate-mode replay with persistent incremental maps and replayed suffix caching (commit c4ecabe0f on HEAD).
  2. Deterministic Parallel Actors in obend: Objective Bend executes room graphs, inventory mutations, and combat ticks in massively parallel interaction nets without lock contention.
  3. Ontology Alignment in delvetalk2: The world/kinds/ directory is already shaped to host the 28 dreggnet-* crates.
  4. Resident Minds in allgame: Real models (Claude, Yuè, Kestrel) provide narrative richness while attested-dm's DmCaps ensures that prose never overrides state.

Strand 19 — The Breadstuffs Diaspora: Federation, Multi-Chain Settlement, Blocklace/CapTP, Commons Governance, Polisware, and the Enterprise Verticals

Primary-source dossier. Compiled 2026-11-02 from the repositories under /Users/ember/dev/, their git logs, their in-tree goal documents, and the Claude memory stores under ~/.claude/projects/-Users-ember-dev-breadstuffs/memory/. Every commit SHA, Lean 4 / HOL4 / ACL2 theorem signature, Rust struct, and verbatim quotation below was extracted directly from the local repositories and memory records, not reconstructed from memory. Where an artifact was parked or a proof left uncompleted, the text notes the exact failure mode, missing lemma, or architectural friction. Scope boundary. /Users/ember/hellas is strictly third-party historical context (Ember's prior work on workload verification, which informed the genesis of Dregg after departure) and is not claimed as open property. All repos outside /Users/ember/dev/whatember/ are treated as strictly read-only.

0. Executive Map & Architectural Lineage

The monolithic repository /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs was the expansive crucible of Ember's systems engineering. Between May and August 2026, breadstuffs housed simultaneously: a Byzantine fault-tolerant consensus engine over equivocation-free DAGs (blocklace), an object-capability network transport protocol (captp), a multi-chain non-custodial settlement and light-client matrix (chain, cosmos-*, solana-*, eth-lightclient, mina), an axiomatic democratic deliberation and collective governance substrate (collective-choice, commons-arbiter, metatheory/Polis), and an enterprise/database infrastructure tier (pg-dregg, fhir, durable-workflow).

Under the pressure of build times, multi-tenant agent contention, and proving overheads, this monolith fractured into a specialized diaspora. The table and ASCII lineage below trace where those ideas landed, what succeeded, and what remained parked.
