1. What Lived in breadstuffs (The Monolithic Peak)

1.1. Federation, Consensus, and the Blocklace

The federation layer was designed to execute multi-node consensus with verified finality. Its primary documents were GOAL-FEDERATION.md and memory file project-federation-payoff.md.

The n=4 Federation Payoff (2026-07-07)

On 2026-07-07 (cv session 5d12f365-a748-4e7a-b4ca-99a0291fef49), Ember achieved the core federation milestone: a foreign, non-operator client signed its own transfer, submitted via POST /turns/submit, and it stream-finalized cross-node on a verified-executor $n=4$ mesh across two physical machines (hbox at 192.168.50.39 and nextop at 192.168.50.130). The test harness node/tests/payoff_client_turn.rs verified:

  • Dest account funded across all 4 nodes;
  • Block heights advanced uniformly: [1,1,1,1] → [2,2,2,2];
  • Finalized in ~25 seconds over two clean rounds under DREGG_TEST_REQUIRE_FINALITY=1.

The $O(\text{history})$ Wall in Verified Finality

The path to this payoff uncovered a severe architectural defect. When running with the verified Lean tau-order finality gate (VerifiedFinality::compute_order, node/src/finality_gate.rs:150 calling dregg_tau_order FFI into Lean's tauOrderFast), an $n=3$ node mesh would plateau while $n=4$ progressed. The investigation proved this was not a correctness fault, but a catastrophic complexity cliff:

*"The verified-Lean tau-order finality gate was $O(\text{history})$, recomputed from scratch on EVERY finality poll: the serial executor rebuilt the whole wire and Lean rebuilt PastCache/RoundCache over the full lace on each call, then discarded them. As the DAG grew, per-poll cost outpaced block production and the consensus stalled."* — project-federation-payoff.md

The Blocklace: Cordial Dissemination and Eviction-by-Past

The consensus engine in /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/blocklace/ implemented a Byzantine-repelling Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) based on Almeida & Shapiro (2024, arXiv:2402.08068) and Cordial Miners (Shapiro et al., 2022, arXiv:2205.09174). In cv session ad7d9679 (sub-agent of 27b28e60, 2026-08-08, recorded in docs/reference/READING-BLOCKLACE-2026-08-08.md), Ember dispatched a literature review that uncovered a fundamental divergence between theory and implementation:

  1. The Live-Gossip Mutation Defect: The implementation in blocklace_sync.rs stated that the tau participant set must be a deterministic function of committed state alone. Yet handle_push called constitution.auto_evict(proof) on gossip arrival, mutating the live participant set. If Node A saw both halves of an equivocation fork and Node B saw only one, Node A ran at $n-1$ while Node B ran at $n$. Consequently, the wave leader formula wave_leader = participants[wave % n] selected different leaders on different nodes, creating a silent fork. Furthermore, because eviction was not a block in the DAG, a reboot silently reinstated the equivocator.
  1. The Fix (Commits 89238335a and 0eccd772d, 2026-08-08/09): Ember corrected this by moving eviction off gossip-arrival mutation into exclusion-by-past (node(b) ∉ byz(⌊b⌋)), making eviction a structural predicate over a block's causal closure:
rust
   // Commit 89238335ad618998cc74daa77328651add0ddcb8
   // Author: ember arlynx <ember@lunar.town>, Date: Sat Aug 8 17:37:36 2026 -0400
   // "feat(consensus)!: exclusion-by-past — eviction was a live mutation keyed on gossip arrival;
   // both papers make it a predicate over the block's own closure"

Followed by commit 0eccd772d1348fb29b79658cf134f3fc18fcfe6d (Sun Aug 9 05:26:58 2026), adding 2,115 insertions across blocklace/src/constitution.rs and blocklace/src/finality.rs to enforce the Cordial Miners Alg. 1:5 two-tips-per-miner flood cap and CreatorTips::Pair evidence requirements.

  1. DFA-Federation: To prevent circular dependencies (cell -> dfa -> federation -> captp -> cell), /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/dfa-federation/ isolated route updates. FederationQcVerifier verified route transitions using a BLS ThresholdQC over old_commitment || new_commitment:
rust
   pub struct FederationQcVerifier {
       committee: FederationCommittee,
   }

   impl ThresholdVerifier for FederationQcVerifier {
       fn verify_threshold(&self, expected_old: [u8; 32], new_comm: [u8; 32], proof_data: &[u8]) -> Result<(), RouteUpdateError> {
           let qc: ThresholdQC = postcard::from_bytes(proof_data)
               .map_err(|e| RouteUpdateError::ThresholdVerificationFailed(e.to_string()))?;
           let mut msg = [0u8; 64];
           msg[0..32].copy_from_slice(&expected_old);
           msg[32..64].copy_from_slice(&new_comm);
           self.committee.verify(&qc, &msg)
               .map_err(|e| RouteUpdateError::ThresholdVerificationFailed(e.to_string()))
       }
   }

1.2. Distributed Object Capabilities: captp

The CapTP implementation in /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/captp/ provided capability transport across federations, combining the classic Agoric/E CapTP architecture with post-quantum cryptography.

Architecture and Invariants

Defined in captp/src/lib.rs:

  • Sturdy References (uri::DreggUri): Portable, durable capability URIs of the form dregg://<fed-id>/<cell-id>/<swiss-number> that survive transport disconnections.
rust
  #[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
  pub struct DreggUri {
      pub federation_id: [u8; 32],
      pub cell_id: CellId,
      pub swiss_number: [u8; 32],
  }
  • Swiss Table (sturdy::SwissTable): Maps unguessable bearer secrets ([u8; 32]) to live capability descriptors. Possession of the swiss number is itself the authorization.
  • Three-Party Handoffs (handoff::HandoffCertificate): Unforgeable offline capability transfers signed by the introducer, verifiable by any third party without contacting the issuer:
rust
  pub struct HandoffCertificate {
      pub introducer_pubkey: [u8; 32],
      pub recipient_pubkey: [u8; 32],
      pub sturdy_ref: DreggUri,
      pub caveat: Pred,
      pub signature: Signature, // Ed25519 or ML-DSA-65
  }
  • Distributed GC (gc): Cross-federation wire reference counting and drop tracking.
  • Hybrid Post-Quantum Cryptography (dregg-pq): ML-DSA-65 (FIPS 204) hybrid capability-handoff signatures and X25519 + ML-KEM-768 hybrid transport sessions to resist "Harvest Now, Decrypt Later" (HNDL) attacks.
  • Store-and-Forward Relay (store_forward::EncryptedMessage): Offline delivery using X25519 ECDH $\to$ HKDF-SHA256 $\to$ ChaCha20-Poly1305 AEAD. Relay operators inspect only ciphertexts; tampering or forging triggers authenticated decryption failure.

The PQ Harness Pitfall

A critical test-suite bug plagued captp. In commits d2181916c and d78b8512b, Ember discovered that 130 tests across captp and related crates had never executed because dregg-pq aborted the process with process::abort() when the Lean-verified ML-DSA-65 cores were missing in test binaries, causing tests to exit prematurely with bare SIGABRTs.

1.3. Multi-Chain Settlement and Semantic Light Clients

The multi-chain vision in breadstuffs was defined by Ember's strict doctrine:

"You only need wraps/bridges when you lack proofs about the other chain's state transitions — just be a full client of all the chains." — project-chain-participation-epoch.md

The 25-Lane Proof Statement

Rather than trusting centralized bridge multisigs, Dregg’s settlement object was an IVC STARK fold compressed into a gnark Groth16 proof over BN254. The canonical statement was defined as 25 BabyBear field lanes ($p = 2013265921 = 0x78000001$):

text
Statement = genesis_root[8] ++ final_root[8] ++ num_turns[1] ++ chain_digest[8]

With SEG_ANCHOR_WIDTH = SEG_DIGEST_WIDTH = 8 (circuit-prove/src/ivc_turn_chain.rs:284).

Cross-Chain Settlement Parity

The 25-lane BN254 Groth16 proof was verified natively across three disparate runtime architectures:

  1. EVM (chain/contracts/DreggSettlement.sol): Concrete 25-lane contract using Ethereum's ecPairing precompile, paired with DreggGroth16Verifier25.sol, DreggVault.sol, and DreggCredentialGate.sol (commits 53efc5503, b997f9cef, ee9f9d887, 35af20966).
solidity
   contract DreggSettlement is IDreggSettlement {
       uint256 public constant BABYBEAR_P = 2013265921;
       IGroth16Verifier25 public immutable verifier;

       uint32[8] private _genesisLanes;
       uint32[8] private _provenLanes;
       uint64 private _provenHeight;

       function settle(
           uint32[8] calldata newRootLanes,
           uint64 numTurns,
           uint32[8] calldata chainDigestLanes,
           uint256[8] calldata proof
       ) external override {
           uint256[25] memory publicInputs;
           // pack _provenLanes, newRootLanes, numTurns, chainDigestLanes...
           require(verifier.verifyProof(proof, publicInputs), "invalid settlement proof");
           _provenLanes = newRootLanes;
           _provenHeight += numTurns;
           _provenRoots[keccak256(abi.encodePacked(newRootLanes))] = _provenHeight + 1;
       }
   }

The VK Commitment Flaw Fixed (Flag Day 2026-07-30): The original contract stored bytes32 private immutable _verifyingKeyHash which was never compared against the key used in ecPairing. It was corrected so that verifyingKeyHash() reads directly from verifier.vkDigest(), making mismatch structurally impossible.

  1. Cosmos (cosmos-settlement/src/lib.rs): A CosmWasm contract verifying the identical 25-lane proof and Pedersen commitment using ark-bn254, without requiring a Cosmos-native curve instantiation.
  1. Solana (solana-settlement/src/lib.rs): A native Solana SBF program (no Anchor) verifying the identical proof on-chain using Solana's alt_bn128 syscalls (solana_bn254::prelude).

Semantic Light Clients

  • EVM (eth-lightclient): Verified Ethereum sync-committee attestations, weak-subjectivity checkpoints, and EIP-1186 Merkle Patricia Trie (MPT) storage proofs. In commit 0aba72c65788 (2026-07-29), the sync committee rotation was transferred to Lean verification after finding that store::bootstrap_committee had bypassed the verified gate. Commit dba55c04b removed an accidental DREGG_LANE_AA_MUTANT=accept bypass.
  • Cosmos (cosmos-lightclient): Verified Tendermint $\ge 2/3$ commit signatures and ICS-23 IAVL Merkle tree membership.
  • Solana (dregg-bridge/src/solana_holdings.rs): Verified Tower-BFT finalized bank hashes and stake-weighted supermajorities.
  • Mina (GOAL-MINA-SEMANTIC-LIGHTCLIENTS.md): Reached acceptance of real Mina Devnet block 539508 via a Lean Kimchi/Pickles verifier (P0–P6), but the Pasta-vs-BabyBear curve-cycle mismatch prevented full arithmetic deployment.

Non-Custodial Cross-Chain Governance: dregg-interchain-gov

In /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/dregg-interchain-gov/src/lib.rs, Ember joined all three foreign light clients into a single compiled governance seam. Foreign token holders proved their balances on their native chain via read proofs:

  • Solana: ProvenHolding
  • EVM: ProvenErc20Holding
  • Cosmos: ProvenCosmosFact

The holder signed an owner -> voter binding (Ed25519 on Solana, EIP-191 on EVM, Cosmos ADR-036). The compile-time pins _EVM_TAG_PINNED and _COSMOS_TAG_PINNED locked the tag mapping (Solana = 0, EVM = 1, Cosmos = 2), allowing foreign balances to flow directly into Dregg's [CollectiveChoice] tally without locking, bridging, or wrapping a single token:

rust
pub fn proven_holding_to_governance(
    proven: &ProvenCosmosFact,
    chain_id: ChainId,
    nullifier_salt: [u8; 32],
) -> Result<ProvenForeignHolding, ForeignFieldsError> {
    let fields = cosmos_lightclient::foreign_holding_fields(proven, nullifier_salt)?;
    ProvenForeignHolding::from_foreign_fields(chain_id, fields)
}

1.4. Democratic Deliberation, Commons Governance, and Polisware

breadstuffs contained one of the most mathematically ambitious governance stacks ever designed, spanning game theory, Ostrom commons management, and formal social choice.

Collective Choice (collective-choice/src/lib.rs)

Composed four formal mechanisms into a unified VoteEngine:

  1. One-Vote-per-Ballot: Ballot cell with a WriteOnce(VOTE) constraint.
  2. Monotone Tally: Poll cells with Monotonic option tallies, preventing replay attacks from lowering counts.
  3. Quorum Gate: In-cell affine inequality constraint: $$\text{AffineLe} \{ M \cdot \text{RESOLVED} - \sum \text{TALLY}_i \le 0 \}$$
  4. Non-Amplifying Delegation (Liquid Democracy): Macaroon attenuation via Mandate::sub_delegate (DelegTree::no_amplify).
  5. One-Vote Nullifiers: Third layer of defense preventing double voting across the network.

The Commons Arbiter (commons-arbiter/src/lib.rs)

An "AI authority nobody owns" (docs/deos/UNCENSORABLE-UTILITY.md). An autonomous LLM evaluator governed by a community Rubric:

  • A member submits a Case (content + rubric ID).
  • An attested LLM session emits a ruling carrying a ZkOracleAttestation.
  • Anti-Prompt-Injection Proof: The attestation proves the ruling is authentic, well-formed JSON, and injection-free (proved free of {{ handlebars delimiters via a verified negative-regex matcher).
  • Quorum Ratification: A ruling becomes final only when a committee of $n$ independent operators ratifies it ($\lfloor 2n/3 \rfloor + 1$).

Grain Commons (grain-commons/src/lib.rs)

A decentralized app-store and execution commons for hosted AI agents (Sandstorm grains):

  • Signed .spk packages where the App ID is the author's Ed25519 public key.
  • Committed Umem memory heaps with tamper-proof Pedigree Merkle trees tracing every code fork.
  • GenesisAgent minting bounded child sub-agents with attenuated capability ceilings.

Polisware: The 66-File Lean Corpus (metatheory/Polis/)

Polisware was the formal mathematical constitution for multi-agent coexistence, synthesizing Ember's work on svenvs with computable politics. The spine is defined in metatheory/Polis/Polis.lean:

lean
/-- **`polis_safety` — verify the cage, not the animal.** For a sound policy, a safe
shield, and a safe start, the enveloped system keeps the floor at EVERY step, for EVERY
controller. The controller is universally quantified and never inspected: no psychometric
classification of the inhabitant can be load-bearing for the guarantee. -/
theorem polis_safety
    {step : State → Action → State} {safe : Floor State}
    {pol : Policy State Action} {shield : State → Action} {init : State}
    (sound : SoundPolicy step safe pol)
    (shieldSafe : ∀ s, safe s → safe (step s (shield s)))
    (initSafe : safe init) :
    ∀ (ctrl : State → Action) (n : Nat),
      safe (traj step (envAct pol shield ctrl) init n) := by
  intro ctrl
  have step1 : ∀ s, safe s → safe (step s (envAct pol shield ctrl s)) := by
    intro s hs
    unfold envAct
    by_cases hp : pol s (ctrl s)
    · rw [if_pos hp]; exact sound s (ctrl s) hs hp
    · rw [if_neg hp]; exact shieldSafe s hs
  intro n
  induction n with
  | zero => exact initSafe
  | succ k ih => exact step1 _ ih

Key constitutional theorems in Polis.lean:

  • Honesty Discrimination via Typing (lines 213–234):
lean
  inductive ClauseEvidence (c : Clause) : Type where
    | structuralProof (proof : c.claim) : ClauseEvidence c
    | adjudicationCost (clerkCost : Nat) : ClauseEvidence c
    | jurisdictionBoundary (reason : String) : ClauseEvidence c

  theorem structural_requires_proof {c : Clause} (e : ClauseEvidence c)
      (h : e.status = LawStatus.structural) : c.claim := by
    cases e with
    | structuralProof p => exact p
    | adjudicationCost _ => simp only [ClauseEvidence.status] at h; exact absurd h (by decide)
    | jurisdictionBoundary _ => simp only [ClauseEvidence.status] at h; exact absurd h (by decide)
  • Monotone Amendment and Non-Regression (lines 279–314):
lean
  theorem regressive_amendment_inert (frozenMin : Agent → Floor State)
      (am : Amendment Agent State) (C : Constitution Agent State)
      (h : ¬ NonRegressive frozenMin am C) :
      guardedApply frozenMin am C = C := by
    unfold guardedApply; rw [if_neg h]

  theorem amendment_stream_nonregression (frozenMin : Agent → Floor State)
      (ams : Nat → Amendment Agent State) {C : Constitution Agent State}
      (hwf : WellFormed frozenMin C) :
      ∀ n, WellFormed frozenMin (amendStream frozenMin ams C n) := by
    intro n
    induction n with
    | zero => exact hwf
    | succ k ih => exact guardedApply_wellFormed frozenMin (ams k) ih
  • Schism as Empty Meet (line 335):
lean
  theorem disjoint_floors_no_polis (floors : Agent → Floor State)
      (hempty : ¬ ∃ s, ∀ i, floors i s) : ¬ CanFormPolis floors := by
    rintro ⟨s, hs⟩
    exact hempty ⟨s, hs⟩

A polis exists only where exported floors share an inhabitant. If floors are disjoint, there is no common court; this is not enforced by censorship or majority vote, but is a structural derivation.

1.5. Enterprise, Storage, and Durable Database Verticals

pg-dregg: Verifiable Postgres Extension

In /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/pg-dregg/, Ember built a PostgreSQL extension (targeting PostgreSQL 18 via cargo-pgrx) that made Dregg's object-capability model the native Row-Level Security (RLS) engine for relational data:

  • src/authz.rs: Tier-A capability decoding, verification LRU, and caveat algebra.
  • src/jsonpath.rs: Compiled Dregg Pred predicates directly into SQL jsonpath filter expressions:
rust
  // Structural translation table from pg-dregg/src/jsonpath.rs
  // Pred::True                   => "true"
  // Pred::False                  => "false"
  // Pred::AttrEq{key, value}     => "@.key == \"value\""
  // Pred::AttrPrefix{key, pfx}   => "@.key starts with \"pfx\""
  // Pred::NotBefore{at}          => "@.clock >= at"
  // Pred::NotAfter{at}           => "@.clock <= at"
  // Pred::Within{nb, na}         => "@.clock >= nb && @.clock <= na"
  // Pred::AllOf([p...])          => "(f1 && f2 && ...)"
  // Pred::AnyOf([p...])          => "(f1 || f2 || ...)"
  // Pred::Not(p)                 => "!(f)"
  • src/mirror.rs: Tier-B/C row types with a RootChain anti-substitution mechanism.
  • src/attest.rs: Tier-C range-attestation proof gate linking SQL queries to zk-circuit receipts.
  • src/workflow.rs: In-database durable workflow execution.

fhir (fhIR-0): FHE Convex Optimization DSL

Despite its name, /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs/fhir/ was not medical HL7 FHIR; it was fhIR-0: Fully Homomorphic Encryption Intermediate Representation:

  • Typed DSL for financial orders and products on top of the fhEgg / Cert-F solver.
  • Products carried a convex program shape (LP / QP / aggregation) and a privacy tier: Dark (Tier 0, FHE, public matrices only), Shielded (Tier 1, solver sees private matrices), and Open (Tier 2, public).
  • types.rs defined the visibility and admissibility rule:
rust
  #[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Serialize)]
  pub enum Visibility {
      Public,
      Private,
  }

  pub struct ProgramType {
      pub curvature: Curvature,
      pub matrices: Vec<Visibility>,
      pub cones: Vec<ConeKind>,
      pub dimension: usize,
  }
  • qp_certificate.rs: Generated exact SDD $\to$ PSD admission witnesses and strict KKT optimality witnesses.

durable-workflow, hosted-durable, and hosted-lease

A deterministic durable execution engine modeled after DBOS and Temporal:

  • durable-workflow/src/lib.rs: Workflows execute deterministic steps checkpointed to a duroxide store. Crashes resume at the first unfinished step without re-executing completed side effects.
  • hosted-durable/src/lib.rs: Conserving settlement rail charging per-period lease fees via recTransfer ($\sum \delta = 0$).
  • hosted-lease/src/lib.rs: Fused meters combining StandingObligation with prepaid_lease into a single atomic write.

auditable-fund and attested-dm

  • auditable-fund/src/lib.rs: An autonomous trading agent that cannot lie about fills. Every trade lands as a light-client verifiable receipt binding a zkOracle price attestation and an attested Hermes decision. A third party auditing the fund requires zero operator trust.
  • attested-dm/src/lib.rs: An attested AI Dungeon Master. Prompt integrity is certified via dregg_zkoracle_prove::render. Player input is strictly slot-confined and checked by a verified negative parser to ensure it cannot escape its data cell into system instructions.

dregg-storage-templates and dregg-stream-ingest

  • dregg-storage-templates/src/cap_inbox.rs: Standard cell templates for CapInbox (deposit accounting + ring root), ProgrammableQueue, PubSubTopic, and BlindedQueue (custom ZK verifier).
  • dregg-stream-ingest/src/lib.rs: Normalized live chat events (YouTube Super Chats, Twitch cheers, TikTok gifts) into paid, weighted ballots for collective voting.

2. The Diaspora: How Ideas and Subsystems Spread Out

When breadstuffs was archived, its core components migrated to specialized repositories across /Users/ember/dev/.

text
breadstuffs Subsystem                 Descendant Repository                   Key Transformation
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Solana Bridge & Settlement    ───▶   dragons-clutch ──▶ dclutch             Decomposed monolith into 8 SBF programs,
                                                                             2,646 Lean theorems, Cohort 17 on devnet.
Store-and-Forward CapTP       ───▶   fn                                      Transformed into formal RFC 9171–9174
                                                                             BPv7 bundle protocol with ACL2 proofs.
Claim & Receipt Ledgers       ───▶   verekos ──▶ minidregg                   Paused EKOS half (2026-10-19); carried
                                                                             claim/receipt model into minidregg.
Polisware Non-Domination      ───▶   svenvs                                  Formulated the svenvs key insight:
                                                                             "govern trace-shape, never motive."
Blocklace / Cordial Miners    ───▶   nanodregg ──▶ obend                     Discarded DAG complexity for Simplex BFT
                                                                             (`Obend.Consensus.Simplex`).

2.1. Solana Settlement: dragons-clutch $\to$ dclutch

The multi-chain Solana work in breadstuffs (solana-lock, solana-settlement, dregg-bridge) moved first into dragons-clutch (gen-1, a 2.15 MB monolith ELF) and was subsequently rewritten into dclutch (gen-2). As documented in Strand 12:

  • Replaced the monolithic WAVE runtime with 8 discrete SBF programs;
  • Proved 2,646 Lean theorems establishing balance conservation and clearing fairness;
  • Deployed Cohorts 8 through 17 on Solana devnet (clutch.dregg.pro), culminating in the first retired market on any chain on 2026-09-06 (AvKSizb7...).

2.2. Asynchronous Federation: fn (BPv7 & NNTP)

The store-and-forward capability relay from captp/src/store_forward.rs and the decentralized news/federation mechanics evolved into /Users/ember/dev/fn/ ("formal news"):

  • Built as an NNTP news server where ACL2 makes every decision;
  • Implemented the full Delay-Tolerant Networking (DTN) Bundle Protocol v7 (BPv7) per RFC 9171, RFC 9172 (BPSec), RFC 9173, and RFC 9174;
  • Namespaces in ACL2:
  • fn-bpp-: Primary bundle blocks (CRC-16/CRC-32C, dtn and ipn endpoint IDs, fragment fields);
  • fn-bpb-: Canonical bundle frame codec;
  • fn-bpn-: Autonomous BPv7 forwarding node and RFC 6.1.1 status-report engine;
  • fn-bpf- & fn-bpfr-: Resumable bundle fragmentation and reassembly over overlapping slices;
  • Keystones certified in ACL2: fn-bpp-decode-of-encode, fn-bpc-dec-yields-shape, and fn-bpp-accepted-input-is-canonical-by-construction.

2.3. Claim/Receipt Ledgers: verekos $\to$ minidregg

The verifiable audit and receipt tracking pioneered in auditable-fund and pg-dregg was systematized in /Users/ember/dev/verekos:

  • Built 190 Lean theorems modeling claim-and-receipt ledgers over an enforced, Mathlib-free WorldCore boundary.
  • Pinned minidregg theorems: pinHolds, transclusion_pinned, toCausalPreimage_injective, transclude_uncovered_refused.
  • On 2026-10-19 (commit 89238335a / 8972945), Ember paused the EKOS half (project/CLOSEOUT-2026-10-19.md) and merged the claim/receipt architecture directly into minidregg.

2.4. Polisware and the svenvs Realization

The mathematical link between metatheory/Polis and /Users/ember/dev/svenvs was captured in project-polis-svenvs-key-insight.md:

*"svenvs resolves the protected-remainder paradox FOR ENFORCEMENT, not for epistemology. The move: the inhabitant is the $\forall$-quantified term the proof never reasons about, so the shadow is real-but-INERT. And the floor is NEGATIVE and least-restrictive.* *The consequence that makes polisware possible: the same $\forall$-blindness that protects the remainder makes the politician UNGOVERNABLE-BY-INTERIOR. Therefore govern TRACE-SHAPE, not motive. That is not a compromise — it is the only coherent option, and it is exactly why polis_safety quantifies over an opaque controller that is never inspected. 'Verify the cage, not the animal.'* *dregg's two floors ARE svenvs's two theorems. The authority floor corresponds to safety_preservation; the human floor corresponds to corrigibility_floor_holds."*

In HOL4 (svenvs/liberty/libertyScript.sml), Ember proved that the cage is not gratuitously restrictive:

  • Theorem maxpol_sound: The maximal policy permitting actions that preserve safety is sound.
  • Theorem maxpol_is_greatest_sound: Every sound policy is a subset of maxpol.
  • Theorem envelope_is_least_restrictive: Any policy permitting an action forbidden by maxpol is provably unsound for some inhabitant.
  • Theorem envelope_never_overrides_safe_action: Safe actions pass through untouched.

2.5. Consensus Transformation: nanodregg $\to$ obend

The complexity of the blocklace DAG and the O(history) finality bottlenecks forced a dramatic simplification:

  1. nanodregg stripped consensus down to its essential state machine, implementing Simplex BFT.
  2. In /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Consensus/Simplex.lean, Ember unified this consensus lineage:
  3. Absorbed minidregg/Kernel/GenericSimplex.lean, GenericSimplexQuorum.lean, and GenericSimplexCertificateSafety.lean;
  4. Eliminated DAG traversal in favor of view-based notarization certificates (NotarizationCert, QuorumCert):
lean
     structure Proposal where
       view : View
       height : Nat
       parent : Block
       turn : Option Obend.Vault.VaultTurn
       deriving BEq

     structure Message where
       sender : NodeId
       view : View
       kind : Kind
       value : Argument
       deriving BEq
  • Bound block payloads directly to Option Obend.Vault.VaultTurn, proving constructive safety and liveness without Mathlib.

3. What Got Dropped, Parked, or Underelaborated

An archaeological excavation reveals why several brilliant systems in breadstuffs were left behind:

text
Dropped / Parked Component        Root Cause of Abandonment / Park
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
pg-dregg                          pgrx cdylib clashed with workspace cargo check; required external PostgreSQL 18
                                  daemon; Lean-FFI bridge was fragile and unmaintained.
fhIR-0 (FHE Order DSL)            Admissibility theorem ("admissible iff it compiles") unproved; FHE public-matrix
                                  envelope too restrictive for general order-book clearing.
Polisware & Commons Arbiter       Real LLM agent runtime harness never deployed; prompt-injection defense relied on
                                  lexical `{{` parsing rather than semantic non-domination.
Auditable Fund / Attested DM      Strictly paper-trading; lacked live broker API integration; prompt integrity was
                                  proven at template-expansion time but output behavior remained unverified.
Blocklace + CapTP                 O(history) finality gate bottleneck; live-gossip eviction bugs; ML-DSA test harness
                                  SIGABRT crashes; multi-round DAG sync complexity.
Cosmos / EVM / Solana Settle      gnark BN254 Groth16 setups relied on single-party dev keys (toxic waste);
                                  lacked on-chain proof-of-holdings derivation to parameterize voting weight.
Mina / Kimchi Light Client        Curve-cycle mismatch (Pasta / Tweedle vs BabyBear); IPA polynomial commitment verifier
                                  exceeded flat gate bounds in production.

Detailed Forensics

  1. pg-dregg: The dream of a verifiable Postgres extension with native capability RLS was functionally sound, but operationally marooned. Because cargo-pgrx manages its own PostgreSQL toolchain, it had to be excluded from the root Cargo.toml workspace. Whenever core types changed in breadstuffs/types, pg-dregg was left uncompiled. Furthermore, its Lean link relied on an ad-hoc C FFI wrapper (lean_producer.rs) rather than a unified compiler.
  1. fhIR-0: While the type checker correctly categorized programs into Dark/Shielded/Open tiers, the underlying mathematics required all constraint matrices in the Dark tier to be public (due to the limitations of FHE matrix-vector multiplication). This severely restricted its usefulness for arbitrary trading products. When the proving lane prioritized Plonky3 recursion, FHE development was paused.
  1. Polisware and the Unification Defect: The meta-review in project-polis-unification-correction.md (2026-07-06) found that the celebrated "non-domination $\equiv$ light-client-unfoolability" grand unification was a shape identity with zero shared proof content. The anti-vacuity checks were checking accept ≠ True rather than ∃ c, accept c, and DECO rung 5 was an empty tautology ($P \to P$). The theory was frozen at tag cut-trace-remainder-0.1 and never wired to live LLM swarms.
  1. Multi-Chain Governance Proof-of-Holdings Gap: As recorded in project-chain-participation-epoch.md, dregg-interchain-gov could verify read proofs from Solana, EVM, and Cosmos, but it had no mechanism to derive voting weight from holdings. Every voting engine defaulted to a static, hardcoded [u8; 32] voter set with one-voter-one-vote.

4. The Grand Revival Roadmap: Unifying with Modern Kernels

Our current formal kernels—obend, minidregg, svenvs, and o7vn—provide the exact architectural foundation needed to revive these dropped gems cleanly, eliminating the ad-hoc FFI layers and fragility of the 2026 monolith.

text
Dropped Gem                   Modern Kernel Anchor              Revival Architecture
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
pg-dregg                      minidregg + o7vn                  Implement as a SQL Foreign Data Wrapper (FDW)
(Verifiable Postgres RLS)                                       where row-security policies evaluate against minidregg's
                                                                Mathlib-free WorldCore theorems.
fhIR-0                        obend/Hardware + Leaf             Formulate convex program admissibility as Open Hypergraph
(FHE Convex Product DSL)                                        transformations and Futamura projections in Bend/Lean.
Polisware & Commons Arbiter   svenvs + o7vn                     Ground democratic deliberation in svenvs's HOL4 libertyScript
(Non-Domination Deliberation)                                   theorems; replace regex prompt filters with Oracle7 specialists.
CapTP over Simplex            obend/Consensus + Vault           Embed sturdy capabilities directly into Obend.Vault hyperedges,
(Distributed Capabilities)                                      using Simplex BFT notarizations instead of blocklaces.
Multi-Chain Interchain Gov    obend/Ledger/Seat                 Map cross-chain read proofs (EVM MPT, Tendermint, Solana)
(Non-Custodial Weighting)                                       directly to Obend Ledger Seats with dynamic token-weighting.
Durable Workflow Engine       hosted-durable + minidregg        Express workflow checkpoint replays as deterministic
(Metered Orchestration)                                         minidregg Turn evaluations, eliminating duroxide SQLite locks.

4.1. Reviving pg-dregg via minidregg and o7vn

In breadstuffs, pg-dregg suffered from an unmaintained C-FFI link to Lean. In minidregg, the entire state transition system is self-contained, Mathlib-free, and compiles to clean C via lake build.

  • Revival Path: Compile minidregg/Kernel/World.lean directly into a PostgreSQL C extension.
  • Instead of maintaining complex LRU caches in Rust, PostgreSQL triggers execute World.step directly, checking RLS policies as pure Lean predicates.
  • o7vn's SQL reasoning specialists can synthesize and verify complex JSONPath queries with formal correctness proofs.

4.2. Reviving fhIR-0 via obend Open Hypergraphs

obend already models categorical computation through Obend.Hardware.OpenHypergraph and Futamura partial evaluation in Obend.Leaf.Futamura.

  • Revival Path: Define convex optimization cones as monoidal categories within OpenHypergraph.
  • The six-part admissibility theorem can be proved by showing that partial evaluation of a Dark-tier product eliminates all private matrix parameters, generating a pure public-matvec residual.
  • This elevates fhIR-0 from a speculative type checker to a verified program-extraction compiler for homomorphic clearing.

4.3. Reviving Polisware and Commons Arbiter via svenvs and o7vn

svenvs holds the ultimate formal formulation of non-domination in higher-order logic (libertyScript.sml).

  • Revival Path: Port Metatheory.Polis.polis_safety directly to the svenvs kernel.
  • Replace the crude {{ prompt-injection detection in commons-arbiter with semantic evaluation through o7vn's 145 verified native specialists.
  • When an AI arbiter rules on a dispute, it emits an o7vn reasoning certificate proving that its conclusion follows strictly from the constitutional rubric without hallucination or premise alteration.

4.4. Reviving CapTP over Simplex BFT in obend

The fatal flaw of CapTP in breadstuffs was its binding to the blocklace DAG, which incurred $O(\text{history})$ tau-ordering costs.

  • Revival Path: Port CapTP's sturdy references and 3-party handoffs to Obend.Consensus.Simplex.
  • Instead of reconciling DAG forks, capability handoffs land as linear transactions in Obend.Vault.VaultTurn.
  • Simplex notarization certificates (NotarizationCert) provide instant 2-step finality, allowing distributed object capabilities to pipeline promises across nodes with zero DAG latency.

4.5. Reviving Multi-Chain Non-Custodial Governance in obend/Ledger

dregg-interchain-gov proved that read proofs could verify cross-chain balances without wrapping tokens, but stalled on the proof-of-holdings weighting bridge.

  • Revival Path: Integrate foreign chain read proofs directly into Obend.Ledger.Seat.
  • An incoming EIP-1186 MPT proof (Ethereum) or ICS-23 proof (Cosmos) is verified against the finalized header in a SeatTurn.
  • The verified balance dynamically parameters the voter's voting power in Obend.Ledger.Book, finally realizing the vision of true cross-chain non-custodial governance.

Dossier 22: Objective Bend (obend) — Language Thesis, Equi-Recursive Core4 Type Theory, LTUO Grounding (fareoo), and the Astra Effect-Law & Consensus Document Substrate

[!IMPORTANT] Scope & Primary Source Grounding Whereas Dossier 07 examined obend's silicon/FPGA synthesis and dynamic partial reconfiguration leaf (Obend.Silicon, ExprCAM, ICAP, Sky130 DRC, CDNA3/FP4 equivalence), this monograph documents Objective Bend (obend) as a programming language, object/extension calculus, equi-recursive / algebraic-subtyping type theory, effect-law runner, and consensus document substrate. Every claim is grounded directly in three primary-source repositories on disk: 1. /Users/ember/dev/obend (386 commits through 7c699ca, Nov 1, 2026; 452 Lean 4 modules under lean/Obend/, 45,588 Lean kernel constants, 0 sorryAx, 4 Rust crates, stdlib/, and sandstorm/). 2. /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions (frozen decision & lane archive at ed2878d / checkpoint-20261101 6266457, capturing 300 Lean modules, 35,127 kernel constants, 0 sorryAx, 59 LANE-*-BRIEF-*.md / REPORT-*.md files, and the October 29 – November 1, 2026 architectural debates). 3. /Users/ember/dev/fareoo (22 commits, Oct 1–3, 2026, HEAD d7ddf53: "Mirror current LTUO and ground stronger Objective Bend semantics").

1. Executive Thesis & Intellectual Genealogy (fareoo → minidregg + nanodregg → obend)

1.1 The Core Thesis of Objective Bend

Objective Bend opens README.md and THESIS.md with a single unifying sentence:

"Objects anyone can extend without forking, laws judged on every write, documents that remember why they say what they say." — /Users/ember/dev/obend/README.md, /Users/ember/dev/obend/THESIS.md

The architectural problem Objective Bend solves is stated in THESIS.md and formalized in paper/objective-bend.md:

  1. Software, prose, and machine-checked proofs rot by the same mechanism: an author publishes an artifact $A$; a downstream stranger wants $A$ except for one field, one policy, or one theorem assumption. Without open recursion and first-class specifications, the stranger must fork $A$—copying its source and severing its history—or beg the upstream maintainer to merge a patch.
  2. Simultaneously, references rot silently: when $A$ is revised, retired, or splits into two incompatible branches, downstream dependents either silently drift to unreviewed code (moving references like latest) or silently point to dead assumptions (pinned hashes whose underlying facts no longer hold).
  3. Why not plain Bend? As documented in ASTRA-SYNTHESIS-20261029.md, HigherCo's Bend / BendTT is an interaction-net functional language whose core discipline is source-level affinity: variables are used at most once so match frees graph nodes in place and fork-join parallelism needs no garbage collector or lock. However, Cook/Bracha/Faré open recursion (mix l u := λself. λsuper. u self (l self super) and fix s b := let rec self = s self (b self) in self) uses self twice in every composition step! Under a strict affine discipline, mix fails live-variable linearity whenever self carries captured methods or non-shareable state.
  4. Objective Bend's Synthesis: Objective Bend places lazy call-by-name open recursion (Core4), first-class specification/prototype reflection, C4 multiple-inheritance linearization, six-occasion state laws (Law), and contextual truth-maintenance slots (Slot, combining Nanevski–Pfenning–Pientka Contextual Modal Type Theory with Doyle's 1979 ATMS supports) at the top level of the language, while compiling closed leaf computations down to affine BendTT / interaction-net Book definitions via Obend.Leaf (lean/Obend/Leaf.lean) and Obend.Lower (lean/Obend/Lower.lean).

1.2 The fareoo Excavation (d7ddf53): Auditing minidregg Against Faré's Lambda, the Ultimate Object

Between October 1 and October 3, 2026 (22 commits, from initial commit 8f1d452 to HEAD d7ddf53 "Mirror current LTUO and ground stronger Objective Bend semantics"), Ember created /Users/ember/dev/fareoo to mirror François-René Rideau's (Faré's) monograph Lambda, the Ultimate Object — Prototype- and Class- orientation, Typed Open Recursion, Purity, Hygiene, Winning Expressiveness vs Performance Trade-offs, Compatibility, Evolution, Great and Small, Using Metaphors, Category Theory, etc. (sources/ltuo-20261001.html, 4,266,596 bytes, SHA-256 4c1845238e04ddba74f13bfaa11dc6a863f54df0cf4532dd6c9059fa3e24e355, with the Objective Bend excerpt in sources/ltuo-objective-bend-20261003.txt).

In /Users/ember/dev/fareoo/FAREOO.md (1,003 lines) and /Users/ember/dev/fareoo/AUDIT.md (792 lines), Ember conducted a brutal clause-by-clause audit of minidregg's existing Objective Bend prototype against LTUO's twelve canonical object-orientation requirements:

  1. The Wegner (1987) Diagnosis: AUDIT.md concluded that minidregg's runtime was object-based, not object-oriented in Wegner's sense ($\text{OO} = \text{objects} + \text{classes/kinds} + \text{inheritance}$), because objects were flat mutable hash cells whose laws and kinds lived outside the open-recursive mix/fix tower.
  2. Three Fatal Structural Gaps Identified in AUDIT.md:
  3. K-LAW-INHERIT (Laws Replaced Whole — Below Teitelman's 1966 ADVISE Floor): In minidregg, adopting or extending a cell's law replaced the prior law outright (newLaw) rather than conjoining it along the C4 ancestry DAG (superLaw && selfLaw). AUDIT.md noted this fell below Warren Teitelman's 1966 PILOT / Interlisp ADVISE (BEFORE/AFTER/AROUND advice combinators) and Keene's 1989 CLOS :before/:after/:around method combination.
  4. K-KIND-CELL (Kinds as Second-Class Host Rebuilds): In minidregg, a "kind" was a host-level table entry rather than a first-class cell in the World with its own content-addressed pin, law, and extends lineage. Consequently, creating an instance (create --from K) or deriving a subkind (derive-kind --from K) could not be tracked by truth-maintenance supports.
  5. K-CARRY (Upgrade by Re-Genesis): Upgrading an object schema in minidregg required re-genesis or out-of-band host migration rather than in-place carry over Self/Super specifications.
  6. Ember's Formal Correction to Faré's LTUO §7.3.5 (The C4 Poset Invariance Bug): In LTUO §7.3.5 (sources/ltuo-objective-bend-20261003.txt), Faré claimed that the C4 multiple-inheritance linearization algorithm (which refines Barrett et al.'s 1996 Dylan C3 linearization by dropping the ad-hoc caller parent-list tie-breaker in favor of a suffix-closed hierarchy) is a canonical function of the bare inheritance poset/DAG. While proving C4's metatheory in Lean 4, Ember discovered a 2-parent counterexample (FAREOO.md, paper/objective-bend.md):
  7. Consider diamond inheritance where C inherits from two incomparable parents A and B (both over root O).
  8. As a bare poset, $\{C < A < O, C < B < O\}$ is symmetric between $A$ and $B$.
  9. Yet any total linearization must order either $A \prec B$ ([C, A, B, O] from presentation ((C A) (C B))) or $B \prec A$ ([C, B, A, O] from presentation ((C B) (C A))).
  10. Therefore, C4 is not invariant on the bare poset; it is invariant up to injective renaming of the ordered presentation (OrderedPresentationInvariant in lean/Obend/Spec/C4Vectors.lean, mechanically verified across 4,000 seeded DAGs and proved structurally in lean/Obend/Spec/Proofs/C4.lean).

1.3 Merging minidregg and nanodregg into obend

As recorded in GROUNDING.md and PLAN.md, obend was created by fusing two complementary lineages into a single zero-axiom (0 sorryAx) Lean 4 + Rust repository:

  • From minidregg (092027a2): The Core4 lazy call-by-name operational semantics (Obend.Core.Term, Obend.Core.Machine, Obend.Core.Soundness), the .obend surface parser and modular template elaborator (Obend.Spec), C4 linearization (Obend.Spec.C4), and the six-occasion law compiler (Obend.Law).
  • From nanodregg (d81faee): The open protocol & truth-maintenance slot calculus (Obend.Slot), the single-pass audited fold (Obend.Fold), the graded certificate judge and verified LRAT SAT checker (Obend.Judge), Merkle-backed receipts with epistemic disclosure dials (Obend.Receipt), pushout patch documents (Obend.Doc), and the guarded activity runner (Obend.Run).

2. The Core4 Calculus, Equi-Recursive Type Theory, and Algebraic Subtyping

2.1 Syntax of Core4 (Obend.Core.Term)

Unlike standard $\lambda$-calculi where objects are encoded via heavy Church/Bruce-Cardelli-Pierce existential packings, Obend.Core.Term (lean/Obend/Core/Term.lean) makes open recursion, specifications, prototypes, reflection, and yielded plans primitive constructors of a 26-constructor de Bruijn syntax:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Core/Term.lean:17-58
inductive Primitive where
  | add | multiply | equal | conjunction | labelEqual
  | subtract | divide | less | lessEqual | modulo
  deriving Repr, DecidableEq

inductive Term where
  | hole (n : Nat) (σ : List Term)
  | bound (index : Nat)
  | lam (body : Term)
  | app (function argument : Term)
  | mix (inheritedExtension wrappingExtension : Term)
  | fix (specification inherited : Term)
  | specification (metadata extension : Term)
  | prototype (specification target : Term)
  | reflect (prototype : Term)
  | metadata (specification : Term)
  | project (prototype : Term)
  | nat (value : Nat)
  | boolean (value : Bool)
  | label (value : String)
  | binary (primitive : Primitive) (left right : Term)
  | extend (inherited : Term) (fields : List (String × Term))
  | record (fields : List (String × Term))
  | get (target : Term) (name : String)
  | ifZero (value zero successorBody : Term)
  | inject (label : String) (payload : Term)
  | case (scrutinee : Term) (arms : List (String × Term))
  | ifBool (condition whenTrue whenFalse : Term)
  | perform (plan : Term)
  | done (value : Term)
  | split (target : Term) (names : List String) (function : Term)
  deriving Repr

The object-oriented and contextual primitives in Term have exact operational reductions in lean/Obend/Core/Term.lean and lean/Obend/Core/Machine.lean:

  • specification m e and metadata s: Packages a metadata record $m$ (such as C4 lineage, law descriptors, or documentation) alongside an open-recursive extension function $e : \text{Self} \to \text{Super} \to \text{Row}$. metadata (specification m e) reduces in one step to $m$; applying (specification m e) as a function reduces to e.
  • mix l u (Wrapper/Mixin Composition): Given lower extension $l$ and upper wrapping extension $u$, mix l u is a weak-head value that, when applied to self and super, feeds l self super as the super argument of u self: $$\mathsf{mix}\;l\;u\;\mathsf{self}\;\mathsf{super} \;\longrightarrow\; u\;\mathsf{self}\;(l\;\mathsf{self}\;\mathsf{super})$$
  • fix s b (Lazy Open-Recursive Tie): Closes specification $s$ over base $b$ by passing the fixed point fix s b itself as self and b as super: $$\mathsf{fix}\;s\;b \;\longrightarrow\; s\;(\mathsf{fix}\;s\;b)\;b$$
  • prototype s t, project p, and reflect p: prototype s t pairs a live target $t$ with the specification $s$ that generated it without losing the open specification. project (prototype s t) ⟶ t extracts the live object (and get (prototype s t) f ⟶ get t f delegates field lookup directly), while reflect (prototype s t) ⟶ s recovers the unclosed specification $s$ so any stranger can later run fix (mix (reflect p) delta) base!
  • hole n σ (Contextual Modal Delayed Substitution): Added in commit d8db9f8 (astra/HOLE-REPAIR-SET-20261030.md), hole n σ represents contextual metavariable ?n suspended with an explicit delayed substitution $\sigma : \text{List Term}$ of its local de Bruijn context $\Delta_n$. Under Term.rename and Term.substitute, operations map functorially across $\sigma$: $$\text{substitute}\;\tau\;(\mathsf{hole}\;n\;\sigma) = \mathsf{hole}\;n\;(\sigma.\text{map}\;(\text{substitute}\;\tau))$$ When the hole is filled by a closed-in-$\Delta_n$ candidate $u$ via fillHole, $\sigma$ is discharged by applySub σ u (Obend.Core.Hole, lean/Obend/Core/Hole.lean), satisfying Contextual Modal Type Theory's (CMTT) commute law: $$\text{fillHole}\;n\;u\;(\text{substitute}\;\tau\;t) = \text{substitute}\;\tau\;(\text{fillHole}\;n\;u\;t)$$
  • perform plan and done value (Yielded Activities): perform p is not a value and has no internal reduction step; when the demand machine reaches perform p at top level, it halts in Yields p so the guarded runner (Obend.Run) can judge the emitted Plan against world laws and resume.
  • split target names function (Multiplicative Linear Record Destructor): Added in commit ef8f814 (lean/docs/REPORT-LINEAR-20261101.md), split forces target to a .record fields, extracts every field in names simultaneously, and passes them in order to curried function, enabling tensor-elimination ($\otimes$-elim) of records containing multiple linear capabilities without dropping siblings.

2.2 The Core4 Type & Quantity System (Obend.Core.Ty and Obend.Core.Check)

Core4's type grammar (lean/Obend/Core/Ty.lean) integrates a 4-point usage semiring (Quantity), closure reuse (Reuse), row-polymorphic records and variants with rigid Self/Super variables, first-class specification/prototype types, linear custody tokens, and computation (activity) types:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Core/Ty.lean:7-35
inductive Quantity where
  | erased | affine | linear | unrestricted
  deriving Repr, DecidableEq
inductive Reuse where
  | once | reusable
  deriving Repr, DecidableEq

inductive Ty where
  | natural | label | boolean
  | variable (index : Nat)
  | arrow (reuse : Reuse) (parameter : Quantity) (domain codomain : Ty)
  | emptyRow
  | field (name : String) (member tail : Ty)
  | specification (metadata extension : Ty)
  | prototype (specification target : Ty)
  | custody (identity : Nat)
  | variant (row : Ty)
  | computation (plan response result : Ty)
  deriving Repr, @[expose] DecidableEq

Resourceful Shadow Retention (insertCanonical)

A subtle soundness hazard in row-typed languages with linearity or capability custody is resource hiding via field shadowing: if a record { cap : Custody(0), cap : Nat } overwrites cap with a shareable Nat, naive row canonicalization drops the shadowed Custody(0) field and makes the resulting record look shareable = true! Core4 prevents this in Ty.insertCanonical (lean/Obend/Core/Ty.lean):

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Core/Ty.lean:118-124
@[expose] def Ty.insertCanonical (row : Ty) (name : String) (member : Ty) : Ty :=
  match row with
  | .field prior old tail =>
      if name = prior then (if old.shareable then .field name member tail else .field name member row)
      else if name < prior then .field name member row
      else .field prior old (tail.insertCanonical name member)
  | tail => .field name member tail

When old.shareable is false (e.g., old is .custody id, a .once closure, an unshared rigid .variable, or a .computation), insertCanonical keeps row (old and its tail) behind the new .field name member, proving Ty.shareableUnder_canonical so canonical row equality can never launder a linear or once-only capability.

Coeffect Usage Vectors and linear Exact Discharge

In lean/Obend/Core/Check.lean, typing judgments synthesize a bottom-up usage vector uses : List Nat indexed by the lexical context $\Gamma$:

  • erased: count = 0 (safeQuantity .erased c := c == 0).
  • affine: count ≤ 1 (safeQuantity .affine c := c ≤ 1).
  • linear: count = 1 at binder discharge (discharged .linear c := c == 1), count ≤ 1 in subterms (safeQuantity .linear c := c ≤ 1), exact equality across control-flow joins (Quantity.agrees .linear c₁ c₂ := c₁ == c₂), and linearFree on lazy thunks that might discard or duplicate their environment (such as unforced arguments to non-.linear parameters, specification metadata discarded on application, or single-field .get projections that drop sibling fields).
  • unrestricted: any count : Nat, provided the argument type satisfies shareableUnder assumptions.shareableVariables and all captured variables in a .reusable closure are shareable (capturesNotShareable).

In lean/Obend/Core/LinearSoundness.lean (closed in commit ef8f814, REPORT-LINEAR-20261101.md), Ember proved that on the strict value-substitution operational semantics (ValueStep) where .linear arrows step by call-by-value (app_linear_val / app_linear_step), a single linear hypothesis at de Bruijn index 0 satisfies Linear1:

  • valueStep_preserves_typing_and_linear1: Every value step preserves both PartialTyping and the exact syntactic occurrence count (occ 0 = 1), and at a final value occ 0 = 0 (value_consumes_linear: the linear resource has been consumed exactly once).
  • Furthermore, LinearSoundness.lean proves the negative theorem cbn_breaks_linear_occ: under Core4's lazy call-by-name reduction, $(\lambda x :_1 A.\; x)\;((\lambda y :_1 A.\; \mathsf{nat}\;0)\;z)$ would step to $(\lambda y :_1 A.\; \mathsf{nat}\;0)\;z$ without forcing the inner redex first, justifying why split and strict linear beta are required for runtime resource accounting.

2.3 The Equi-Recursive Type Migration (89bd283, Oct 31, 2026)

One of the deepest type-theoretic achievements in obend is the equi-recursive type agreement replacement executed on October 31, 2026 (commit 89bd283, documented in lean/docs/REPORT-EQUIREC-20261031.md and the 131-entry mechanical journal lean/docs/EQUIREC-JOURNAL-20261031.txt).

Why 1-Step legacySameType Failed on Self-Referential Specifications

Prior to 89bd283, Obend.Core.Check.sameType only unfolded declared rigid type bounds assumptions.bounds by one step (or along acyclic alias chains). However, in LTUO / Objective Bend, any object whose method returns Self (such as a counter whose inc : Nat → Self returns a new counter, or a stream whose tail : Self returns the rest of the stream) induces a cyclic bound in Assumptions: $$X_0 \;\mapsto\; \{\;\mathsf{val} : \mathsf{Nat},\; \mathsf{inc} : \mathsf{Nat} \to X_0\;\}$$ Under 1-step legacySameType, comparing $X_0$ against its twice-unfolded expansion $\{\;\mathsf{val} : \mathsf{Nat},\; \mathsf{inc} : \mathsf{Nat} \to \{\;\mathsf{val} : \mathsf{Nat},\; \mathsf{inc} : \mathsf{Nat} \to X_0\;\}\;\}$ failed! This forced lean/Obend/Core/BidirectionalCompleteness.lean to carry a negative theorem full_corollary_fails showing that a legitimate declarative PartialTyping derivation could be rejected by checkAgainst when recursive bounds were unfolded to different depths.

The 8-Module Amadio–Cardelli Finite-Graph Bisimulation Spine

Ember replaced sameType with an executable, sound, and complete Amadio–Cardelli equi-recursive decision procedure across eight dedicated modules under lean/Obend/Core/:

ModulePathKey Definitions & Theorems (0 sorryAx)
FiniteBisimulationlean/Obend/Core/FiniteBisimulation.leanGeneric cycle-detecting reachability engine over finite state spaces (Finset S), proving visitedSame_iff and Equivalence (reflexivity, symmetry, transitivity of greatest bisimulation).
RecursiveRowslean/Obend/Core/RecursiveRows.leanDefines semantic row observations: visible head field lookup (lookup), tail kind (emptyRow vs rigid variable), and ordered resourceful shadow streams (shadowStream) so that shadowed non-shareable fields remain bisimulation-observable at every unfolding depth!
TypeGraphlean/Obend/Core/TypeGraph.leanBuilds the finite subterm closure of (actual, expected, assumptions.bounds) and proves candidate_iff: candidate assumptions a b = true ↔ Equivalent assumptions a b.
TypeGraphAliaslean/Obend/Core/TypeGraphAlias.leanProves alias_equivalent: every declared bound (i, bound) ∈ assumptions.bounds satisfies Equivalent assumptions (.variable i) bound, even when i occurs guarded inside bound.
FieldCongruencelean/Obend/Core/FieldCongruence.leanProves field_congruence: adding or replacing a field preserves Equivalent across arbitrary cyclic tails.
FiniteRowLawslean/Obend/Core/FiniteRowLaws.leanProves finite_field_commute ($f_1 \neq f_2 \Rightarrow \{f_1:A, f_2:B \mid r\} \sim \{f_2:B, f_1:A \mid r\}$) and finite_field_shadow (when the shadowed field is shareable).
CanonicalExtensionlean/Obend/Core/CanonicalExtension.leanProves legacySameType_extends: every pair accepted by the legacy canonicalizer is in Equivalent.
EquiRecursiveAgreementlean/Obend/Core/EquiRecursiveAgreement.leanExports agrees, agrees_iff, agrees_eq_candidate, agrees_equivalence, and all structural congruence lemmas, wired into Obend.Core.Check.sameType.

With sameType := EquiRecursiveAgreement.agrees wired into Obend.Core.Check, lean/Obend/Core/BidirectionalCompleteness.lean flipped full_corollary_fails into the positive completeness theorem full_annotated: every declaratively well-typed PartialTyping term with representative annotations is accepted by checkAgainst (0 sorryAx), while preserving progress_and_preservation and checked_execution_soundness in lean/Obend/Core/Soundness.lean.

2.4 The Algebraic Subtyping Investigation (review/ALGEBRAIC-SUBTYPING-20261031.md)

Alongside equi-recursive equality, Ember investigated whether Objective Bend should adopt Dolan's (2017) Algebraic Subtyping (MLsub) or Parreaux's (2020) Simple-sub (biunification over polar type lattices with $\top, \bot, \sqcup, \sqcap, \mu$). The complete mathematical analysis is preserved in /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/review/ALGEBRAIC-SUBTYPING-20261031.md:

  1. Where Biunification Helps: Every Core4 type constructor except .specification and .prototype has clean polarity:
  2. arrow r q A B: contravariant in $A$, covariant in $B$, covariant in Reuse ($\mathsf{reusable} \le \mathsf{once}$), contravariant in Quantity along the usage-allowance order ($\mathsf{erased} \sqsubseteq \mathsf{linear}, \mathsf{affine} \sqsubseteq \mathsf{unrestricted}$).
  3. field l A R and variant R: covariant in member $A$ and tail $R$, with dual width subtyping (records drop fields going up: $\{l:A \mid R\} \le_{\text{rec}} R$; variants inject cases going up: $R \le_{\text{var}} [l:A \mid R]$).
  4. computation P R A: covariant in emitted plan $P$, contravariant in environment response $R$, covariant in result $A$: $$\frac{P \le P' \qquad R' \le R \qquad A \le A'}{\mathsf{computation}\;P\;R\;A \;\le\; \mathsf{computation}\;P'\;R'\;A'}$$
  5. Why mix and fix Break Plain MLsub Without Guarded Recursive Rows: As shown in ALGEBRAIC-SUBTYPING-20261031.md, in specification M (Self → Super → Row), the extension type has Self and Super in contravariant domain positions, while fix ties Self to the covariant output Row (Row ≤ Self). Under biunification, fix induces a polar cycle $\alpha^+ \le \{\text{fields}\}^+ \le \alpha^-$ that simplifies directly to an equi-recursive $\mu$-type $\mu\alpha.\{\text{fields}(\alpha)\}$—which is why the equi-recursive graph engine (Obend.Core.TypeGraph) is the exact prerequisite for polar subtyping.
  6. The Three Hard Boundaries Against Naive Width Subtyping:
  7. Resourceful Width Subtyping (linear / custody): Dropping a field via record width subtyping $\{a:\mathsf{Custody}(0), b:\mathsf{Nat}\} \le \{b:\mathsf{Nat}\}$ leaks the linear custody token! Width forgetting is sound only on fields where member.shareable = true—which is the exact invariant enforced by Obend.Elab.Coerce (lean/Obend/Elab/Coerce.lean).
  8. Reflective Transparency (prototype S T): Because reflect : prototype S T → S returns $S$ covariantly while prototype : S → T → prototype S T consumes $S$, prototype S T must remain invariant in $S$ whenever reflection is permitted, or else be split into a write/reflect view vs. a read-only projected view.
  9. Shadowed Field Subtyping under Late extend: If an object $\{a:\mathsf{Nat}, b:\text{self}.a\}$ is coerced by dropping $a$ before fix closes self, late binding of self.a dangles. Thus subtyping coercions apply to closed values (prototype/record) after fix, whereas open specifications retain rigid row tails.
