3.1 Obend.Slot (lean/Obend/Slot.lean) — Contextual Truth-Maintenance Holes

Obend.Slot bridges Obend.Slot.Protocol (free indexed monads Prot S Hole A i with static holes) to a live changing World (Obend.Slot.World, lean/Obend/Slot/World.lean). Every deferred decision is a Slot Fact Cand (lean/Obend/Slot.lean):

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Slot.lean:37-81
structure Slot (Fact Cand : Type) where
  supports : Cand → List Fact
  judge : Cand → Bool

inductive Reading (Fact Cand : Type)
  | empty
  | rejected (c : Cand)
  | usable (c : Cand) (evidence : List (Fact × Bool))
  | blocked (c : Cand) (dead : List Fact)

@[expose] def Slot.read (S : Slot Fact Cand) (holds : Fact → Bool) : Option Cand → Reading Fact Cand
  | none => .empty
  | some c =>
    if S.judge c then
      match S.dead holds c with
      | [] => .usable c (S.evidence holds c)
      | d => .blocked c d
    else .rejected c

Four fundamental theorems (each guarded against vacuity by #tooth ... by mutation, lean/Obend/Slot.lean) govern every slot read:

  1. read_usable_supports (Slot.lean:130-132): (∃ ev, S.read holds (some c) = .usable c ev) ↔ S.judge c = true ∧ ∀ f ∈ S.supports c, holds f = true.
  2. read_blocked_exact (Slot.lean:135-138): S.read holds (some c) = .blocked c' d → d ≠ [] ∧ ∀ f, f ∈ d ↔ f ∈ S.supports c ∧ holds f = false (a blocked read names every dead support and nothing else, at least one).
  3. permanent_blocked_forever (Slot.lean:142-155): If a support $f \in S.\text{supports}(c)$ is Permanent holdsAt later f (such as a retired cell or an answered one-shot ring), then once $f$ goes false at view $v$, $c$ reads .blocked c d with $f \in d$ at every later view $v'$.
  4. read_of_usable (Slot.lean:158-165): Selecting a usable candidate $c'$ immediately reads .usable c' (S.evidence holds c') without reinstalling the slot's supports or judge.

In lean/Obend/Slot/World.lean, a World V Fact equips cells with versioned, content-addressed pins (PinId = ByteArray, computed via cSHAKE256 in Obend.Receipt.CShake256) and proves:

  • retired_dead: Retiring a cell permanently falsifies .live cell.
  • ver_step: Revising a cell increments its version strictly.
  • stale_behind_forever: A support citing a superseded pin remains false at every later view unless a hash collision (Collides) occurs.

In lean/Obend/Slot/Selection.lean and lean/Obend/Slot/Integrity.lean, SelectionSound and incumbentTransition prove that incumbent-preserving slot selection never switches away from a still-usable incumbent (usable_incumbent_kept) and switches to a fallback only when the incumbent is blocked (switched_only_when_incumbent_blocked).

3.2 Obend.Intention (lean/Obend/Intention.lean) — LTUO §9.3.7 Named References as Slots

In LTUO §9.3.7, Faré defines an Intention as a named reference that records how the author intends it to track upstream evolution (e.g., pinned to a specific content hash vs. following a branch/cell head). Obend.Intention proves that an Intention is not a separate primitive: it is a Slot Fact Pin whose candidates are pins of the target cell (lean/Obend/Intention.lean):

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Intention.lean:36-93
structure Intention (W Ref Pin Fact Err : Type) where
  resolve : W → Ref → Except Err Pin
  supports : Ref → Pin → List Fact
  judge : Ref → Pin → Bool
  holds : W → Fact → Bool

def Intention.slot (I : Intention W Ref Pin Fact Err) (r : Ref) : Slot Fact Pin where
  supports := I.supports r
  judge := I.judge r

def Intention.stale (I : Intention W Ref Pin Fact Err) (w : W) (r : Ref) (p : Pin) : List Fact :=
  (I.slot r).dead (I.holds w) p

Its four core theorems (Intention.lean:111-189) establish:

  • stale_iff_blocked: When I.judge r p = true, I.read w r (some p) = .blocked p d ↔ d ≠ [] ∧ d = I.stale w r p.
  • resolve_fill: If I.resolve w r = .ok p and ValidResolve holds, then (I.slot r).read (I.holds w) (some p) = .usable p ((I.slot r).evidence (I.holds w) p).
  • renew_repairs: I.renew w r = .ok p (re-running I.resolve w r when a following reference falls behind) produces a pin that reads .usable immediately.
  • stays: For any view transition w → w' that preserves the supports of (r, p), a usable pin p remains .usable at w'.

Obend.Intention is instantiated in three distinct places across the codebase:

  1. Package extends references (Obend.Spec.Ecosystem, lean/Obend/Spec/Ecosystem.lean).
  2. Document section citations (Obend.Doc.Section, lean/Obend/Doc/Section.lean).
  3. Nominal kind parent lineages (Obend.Kind.kindRefs, lean/Obend/Kind.lean).

3.3 Obend.Spec (lean/Obend/Spec.lean) — The .obend Front End, C4 Linearizer, and Modular Template Elaborator

Obend.Spec compiles .obend source files into checked Obend.Core.Term programs plus a typing proposal verified by Obend.Core.Bidirectional.checkAgainst.

Concrete .obend Surface Syntax (stdlib/std.obend and Spec/Syntax.lean)

A .obend program consists of optional package headers (package NAME [extends REF ...]), law blocks, let bindings, template declarations (template (self : Self, super : Super) [metadata M] [law L] { field = expr, ... }), mix, fix, prototype, reflect, project, extend, split, perform, and done. For example, /Users/ember/dev/obend/stdlib/std.obend defines reusable standard-library specifications:

obend
// /Users/ember/dev/obend/stdlib/std.obend (excerpt)
let counter_base =
  template (self : Self, super : Super)
  metadata { name = "std.counter.base", version = 1 }
  {
    count = 0,
    step  = 1,
    read  = self.count,
    bump  = self.count + self.step
  }
in
let counter_double =
  template (self : Self, super : Super)
  metadata { name = "std.counter.double", version = 1 }
  {
    step = super.step * 2
  }
in
let spec = mix counter_base counter_double in
let proto = prototype spec (fix spec { count = 0, step = 1, read = 0, bump = 0 }) in
proto.bump

Modular Template Typing (Rules D1–D6) and Composition Contracts (Spec/Elaborate.lean, Spec/Contract.lean)

As documented in paper/objective-bend.md and implemented in lean/Obend/Spec/Elaborate.lean and lean/Obend/Spec/Contract.lean:

  • Every template (self, super) is elaborated once, at definition site, against two distinct rigid row variables $X_{\text{self}}$ and $X_{\text{super}}$ whose lower bounds in Assumptions.bounds record only the fields read from self.f and super.g inside that template's body (Rules D1–D6).
  • Obend.Spec.Contract proves that layer contracts form a monoid under mix (run_append, run_assoc, checkLayer_ok, discharge_ok), and Obend.Spec.Proofs.Compose proves composeTy_closed and compose_typed: Specification<T> is closed under compose without re-inspecting template bodies at fix time!
  • End-to-end front-end soundness is proved in Obend.Spec.Proofs:
  • accepted_typed: Every .obend source accepted by FrontEnd.accept has a valid PartialTyping derivation in Obend.Core.Check.
  • accepted_never_refused: The bounded demand machine never halts in MachineError on an accepted program.
  • accepted_execution_semantics: A finished demand-machine run extracts the exact deep source evaluation (DeepEval) of the elaborated term.
  • decode_json / decodePacket_term: The wire decoder inverts the front end's canonical wire encoding, so a receiving node checks the exact Term rather than re-parsing untrusted source text.

LawDials and Bellman-Ford Law Repair Synthesis (Spec/LawDial.lean)

When composing multiple inheritance layers whose per-field laws impose numeric difference constraints $x_i - x_j \le c_{ij}$ (for example, min_balance <= balance in one layer and balance <= cap in another), lean/Obend/Spec/LawDial.lean provides:

  • confluent_order_independent: Conjunction of pure predicate laws over C4 is commutative, associative, and idempotent up to boolean equivalence.
  • synthesizeLawRepair: Runs a verified Bellman-Ford shortest-path / negative-cycle detector over difference-constraint laws:
  • synthesizeLawRepair_sound: Every synthesized repair assignment satisfies all conjoined layer constraints.
  • synthesizeLawRepair_complete_refutation: When synthesizeLawRepair refuses, it emits a negative-weight cycle witness proving that the conjoined laws are unsatisfiable over $\mathbb{Z}$.

3.4 Obend.Elab (lean/Obend/Elab.lean) — Annotation Preparation, Coercions, and the 16-Cell Quantity Matrix

Obend.Elab sits between unannotated/partially-annotated terms and Obend.Core.Bidirectional:

  1. Obend.Elab.Prepare (prepare_checks): lean/Obend/Elab/Prepare.lean implements prepare : Assumptions → Context → Term → Annotations → Ty → Annotations, which walks any declaratively well-typed Checkable term and fills in every representative annotation required by Bidirectional.checkAt. Theorem prepare_checks proves that checkAgainst succeeds on (prepare ...) (0 sorryAx).
  2. Obend.Elab.Coerce (coerce_checks and the 16-Cell Quantity Matrix): lean/Obend/Elab/Coerce.lean synthesizes explicit Core4 $\eta$-expansion wrappers witnessing subtyping coercions Sub assumptions A B without changing the kernel checker!
  3. Dropping a shareable record field $f$ elaborates to a record reconstruction reading the kept fields.
  4. Coercing a function type (r₁, q₁, A₁ → B₁) ≤ (r₂, q₂, A₂ → B₂) wraps $f$ in $\lambda x :_{q_2} A_2.\; \text{coerce}_{B_1 \le B_2}(f\;(\text{coerce}_{A_2 \le A_1}\;x))$.
  5. Because the wrapper binds $x$ at $q_2$ and passes it to $f$ expecting $q_1$, Coerce.lean proves the exact 16-cell quantity compatibility matrix (qle : Quantity → Quantity → Bool, verified completeness by counterexample on all 9 false cells in lean/docs/REPORT-ELAB-20261031.md):
q₁ \ q₂ (Domain Expects \ Wrapper Binds)erasedaffinelinearunrestricted
erasedfalsetruefalsetrue
affinefalsetruefalsetrue
linearfalsetruetruetrue
unrestrictedfalsefalsefalsetrue

(Why is (erased, erased) false for an $\eta$-wrapper $\lambda x.\; f\;x$? Because the wrapper's syntactic body f x mentions x once in the argument position, so an erased binder on the $\eta$-lambda fails binderOverused with count = 1 > 0!)

  1. Obend.Elab.Work (checkWork): lean/Obend/Elab/Work.lean defines the fuel-free structural recursion measure checkWork : Term → Nat (sizeOf), proving checkWork_pos and strict decrease on every recursive call of synthAt / checkAt.

3.5 Obend.Judge (lean/Obend/Judge.lean) — Graded Proof Checkers and Verified Foreign LRAT SAT Checking

Obend.Judge makes proof and certificate checking pluggable while preventing trivial (fun _ _ => true) judges from being registered:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/obend/lean/Obend/Judge.lean:38-80
class Judge (Obj Cert : Type) where
  Valid : Obj → Prop
  Invalid : Obj → Prop := fun o => ¬ Valid o
  Fragment : Nat → Obj → Prop := fun _ _ => True
  disjoint : ∀ {o}, Valid o → Invalid o → False := by intro _ hv hi; exact hi hv
  check : Nat → Obj → Cert → Decision
  sound : ∀ {k o c}, check k o c = .proved → Valid o
  refuted : ∀ {k o c}, check k o c = .refuted → Invalid o
  complete : ∀ {k o}, Fragment k o → Valid o → ∃ c, check k o c = .proved
  mono : ∀ {k k' o c d}, k ≤ k' → d ≠ .inconclusive → check k o c = d → check k' o c = d
  • trivial_unregistrable (Judge.lean:216-221): Every registered Format Obj must carry a planted invalid witness (witnessObj, witnessCert) that its decoder + checker does not accept (witness_not_proved), making fun _ _ _ => .proved unregistrable at the type level.
  • Verified Foreign LRAT Checker (Judge/Lrat.lean, Judge/Identity.lean, Judge/Certified.lean): In lean/Obend/Judge/Lrat.lean, Ember implemented a total, verified LRAT (Linear Reverse Asymmetric Tautology) unsatisfiability certificate checker with lrat_sound proved against boolean assignment semantics (0 sorryAx). In lean/Obend/Judge/Certified.lean, an external CaDiCaL 3.0.1 SAT solver emitted a 215-step LRAT proof (noCarryLrat, 272 clauses over 97 variables) for the 8-bit noCarry arithmetic identity (a + b == a ^ b) ↔ (a &&& b == 0); Lean's kernel verifies the foreign LRAT certificate via decide +kernel, discharging noCarry_holds : ∀ a b : BitVec 8, ...!

3.6 Obend.Fold (lean/Obend/Fold.lean) & Obend.Receipt (lean/Obend/Receipt.lean) — Single-Pass Audited Folds and cSHAKE256 Epistemic Receipts

In Objective Bend, batch verification of a state log, a document history, or a multi-runner ledger is a single left fold List.foldl parameterized by an Admission W Rest Row Fact Clause (lean/Obend/Fold.lean):

  • receipt_sufficient (Fold.lean:160-169): Any two worlds w and w' that agree on the pins read by row r (∀ c ∈ A.reads r, w.pinOf c = w'.pinOf c) step identically (A.step (w, rest) r = .ok (w, rest') ↔ A.step (w', rest) r = .ok (w', rest')). Thus a verifier never needs the full World—only the Merkle openings of A.reads r!
  • project_moves_run (Fold.lean:245-261): Proves Jones-style rely–guarantee decomposition over a shared Family: if other principals' moves satisfy OtherStep (preserving this principal's relied-upon invariants), projecting a joint multi-principal fold onto principal $i$ yields a valid local Run of $i$.

Each admitted row issues a Receipt Cell Pin Fact Clause (lean/Obend/Receipt.lean) backed by a positional FIPS 202 cSHAKE256 Merkle tree (Obend.Receipt.CShake256 and Obend.Receipt.Merkle, lean/Obend/Receipt/Merkle.lean, proved collision-binding in opens_binds). Every receipt carries three explicit epistemic dimensions:

  1. Shape (Receipt.lean:274-292): Records the quantifier alternation of the verified claim, ordered linearly from weakest to strongest: $$\mathsf{existsRun}\;(\exists)\;<\;\mathsf{planForAll}\;(\exists\text{plan}\,\forall\text{env})\;<\;\mathsf{policyForAll}\;(\exists\text{policy}\,\forall\text{env})\;<\;\mathsf{forAllPlan}\;(\forall\text{plan}\,\exists\text{run})\;<\;\mathsf{forAll}\;(\forall)$$ Theorem shape_strict (Receipt.lean:314-319) proves that each level strictly implies the previous and is separated by a concrete countermodel (exists_not_forAll).
  2. Trust (Receipt.lean:328-367): Tags each support as .checked (verified by a kernel Judge) or .attested (signed by an external oracle/principal). Theorem ceiling_checked proves that a receipt's overall trustCeiling is .checked if and only if every support it cites is .checked (∀ p ∈ r.checked, p.2 = .checked).
  3. Dial (Receipt.lean:376-403): Controls selective disclosure (full vs. cited vs. bit). Theorem dial_invariant proves that redacting a receipt via r.disclose d never alters its shape, trustCeiling, or outcome!

3.7 Obend.Law (lean/Obend/Law.lean) & Obend.Kind (lean/Obend/Kind.lean) — Six-Occasion Laws, C4 Conjunction, and First-Class Nominal Kinds

To close fareoo's K-LAW-INHERIT and K-KIND-CELL gaps, Obend.Law and Obend.Kind formalize laws and nominal kinds directly over Core4:

  • Six Occasions (Occasion, Law.lean:45-64): Every cell transition fires on one of six occasions:
lean
  inductive Occasion where
    | birth | adopt | relaw | retire | write | invoke
    deriving DecidableEq, Repr
  • Laws as Core4 Terms (lawTy S, Law.lean:77-78): A law over state row S is an ordinary Core4 term of type Ctx(S) → Bool, where Ctx(S) is the 7-field record { occasion : Label, who : Label, before : S, after : S, action : Label, spend : Nat, epoch : Nat }.
  • Standard Law Generators (Obend.Law.Std, lean/Obend/Law/Std.lean): Provides six composable combinators (ownerSigns, allowlist, bounded, machine, quorum, windowed) plus keeps : Law → Claim → Prop, proving guardedBy_sound and chain_keeps (an inductive invariant preserved across arbitrary admitted transition chains).
  • C4 Conjunction Inheritance (K-LAW-INHERIT Closed, Law.lean:268-401): Given C4 ancestry anc : List (String × Term) from root to self, effectiveLaw anc compiles to a right-associated Core4 .ifBool short-circuit conjunction. Three theorems seal K-LAW-INHERIT:
  • effective_law_eval: Evaluating effectiveLaw on the Core4 demand machine equals (anc.all fun (_, l) => evalLaw B l c = .ok true).
  • inherited_never_widens: Extending an ancestry anc with additional layers more can never turn a false verdict into true (evalEffective B anc c = .ok false → evalEffective B (anc ++ more) c = .ok false). A subkind or wrapper can only strengthen its ancestors' laws, never bypass them!
  • effective_refuses_first: When a transition is refused, checkEffective blames the first ancestor (by, l) in C4 root-to-self order whose law returned false, returning Refusal := { by, reason }.
  • First-Class Nominal Kinds and Lineage Staleness (Obend.Kind, Kind.lean:39-340): A KindCell lives in KindWorld with its own content-addressed KindPin (computeKindPin over name, parents, lawTag, shape, and full resolved lineage).
  • Why does KindPin hash the full lineage : List (String × ByteArray) rather than just direct parents? During the October 30 audit (review/KIMI-REVIEW-20261030.md), Kimi pointed out that if C extends B and B extends A, revising grandparent A without modifying B's declaration would leave B's pin unchanged if B only hashed parent names! Including lineage in KindPin enabled Ember to prove ancestor_revised_stale (Kind.lean:291-340): revising any proper ancestor $A$ in a kind's C4 DAG changes resolveLineage, changes computeKindPin, and immediately marks every downstream following kind reference stale!
  • Furthermore, nominal_tooth (Kind.lean:231-244) proves that two objects c₁ and c₂ with identical structural shape and body (c₁.shape = c₂.shape ∧ c₁.body = c₂.body) but distinct nominal kind tags ("Meters" vs "Feet") dispatch to different methods under Kind.dispatch (dispatch table c₁ = some "add-meters" vs some "add-feet").

3.8 Obend.Doc (lean/Obend/Doc.lean) — Patch Groupoid, Pushout Merge, and Documents That Remember Why

Obend.Doc turns structured documents into consensus objects with categorical patch history:

  • Typed Graph Patches (Obend.Doc.Patch, lean/Obend/Doc/Patch.lean): Document edits are typed operations Add, Delete, Connect, SetField over node/edge graphs, forming a partial action with composition (apply_compose), inversion (apply_invert: invert q undoes q on any document where q succeeds), and framing (apply_frame: patches on disjoint footprints commute).
  • Pushout Merge and Conflict Characterization (Obend.Doc.Merge, lean/Obend/Doc/Merge.lean): Over the thin category Le of non-conflicting patch extensions (Obend.Doc.Category), Doc.merge is proved to be the categorical pushout:
  • merge_isPushout & pushout_unique: When two branches $p, q$ do not conflict (conflicts p q = []), merge p q is the unique least upper bound in Le.
  • no_cocone: When conflicts p q ≠ [], no cocone exists in Le (∀ u, ¬(Le p u ∧ Le q u)).
  • files_no_pushout: Formalizes Mimram & Di Giusto's (2013) diagram (3) showing that line-based file patches do not have pushouts (two compatible upper bounds with no common refinement), justifying why Objective Bend versions typed node/edge/field graphs rather than raw text lines!
  • Sections as Slots & Backlinks (Obend.Doc.Section, Obend.Doc.Backlink, Doc.lean:155-268): Every document section is a Slot whose candidates carry prose, code, and citations (Intentions to other sections or proof objects). When merge encounters a conflict on key k, conflict_is_section turns the conflict into a live Section holding both competing candidates [v₁, v₂] rather than aborting the document (conflict_section_usable). And usable_unmoved proves that editing any node outside a section's cited support cone leaves the section's .usable reading untouched.

3.9 Obend.Run (lean/Obend/Run.lean) — The Guarded Runner, Selection Control, Shared Ledger, Crash Recovery, and Offline Fork/Stitch

Obend.Run executes open objects whose methods yield Activity<Plan, Response, Result> computations (Term.perform plan):

  1. Guarded Dispatch & Replanning (Obend.Run.Runner, Obend.Run.Proofs.Invariant):
  2. refines: Every reachable configuration of Runner.run satisfies Inv, so its accepted event log is Legal (legal_iff_admission).
  3. inflight_replan_refused & lost_reply_frozen: While an external call is in flight, replanning that slot is refused (busy); if a reply is lost (abandon), the obligation remains recorded in the log (abandon_keeps_obligation).
  4. Selection Control (Obend.Run.Control, lean/Obend/Run/Control.lean):
  5. Every hole resolution checks the caller's principal against the slot's chooser office and applies one of three verified presets (firstUsable, incumbentFirst, chooserRequired).
  6. Tenure (whileInOffice vs lasting): Under .whileInOffice, revoking a chooser's office immediately invalidates selections resting on .holdsOffice who (office_revoked_blocks_whileInOffice), whereas .lasting selections survive office rotation provided their domain supports still hold (office_revoked_keeps_lasting).
  7. Multi-Runner Shared Ledger (Obend.Run.Shared, lean/Obend/Run/Shared.lean):
  8. Arbitrates any number of controlled runners over a shared World through an atomic commit boundary.
  9. joint_reachable_ticket_exclusive & loser_refused_names_winner_and_epoch: A one-shot capability token (OnceSpent) can be spent by at most one runner across the entire joint execution, and the losing runner receives a structured refusal naming the winning principal and commit epoch.
  10. Journal-First Durable Crash Recovery (Obend.Run.Durable, lean/Obend/Run/Durable.lean):
  11. Formalizes a host with volatile memory and a durable append-only journal + outbox where the journal append is the linearization point.
  12. recover_restores_memory & exposed_once: Across arbitrary crash-and-replay sequences, volatile state is restored to the last journaled commit and any external effect consuming a single token is exposed to the outbox at most once.
  13. Offline World Fork & Atomic Stitch (Obend.Run.Fork, lean/Obend/Run/Fork.lean):
  14. An offline worker forks the World (Fork.fork); monotone rows merge by union, while bounded/exclusive rows escalate unjudged.
  15. When reconnecting, Fork.stitch replays escalated dispatches through the joint runner+ledger transition atomically: stitch_is_commit proves accepted stitches equal online commits, stitch_double_spend proves a token spent both on main and on the offline fork is refused at stitch time naming the online winner and epoch, and every refused offline branch is preserved as a conflict Doc.Section (stitch_refusal_kept_as_section).

4. The Late-October / November 1, 2026 Architectural Debates in obend-astra-decisions

The repository /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions preserves the complete decision archive, formal lane briefs (59 LANE-*-BRIEF-*.md and REPORT-*.md files in lean/docs/), adversarial model reviews (review/), and Astra synthesis documents (astra/) from the intense October 29 – November 1, 2026 verification sprint. Below we reconstruct the seven defining technical debates and their formal resolutions.

4.1 Astra Effect Laws (astra/ASTRA-EFFECT-LAWS-20261031.md) and Guarded Law Judgments (Obend.Run.LawJudge)

In /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/astra/ASTRA-EFFECT-LAWS-20261031.md, Ember and Astra confronted how object laws interact with effectful activities (Term.perform plan in Obend.Run):

  • The Problem: In minidregg, a cell law was only a state predicate Ctx(S) → Bool evaluated synchronously on state writes. When Objective Bend introduced Activity<Plan, Response, Result> (Ty.computation), an object could yield an external Plan (such as transferring custody or invoking a remote capability) across multiple asynchronous turns. If laws only checked completed state writes, an illegal external Plan could escape to the outbox before its state write occurred!
  • The Resolution (Obend.Run.LawJudge and Obend.Law.Machine):
  • Every emitted Plan is judged at dispatch time (Occasion.invoke) before leaving the runner (Obend.Run.LawJudge, lean/Obend/Run/LawJudge.lean), passing through the full C4 effectiveLaw conjunction of the acting cell and its kind (dispatch_checked_by_effective_law).
  • When a reply returns (or is abandoned), the continuation transition is re-judged against the updated world epoch (resume_rechecked_at_epoch), preventing time-of-check to time-of-use (TOCTOU) drift across asynchronous yields.
  • In lean/Obend/Law/Machine.lean (REPORT-LAWFINISH-20261031.md), stateful multi-step protocol laws are compiled into deterministic finite-state probes (Machine.probe) with proved judge_sound and judge_complete instances for Obend.Judge.

4.2 The Contextual Hole Repair Exercise (astra/HOLE-REPAIR-SET-20261030.md)

On October 30, 2026 (commit d8db9f8), Ember tested a major metatheoretic hypothesis: Can contextual modal holes (Term.hole n σ, with delayed explicit substitutions $\sigma : \text{List Term}$) be added directly to the 25-constructor Core4 AST without shattering the existing 15,000-line soundness and bidirectional completeness proofs?

As documented in /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/astra/HOLE-REPAIR-SET-20261030.md:

  • First, Ember factored a generic binder-aware functorial traversal Obend.Core.Traverse (lean/Obend/Core/Traverse.lean), proving that Term.rename and Term.substitute are instances of one structural traversal (traverse).
  • When Term.hole (n : Nat) (σ : List Term) was added as constructor #1 of Term, 572 kernel declarations had their types or terms revised, yet only 20 authored proofs across 10 files required manual human/agent repair! Every other lemma across Core/, Spec/, and Law/ discharged automatically via the uniform Traverse induction principle.
  • In lean/Obend/Core/Hole.lean, Ember proved the CMTT contextual substitution commutation laws (rename_fillHole, substitute_fillHole, and typing_fillHole), establishing that Obend.Slot's runtime hole resolution and Obend.Core.Term's syntactic .hole n σ share a single formal foundation.

4.3 The Justification Semiring Refutation & The Parsimony Deletion (REPORT-JUST-20261031.md, REPORT-PARSIMONY-20261101.md)

One of the most striking episodes in obend-astra-decisions is the rise, formal refutation, and surgical deletion of the justification polynomial semiring (Obend.Just and Obend.Slot.PolySlot).

  1. The Original Hypothesis (LANE-JUST-BRIEF-20261031.md): Early design notes proposed generalizing Slot's flat support list supports : Cand → List Fact to positive boolean provenance polynomials $\mathbb{N}[X]$ quotiented by Distributive Lattice (DL) equations ($a + a = a$, $a \cdot a = a$, $a + a \cdot b = a$), claiming that (a) absorption $a + a \cdot b = a$ implies multiplicative idempotence $a \cdot a = a$, (b) automated truth-maintenance repair always finds a single-pin replacement (T2), and (c) factoring Slot, Intention, and Receipt through Just.lean would shrink downstream proof obligations.
  2. Astra's Two Formal Countermodels (lean/docs/REPORT-JUST-20261031.md):
  3. Algebraic Countermodel (absorption_not_mul_idempotent): Astra constructed a 3-element commutative semiring $\{0, 1, 2\}$ where addition is idempotent and absorption $a + a \cdot b = a$ holds everywhere, yet $2 \cdot 2 = 0 \neq 2$! Thus mul_idem had to be added as an independent axiom of Why polynomials.
  4. Repair Soundness Countermodel (clash_unsat): Astra proved that naive repair completeness (T2) is false when a candidate's justification polynomial conjoins two distinct pins of the same cell ($c@p_1 \wedge c@p_2$ with $p_1 \neq p_2$), because a single cell in World has a unique current pin w.pinOf c! Thus repair soundness holds if and only if the support conjunction satisfies the pairwise pin-compatibility invariant Repair.Compatible.
  5. The Parsimony Audit and Deletion (commit f1e1406, lean/docs/REPORT-PARSIMONY-20261101.md): On November 1, 2026, Ember wrote tools/proofsize.py to measure whether factoring Slot through Just.lean actually shortened the proof spine. The script revealed that the polynomial-factored spine was 5 lines longer (and added 1,872 lines of semiring canonicalizer machinery) than the direct List Fact formulation! Following Occam's razor, commit f1e1406 (LANE-PARSIMONY) deleted Just.lean, PolySlot.lean, and Slot/Repair.lean (-1,872 net lines), preserving only the battle-tested List Fact spine in Obend.Slot.

4.4 Clairvoyant Call-by-Value vs. Lazy Self-Referential Records (REPORT-COST-20261101.md)

In LTUO and DESIGN.md, a recurring question was whether Core4's lazy call-by-name mix/fix evaluation could be cost-modeled by Hackett & Hutton's (2019) clairvoyant call-by-value semantics (which models call-by-need sharing by non-deterministically choosing at each binder whether to evaluate the argument once or skip it if unused).

In lean/Obend/Core/Cost.lean and lean/Obend/Core/Clairvoyant.lean (reported in lean/docs/REPORT-COST-20261101.md):

  1. Positive Theorem (mix_assoc_cost): On the Core4 demand machine (Obend.Core.Machine), re-associating a 3-layer mixin composition mix (mix a b) c vs. mix a (mix b c) preserves the exact result and changes the machine tick count by at most an additive constant of 8 ticks (CostWithin 8, 2 ticks per mix frame push/pop).
  2. Negative Refutation (self_field_no_cv and ticks_not_within_cv): When Astra formalized Hackett–Hutton clairvoyant CBV over Core4's open-recursive records, it discovered that whole-record clairvoyant CBV has NO derivation at all for { a = 7, b = self.a }.b!
  3. Why? To evaluate fix spec base eagerly to a record value under whole-record CBV, self must be demanded before spec self base returns the record { a = 7, b = self.a }, creating a strict cycle on self even when b only reads the already-convergent sibling field self.a!
  4. By contrast, on Core4's lazy demand machine, { a = 7, b = self.a }.b converges in 25 ticks because fix substitutes fix spec base by name and .get ... "a" only forces field a, never forcing the entire record at once.
  5. Consequently, Clairvoyant.lean proves self_field_no_cv (no clairvoyant CBV cost exists for self-sibling record projection) and restricts the clairvoyant cost correspondence to the non-self-sibling fragment (cv_sound_non_self).

4.5 The Symbolic Mathematics Substrate (NEXT-MATHEMATICA.md and Obend.Cas / Obend.Math)

In /Users/ember/dev/obend/NEXT-MATHEMATICA.md (253 lines), Ember articulated why Objective Bend's combination of open-recursive specifications, Judge-verified certificates, and Doc sections is the natural substrate for a verified symbolic computation & computer algebra system (CAS) surpassing untrusted rewrite systems like Mathematica:

  • The Diagnosis of Traditional CAS: In Mathematica or SymPy, symbolic simplification rules are global, unverified pattern-rewrite tables; adding a domain assumption (e.g., $x > 0$ or $R$ is a commutative ring) mutates global context or forks the simplifier, and when a branch cut or pole invalidates an identity, notebook cells downstream silently display stale formulas.
  • Objective Bend's CAS Architecture (lean/Obend/Cas/ and lean/Obend/Math/):
  • Algebraic Theories as Open Specifications: Ring, field, differential, and polynomial normalizers are open templates composed via mix; domain assumptions are Slot supports (Fact).
  • Rewrites Judged by Certificates (Obend.Judge): Every non-trivial symbolic step (polynomial ideal membership, Positivstellensatz witness, truth-table / LRAT unsatisfiability, interval enclosure) emits a certificate checked by a registered Judge, recording its quantifier Shape and Trust ceiling in an Obend.Receipt.
  • Live Notebooks as Obend.Doc: Equations and derivations are Doc.Section slots citing upstream lemmas via Obend.Intention. If an upstream domain assumption is retracted or a parameter crosses a branch cut, every dependent symbolic derivation transitions immediately from .usable to .blocked naming the exact falsified assumption!

4.6 Consensus Contracts and DeOS / Dregg2 Grounding (DEOS-CONTRACTS-CONSENSUS.md and Obend.Dregg2 / Obend.DeOS)

In /Users/ember/dev/obend/DEOS-CONTRACTS-CONSENSUS.md (185 lines), Ember mapped how Objective Bend serves as the smart-contract and decentralized operating system (DeOS / Dregg2) kernel:

4.7 The November 1, 2026 Multimodel Council Audits (ASTRA-COUNCIL-20261101.md and FABLE-COUNCIL-20261101.md)

On November 1, 2026, Ember convened two independent multimodel verification councils—recorded in /Users/ember/dev/obend/astra/ASTRA-COUNCIL-20261101.md and /Users/ember/dev/obend/review/FABLE-COUNCIL-20261101.md (synthesizing Kimi, Argon, and Fable)—to audit both the checkpoint-20261101 spine (commit 6266457, 300 Lean modules, 35,127 constants, 0 sorryAx) and the subsequent late-November-1 burst (commits ec3e54c..7c699ca, which expanded the tree to 452 Lean modules and 45,588 constants, 0 sorryAx).

The Councils' findings are remarkable for their precision:

  1. Zero Kernel Axioms & Non-Vacuity Tooth Guards: Across all 452 Lean modules (45,588 constants), sorryAx count is strictly zero (0), and the core spine uses #tooth <thm> by mutation <def> := ... (Obend.Tactic.Tooth, lean/Obend/Tactic/Tooth.lean) to mechanically verify that mutating the underlying definition breaks the theorem's proof, ruling out vacuous False → ... premises.
  2. Four Genuine Kernel Advancements in the Nov 1 Burst (ec3e54c..7c699ca):
  3. ef8f814 (REPORT-LINEAR-20261101.md): Added Term.split (multiplicative record destructor) and proved Obend.Core.LinearSoundness (valueStep_preserves_typing_and_linear1).
  4. a4959c0 (REPORT-KINDS-20261101.md): Bridged Obend.Kind into Core4's type checker by encoding a nominal kind tag K as a phantom #kind : Label(K) field in Ty.field, proving that structural width coercion (Obend.Elab.Coerce) refuses to coerce across distinct #kind tags when #kind is protected.
  5. c490226 (REPORT-QUOTE1-20261101.md): Oriented Obend.Quote.Eval so reflect (prototype s t) reifies s into a quotation record carrying its content hash and source AST (quote_reflect_sound).
  6. f0bb666 (Obend.Lower): Built a Tait–Girard reducibility predicate (Obend.Lower.Tait, lean/Obend/Lower/Tait.lean) proving strong normalization on the fix-free Core4 fragment and compiling closed first-order functions to affine BendTT interaction-net Book rules (Obend.Lower.Bridge).
  7. Critical Findings & Gaps Exposed by the Councils:
  8. Prose vs. Code Drift in DESIGN.md §1: FABLE-COUNCIL-20261101.md §2 pointed out that DESIGN.md still describes aspirational dependent type constructors (pi, sigma, id, refl, jElim, universe u) from an early BendTT design draft that do not exist in Obend.Core.Term or Obend.Core.Ty (which implement the simply-typed/row-polymorphic/equi-recursive Core4 calculus).
  9. Trust Boundary Probes in AstraDecisionProbe.lean: In /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/AstraDecisionProbe.lean, Ember constructed machine-checked counterexample probes demonstrating two subtle trust boundaries:
  10. Export.verify Caller-Supplied Root (probe_export_accepts_any_dated): In Obend.Receipt.Export, Export.verify checks that a receipt's Merkle openings match the dated root passed to verify, meaning the caller must authenticate the dated state root out-of-band (or bind it to Run.Shared's ledger root).
  11. quoteVia Principal Agnosticism (probe_quoteVia_ignores_principal): In Obend.Quote, quoteVia checks that a capability grant exists in the table without binding the grant's Principal to the caller—a gap that was subsequently closed in Obend.Run.Reflect (lean/Obend/Run/Reflect.lean, theorem forged_descriptor_refused and metadata_grant_cannot_quote).
  12. Shallow Encoding in Late-Burst Domain Leaves: Both councils distinguished the deeply verified 300-module core spine (Core, Spec, Elab, Slot, Intention, Judge, Fold, Receipt, Law, Kind, Doc, Run, Leaf, Lower) from the late Nov 1 domain-application leaves (Compat, Vcs, Verify, Effects, Capabilities, Consensus, Silicon), noting that several late domain leaves model state transitions as finite struct/boolean predicates rather than embedding through Obend.Core.Term.

6.2 Open Architectural Decisions Awaiting Ember's Judgment

In /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/astra/ASTRA-DECISIONS-20261101.md, /Users/ember/dev/obend-astra-decisions/review/DECISIONS-20261031.md, AstraDecisionEmbedded.lean, and AstraDecisionProbe.lean, six concrete architectural decisions are queued for Ember's final ruling:

  1. Decision 1 — Kernel Subtyping vs. Elaboration Coercions (Obend.Elab.Coerce vs. Polar TypeGraph):
  2. Current State: Obend.Core.Check uses symmetric equi-recursive agreement (EquiRecursiveAgreement.agrees), while width/depth/quantity subtyping is elaborated via explicit $\eta$-wrappers in Obend.Elab.Coerce (coerce_checks).
  3. Trade-off: Explicit $\eta$-wrappers keep the kernel small and respect the 16-cell quantity matrix, but allocate wrapper closures at runtime and cannot coerce inside invariant prototype S T specifications. Replacing sameType with directed polar simulation in TypeGraph (review/ALGEBRAIC-SUBTYPING-20261031.md) would make shareable width subtyping zero-cost at runtime.
  4. Decision 2 — Call-by-Need Heap Machine vs. Demand Machine (Obend.Core.Machine + Obend.Core.Clairvoyant):
  5. Current State: Obend.Core.Machine is a lazy call-by-name environment machine, and REPORT-COST-20261101.md proved that Hackett–Hutton whole-record clairvoyant CBV cannot type { a = 7, b = self.a }.b (self_field_no_cv).
  6. Decision Needed: To get proved memoization sharing across diamond self projections without losing lazy field convergence, should Core4 add a per-field lazy heap store (Launchbury / Sestoft mark-1 abstract machine with per-field thunks) and prove representation adequacy against Obend.Core.Term?
  7. Decision 3 — Binding Export.verify to Authenticated World Roots (AstraDecisionProbe.lean):
  8. Current State: As demonstrated in AstraDecisionProbe.lean, Obend.Receipt.Export.verify validates a receipt against a caller-supplied dated Merkle root.
  9. Decision Needed: Should Export.verify require an authenticated Ledger.CommitRoot (from Obend.Run.Shared / Obend.Vault) or principal signature so unanchored receipts cannot be verified against self-signed roots?
  10. Decision 4 — Deep Embedding vs. Pruning of the Late Nov 1 Domain Leaves (ASTRA-COUNCIL-20261101.md):
  11. Current State: The 300-module spine at checkpoint-20261101 (6266457) is deeply connected end-to-end, whereas ~120 modules added in the late Nov 1 burst (Compat, Vcs, Verify, Effects, Capabilities, Consensus) are self-contained shallow models.
  12. Decision Needed: Should those late domain leaves be refactored to compile through Obend.Spec / Obend.Core.Term / Obend.Run (as AstraDecisionEmbedded.lean demonstrates by running a parsed .obend specification through Spec.FrontEnd.accept → Law.effectiveLaw → Run.Runner), or pruned back to checkpoint-20261101 + LinearSoundness + Lower + Kind + Quote?
  13. Decision 5 — Reconciling DESIGN.md §1 with Core4's Actual Type Grammar (FABLE-COUNCIL-20261101.md):
  14. Current State: DESIGN.md §1 still lists dependent pi, sigma, id, refl, jElim constructors from an early draft, whereas all theorems in Obend.Core.* are proved over Core4 (Ty.arrow, Ty.field, Ty.specification, Ty.prototype, Ty.custody, Ty.variant, Ty.computation) and dependent proof checking is delegated to Obend.Judge certificates.
  15. Decision Needed: Update DESIGN.md §1 so the prose matches the proved Core4 + Obend.Judge architecture (or decide whether any indexed dependent fragment belongs inside Core.Ty).
  16. Decision 6 — Strict CBV vs. Lazy CBN for Linear Capabilities (Obend.Core.LinearSoundness):
  17. Current State: LinearSoundness.lean proves exact single-consumption (occ 0 = 1 → 0) under strict ValueStep for .linear arrows (app_linear_val) and .split, and proves cbn_breaks_linear_occ for lazy CBN substitution.
  18. Decision Needed: Should Obend.Core.Machine (the primary demand machine) switch .linear-annotated applications to strict call-by-value evaluation so the main machine inherits LinearSoundness directly?

Dossier 23: The Capability OS, Semantic Kernel, and Sovereign Edge Firmament (minidregg, claudesplosion, nanodregg, delvetalk2, drorb, DreggNet, DreggCloud)

[!IMPORTANT] Primary-Source Archaeological Scope: Every claim, architectural invariant, theorem signature, and worktree disposition in this monograph is excavated directly from Ember's primary repositories and worktree farms on disk as of November 2, 2026: - /Users/ember/dev/minidregg (8,953 commits, HEAD c4ecabe0f, 2026-10-31), /Users/ember/dev/minidregg-lanes (273 lane entries: 247 git worktrees + 26 build/backup directories), /Users/ember/dev/minidregg-wt (3 git worktrees + seats/), and /Users/ember/dev/claudesplosion (160 commits, HEAD 6c45f2e, 2026-10-03). - /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg (871 commits, HEAD 80c622a, 2026-10-27), /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg-scout (27 scout directories C1..T2), and /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg-wt (9 git worktrees). - /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk (21 commits, HEAD 9f70515) & /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2 (2,583 commits, HEAD fb2179968, 2026-10-15) & /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2-lanes (66 lane entries: 45 git worktrees + 21 handoff/artifact files). - /Users/ember/dev/drorb (618 commits, HEAD ec01e68, 2026-10-06), /Users/ember/dev/drorb-swarm-salvage (7 salvaged Lean 4 modules + 50.6 KB patch), /Users/ember/dev/DreggNet (520 commits, HEAD 2c574b6, 2026-08-06), and /Users/ember/dev/DreggCloud (88 commits, HEAD e9ff17a, 2026-08-29).

1. Executive Thesis & Architectural Lineage

Across more than 13,800 commits and 420+ parallel worktree lanes, Ember constructed a vertically integrated, formally verified capability operating system, semantic kernel, object-capability social world, post-quantum edge network, and sovereign application cloud. The central thesis uniting these repositories is Zero-Twin Semantic Ownership: Lean 4 owns the meaning, the wire format, the transition semantics, the capability admission laws, and the soundness proofs; Rust and Pancake/C move bytes, schedule I/O rings, and enforce OS sandbox boundaries without ever deciding semantic admissibility.

1.1 The Four Structural Movements

  1. From Hand-Written Twins (breadstuffs) to the Derived Semantic Computer (minidregg & claudesplosion):
  2. In /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/ATLAS.md, Ember diagnosed the fundamental pathology of /Users/ember/dev/breadstuffs (~2.89M LOC: 1.82M Rust across 137 crates, 913K Lean across 1,039 files, 160K TS/JS): every derivation compiler had been built and proved at $n = 1$, and then a hand-written twin was maintained beside it in production.
  3. minidregg ("Mini") was founded on a strict ≤10% LOC mandate where the derived path is the only path: a single Lean-compiled host binary serves one append-only state-and-event log (World), evaluates total capability programs written in Objective Bend, enforces relational predicates (Pred), and emits commitment proofs over the Selvage BaseFold + sumcheck + Poseidon2/Goldilocks stack.
  4. Between September 29 and October 3, 2026, Ember orchestrated /Users/ember/dev/claudesplosion: first a 23-lane + 6-lane adversarial formal audit (01..23, mini-1..6, 99-SYNTHESIS.md) that exposed 22+ confirmed-at-source defects across breadstuffs, fn, and minidregg, and then a 67-lane subagent takeover (planning/handoff-2026-10-02/LANE-INDEX.md) that hardened Mini's resident agent loop (Hermes), Sandstorm application marketplace, DREGG/RESOURCE/TRANSACTION v9 compute funding, and prospective document privacy (OBSERVE-CHALLENGE/v9 + OBSERVE-SIGNED/v9).
  5. The Spec-First Single-Writer Distillation (nanodregg):
  6. On October 16, 2026, drawing on the lessons of minidregg's 273 lanes, Ember launched /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg under a 12-law CHARTER.md and a monotonic SIZE.md ratchet: 32,077 deployed Lean lines, 6,703 named theorems, 0 partial defs, 0 sorry, 99 lines of Rust in nd, and 164 lines of Rust in nd-supervise, backed by 27 architectural scouts (nanodregg-scout C1..T2). Every top-level claim in Spec/README.md (OneHistory, Integrity, Consent, Shown) is simultaneously proved of the deployed system (top_*) and refuted on a lazy trivial counterparty (floor_*).
  7. The Live Object-Capability Social Town (delvetalk & delvetalk2):
  8. In /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2 (2,583 commits, live at gsb.fg-goose.online for delve.town and obend.fg-goose.online), Ember turned the Objective Bend kernel into a live multi-world object town (Record, Thing, Kind, Avatar, Library, Mending, Policy, Tide, Workshop) with Faré-style prototype inheritance (fix/mix), Spween scene graphs (world/station.spween), unified law / rule / want modal clauses (ObjectiveBendLaw.lean), stroke-verified LLM "enchantment" painting (Host/Enchant.lean), cross-edition world carry (Host/Carry.lean), and native AT Protocol (did:web, signed k256 MST + DAG-CBOR, 18 online.fg-goose.gsb.* lexicons) federation.
  9. The Verified Post-Quantum Edge, Execution Mesh, and Sovereign Cloud (drorb, DreggNet, DreggCloud):
  10. At the network and hosting perimeter, /Users/ember/dev/drorb provides a formally verified post-quantum (ML-KEM-768 + ML-DSA-65 + verified FIPS 202 Keccak) reverse proxy, TLS 1.3 / HTTP/2 / HTTP/3 / QUIC v1 terminator, and full Tailscale (ts2021) control and data plane in Lean 4 + HOL4 Pancake (datacake) + Rust io_uring/kqueue.
  11. /Users/ember/dev/DreggNet pairs a Lean 4 Pancake reactor compiler (Dsl/Reactor.lean, Dsl/EmitPancake.lean) with a 6-crate Rust execution cloud (dreggnet-exec wasmi, dreggnet-bridge, dreggnet-control boringtun WireGuard mesh, dreggnet-gateway).
  12. /Users/ember/dev/DreggCloud binds the entire stack into a House / World / Room sovereign custody firmament (dreggcloud-house), featuring hybrid Ed25519 + ML-DSA (cipherclerk) command envelopes, DEOS Shadow DOM view trees (dc-delivery-*), live MPC-TLS (TLSNotary schema v14), exact Universe world custody (universe-house-custody schema v15), prepaid service archive custody (service-house-custody schema v16), and the grain-serve Sandstorm-serve capability weld.

2. /Users/ember/dev/minidregg (8,953 Commits): The Lean-Owned Semantic Computer

2.1 The 17 Atlas Laws and the Diagnosis of breadstuffs

In /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/ATLAS.md, Ember documented why minidregg exists. While breadstuffs proved that logic-arithmetization compilers, protocol state-machine generators, and Lean-to-Rust codegen worked individually, each compiler in breadstuffs had been deployed at $n = 1$ alongside a hand-written twin:

  • lean/Proto/Specs/Noise.lean compiled only Noise; the other 7 protocols in crates/proto/ were hand-written Rust.
  • lean/Drakov/Circuits/ByteDecompCircuit.lean discharged only 1 of 20 circuit families; the other 19 were hand-written Plonky2 gates in crates/circuit/.
  • lean/Contracts/Generated/ emitted only 3 files (poseidon_constants.rs, transcript_schedule.rs, error_codes.rs), leaving 114 hand-written files in crates/core/.

ATLAS.md establishes 17 Design Laws for minidregg:

  1. Law 1 (Total & Safe by Construction): User programs are written in Objective Bend, a total functional/imperative capability language with affine linear resources and bounded recursion (fold/bend). Every well-typed program terminates and has a deterministic static polynomial cost bound.
  2. Law 2 (Automatic Arithmetization): The user never writes circuits, AIR rows, or witness generators. Compiler/TraceTable.lean lowers total programs directly to Rank-1 / Degree-2 trace tables over Goldilocks ($\mathbb{F}_p, p = 2^{64} - 2^{32} + 1$).
  3. Law 3 (Automatic Incrementalization / IVC): State machines S → I → S automatically compile to incremental steps without user-managed folding transcripts.
  4. Law 4 (Automatic Privacy / Oblivious Lowering): Values carry explicit security labels; branches on secret data lower to branchless MUX rows (SUMS-DESIGN.txt).
  5. Law 5 (Capability-Safe Shared State): Shared state is factored into independent Cells (CellId → Cell R); transactions declare their cell footprint (read, write, claim, retire) upfront.
  6. Law 6 (Content-Addressed Code & Data): Every program, type, and cell value is addressed by its canonical cryptographic digest.
  7. Law 7 (One Cryptographic Primitive — Poseidon2 over Goldilocks): Hashing, Fiat-Shamir transcripts, Merkle commitments, and AEAD stream encryption all derive from a single permutation (Selvage/Poseidon2Spec.lean).
  8. Law 8 (One Proof System — BaseFold + Sumcheck): Multilinear polynomial commitments via foldable Reed-Solomon codes (Selvage/BaseFold*) + sumcheck replace the 10-way proof-system zoo (Plonky2, Halo2, Nova, SuperNova, Groth16, Stark, KZG, Bulletproofs) of breadstuffs.
  9. Law 9 (Lean Owns the Meaning; Rust Owns Only Execution & OS Sandboxing).
  10. Law 10 (No Hand-Written Twins).
  11. Law 11 (Everything Speaks One Self-Describing Schema): Pred / Kernel.Value replaces separate SQLite, Protobuf, JSON, and custom binary wire schemas.
  12. Law 12 (Effects Are Capabilities over the Same Log): HTTP, timers, LLM calls (Hermes), and Sandstorm grain bridges are algebraic effects (perform) resolved as journal turns.
  13. Law 13 (Local-First, Sync-by-Merkle-Diff).
  14. Law 14 (Upgrades Are Migration Functions).
  15. Law 15 (Errors Carry Blame): Refusals are typed data values naming the exact violated precondition or law clause.
  16. Law 16 (Zero Configuration): One host binary, one store directory.
  17. Law 17 (Never Slower Over Time — $O(\log n)$): Added in late October 2026 (ATLAS.md) after profiling live stores: no admitted operation or read may scale linearly with journal height $H$ or total cell count $N$; every index must be maintained incrementally or backed by a persistent balanced tree (SparseMap / binary Merkle trie).

2.2 Maturity Classification (S / A / P / D / B) in PROJECT.md

To prevent documentation inflation, /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/PROJECT.md grades every subsystem with an explicit 5-tag maturity code:

  • [S] Spec: Total Lean definitions.
  • [A] Axiom-clean: #print axioms verified clean (only propext, Quot.sound, Classical.choice where permitted, zero sorry) via Assurance/TokenCensus.lean.
  • [P] Mechanized Proof: Non-vacuous soundness/refinement theorem discharged in Lean 4.
  • [D] Derived: Emitted automatically from Lean into Rust/C/tables without hand-written glue.
  • [B] Bridged: Wired into the live host binary and exercised by end-to-end executable tests.

2.3 The Semantic Kernel: Kernel/World.lean and Kernel/HostRefinesWorldCore.lean

At the heart of minidregg is /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/Kernel/World.lean. The entire machine state World R is a single sparse map from CellId (Nat) to Cell R, where cell 0 is a distinguished system cell (sysCellId) partitioned by SysSpace:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/Kernel/World.lean:38-115
/-- A single cell in the world state.
    - `program`: digest of the admission predicate `P : R → R → R → Bool`.
      The predicate takes `(oldValue, newValue, txContext)` and returns `true`
      iff the transition is permitted.
    - `value`: the current typed value (`R`).
    - `nonce`: monotone counter incremented on every write.
    - `retired`: once `true`, the cell is permanently frozen / consumed
      (used for one-shot capabilities and nullifiers). -/
structure Cell (R : Type) where
  program : Nat
  value   : R
  nonce   : Nat
  retired : Bool
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/-- The world state is a sparse map from `CellId` to `Cell R`. -/
abbrev World (R : Type) := Minidregg.Theory.SparseMap CellId (fun _ => Cell R)

/-- Read-only ROM commitment view used by ZK / IVC step circuits. -/
structure RomOnly where
  root : Nat
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/-- Sub-namespaces inside the distinguished system cell (`sysCellId = 0`). -/
inductive SysSpace where
  | journal    -- append-only turn journal
  | head       -- current chain head digest / height
  | retired    -- nullifier / retired-cell set
  | parent     -- parent cell provenance
  | spent      -- spent capability tokens
  | allowance  -- storage / compute quota accounting
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/Users/ember/dev/minidregg/Kernel/HostRefinesWorldCore.lean connects the imperative Host loop (HostState, Host.submit) to the pure relational World.step transition:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/Kernel/HostRefinesWorldCore.lean:45-168
/-- Case analysis for any cell across a valid `World.step`: a cell is either
    untouched, freshly created, updated in place with `nonce' = nonce + 1`,
    retired, or is the system cell recording the journal turn. -/
theorem step_cell_cases
    {w w' : World Value} {tx : Transaction}
    (hstep : World.step w tx = .ok w') (cid : CellId) :
    StepCellOutcome w w' tx cid

/-- Rewriting an append-only projection without a valid journal turn is impossible. -/
theorem appendOnly_rewrite_has_no_turn
    {w w' : World Value} (h : IsAppendOnlyRewrite w w') :
    ∀ tx, World.step w tx ≠ .ok w'

/-- Every confirmed receipt returned by the Host represents an admitted World state. -/
theorem confirmed_represents
    {s s' : HostState} {req : SubmitReq} {resp : SubmitResp}
    (h : Host.submit s req = (s', .confirmed resp)) :
    Represents s'.store resp.worldRoot

/-- T4 refinement target: a successful `Host.submit` corresponds to a valid `World.step`
    from the represented pre-state to the represented post-state. -/
theorem host_submit_is_step
    {s s' : HostState} {req : SubmitReq} {resp : SubmitResp}
    (hinv : HostInvariant s)
    (hsub : Host.submit s req = (s', .confirmed resp)) :
    ∃ w w', Represents s.store w ∧ Represents s'.store w' ∧
      World.step w req.toTransaction = .ok w'

2.4 Objective Bend Core4: PROOF-ENG-DESIGN.md, SUMS-DESIGN.txt, and EVENTS-DESIGN.txt

Three foundational design documents in /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/ record how Objective Bend's operational semantics and proof engineering were scaled to handle real applications without proof-Checking blowups:

  1. Structural Case-Principle Refactoring (/Users/ember/dev/minidregg/PROOF-ENG-DESIGN.md):
  2. Prior to the Core4 refactor, Theory/ObjectiveBendTyping.lean had 14 positional PartialTyping.rec inductions (each with 29 positional arguments fun _ _ _ _ ... =>) and 5 duplicated stepRaw case trees across 2,400 lines. Adding a single typing constructor broke 406 positional binders.
  3. Ember introduced Theory/ObjectiveBendStepCases.lean, packaging the per-step case split into a single reusable structure StepCases (P : State → Prop) with one named field per semantic reduction family (stepVar, stepLet, stepPair, stepRecord, stepSum, stepPerform, etc.) and split the subproofs into Theory/ObjectiveBend/Cases/{Activities,Arithmetic,Functions,Pairs,Records,Sums}.lean.
  1. Exhaustive Closed Variant Rows & Oblivious Sums (/Users/ember/dev/minidregg/SUMS-DESIGN.txt):
  2. Implemented in lane W1.3, SUMS-DESIGN.txt added tagged union types Ty.variant (row : Ty) ( reusing Ty.rowEmpty and Ty.rowExtend label payload rest from records) along with Term.inject (label : Label) (payload : Term), Term.case (scrutinee : Term) (branches : Branches), and Term.ifBool:
lean
   -- /Users/ember/dev/minidregg/SUMS-DESIGN.txt:28-64
   -- Typing rules for closed row-typed variants:
   --   RowHas label A row
   --   ----------------------------------------------- (T-Inject)
   --   Γ ⊢ Term.inject label e : Ty.variant row
   --
   --   Γ ⊢ s : Ty.variant row
   --   BranchesCover row branches C
   --   ----------------------------------------------- (T-Case)
   --   Γ ⊢ Term.case s branches : C
  • Crucially, Ty.variant row is shareable iff every payload type in row is shareable (Shareable (Ty.variant row) ↔ RowAllShareable row), and in the oblivious circuit backend (Law 4), Term.case over a secret tag lowers to evaluating all branches over a canonical dummy payload of each branch's type and selecting the result via a chain of Primitive.labelEqual multiplexers (MUX).
  1. Quiescent Algebraic Activities (/Users/ember/dev/minidregg/EVENTS-DESIGN.txt):
  2. Implemented in lane W3.EVENTS, EVENTS-DESIGN.txt replaced ad-hoc host callbacks with a typed algebraic effect discipline:
  3. Types: Ty.computation (plan response result : Ty) (written Activity<Plan, Response, Result> in surface syntax).
  4. Terms: Term.perform (plan : Term) and Term.done (value : Term), sequenced by let x <- e1; e2.
  5. The Quiescent Yield Invariant: By making Ty.computation non-shareable (Shareable (Ty.computation _ _ _) = False), a computation can never be stored inside a shared heap cell or evaluated underneath a lazy update frame on the heap! Consequently, when stepRaw reaches Term.perform vPlan, the machine is guaranteed to have zero Frame.update frames on its continuation stack (no_update_frame_at_yield). The suspended continuation K is a pure first-order value that is serialized into the cell's journal turn, and the turn boundary acts as Nakano's later modality ($\triangleright$)—preserving total normalization within every single turn while permitting arbitrary multi-turn reactive protocols across turns.

2.5 Cross-Repository Cryptographic & Wire Contracts (CROSS_REPO_CONTRACTS.md)

/Users/ember/dev/minidregg/CROSS_REPO_CONTRACTS.md freezes the exact byte-level and field-level contracts shared between minidregg and external verification consumers:

  • UwueavePreoProjectionV2 / ProjectionV3: Deterministic pre-order and projection artifact schemas emitted into prover/generated/uwueave_preo_projection_v2.rs.
  • RuntimeAuthV4: Capability-bound authentication envelope binding (principalPk, worldRoot, cellId, nonce, expiryHeight, actionDigest).
  • ZkmlSuiteArtifact & BaseFoldBcs*: Fixes the Goldilocks Poseidon2 sponge schedule (rate = 8, capacity = 4, domain tag in capacity lane 0), BaseFold Reed-Solomon folding arity, and Rank-1 MLE sumcheck transcript ordering so Lean verifiers (Selvage/) and Rust provers (prover/) agree bit-for-bit.

  • Generated 65 KB (1,016-item) inclusion proofs on every single cell read because the store serialized the full unpruned sparse-map spine per read.
  • This exact forensic trace directly drove the October perf-client, wave-host-light, iter-speed, and nanodregg CHARTER.md ("THE BAR: 1000 records, write p99 < 15ms, cold reopen < 200ms") engineering waves.

5. /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg (871 Commits), /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg-scout (27 Scouts), and /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg-wt

On October 16, 2026, Ember initiated /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg (871 commits through October 27, 2026, HEAD 80c622a)—a radical, spec-first, single-writer distillation of minidregg governed by a 12-law charter, a Critique/Meta-Critique ledger, and an automated size and theorem ratchet.

5.1 The 12 Charter Laws (CHARTER.md) and the Monotonic Size Ratchet (SIZE.md)

/Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/CHARTER.md establishes 12 inviolable laws:

  1. Law 1 (One of Everything): One representation per concept, one wire format (Codec/), one hash/MAC family (cSHAKE256 / KMAC256), one signature scheme (RFC 8032 Ed25519), one store format (seg.log + sealed bases), one language (Objective Bend in Lang/).
  2. Law 2 (Lean Decides; Rust Moves Bytes): Every policy, framing bound, retry budget, and admission rule lives in Lean (Kernel/, Lang/, Protocol/, Host/, Hosting/) or is emitted into Rust (Emit/Rust.lean → client/src/table.rs). The Rust binaries (nd at 99 lines, nd-supervise at 164 lines) contain zero policy branches.
  3. Law 3 (Bytes End-to-End): Proofs do not stop at abstract ASTs; Codec/Proofs/ proves round-trip invertibility (decode (encode x) = .ok x) down to raw ByteArray streams.
  4. Law 4 (TigerStyle Total Fuel-Bounded Loops): Zero partial definitions in deployed Lean modules (0 across all 32,077 lines); every loop is bounded by an explicit structural fuel or protocol Bounds constant.
  5. Law 5 (Performance Designed — THE BAR): Every full judge runs scripts/bar.sh driving two confined members and one confined writer through real sockets over 1,000 records, enforcing write latency and cold reopen budgets.
  6. Law 6 (Named Theorems, No #guard, Tree-Wide Axiom Census): Every claim is a named theorem inspected by gates/ across the entire dependency cone.
  7. Law 7 (Refusals by Name): Every error across all layers is an explicit constructor of a sum type (86 refusal constructors across Kernel.Reject, Lang, Protocol.Fault, Host.Exit, and Hosting), never a string.
  8. Law 8 (Split Proofs by Proofs/ File): Implementation modules (Kernel/World.lean, Protocol/Rows.lean) stay compact and readable; proofs live in */Proofs/*.lean (287 proof modules out of 529 total Lean files).
  9. Law 9 (Every Commit Builds via CAS scripts/commit): Commits land only through a compare-and-swap build judge that compiles every target and runs the theorem/axiom census.
  10. Law 10 (Decisions + Critique + Meta-Critique): Every architectural choice in DECISIONS.md and decisions/*.md (HOST-1..7, CLIENT-1..39, KERNEL-1..66, PROTOCOL-1..72, HOSTING-1..48) includes an adversarial Critique and a Meta-Critique before code is written.
  11. Law 11 (No Twins of minidregg).
  12. Law 12 (The Size Ratchet — SIZE.md): /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/SIZE.md at 80c622a records the exact audited footprint:

5.2 Inside Kernel/World.lean, Protocol/Rows.lean, and Spec/README.md

In /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/Kernel/World.lean, the world state is represented by three persistent binary Merkle tries (Cells, Journal, WindowSlot) indexed over explicit bit-depth keys, and admission returns a closed 39-constructor Reject sum type:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/Kernel/World.lean:24-142
structure Env where
  digest   : ByteArray → Digest
  mac      : Key → ByteArray → Digest
  verify   : PubKey → ByteArray → Sig → Bool
  checkLaw : LawPkg → Value → Value → CallCtx → Except LawRefusal Unit

structure Slot where
  owner     : PubKey
  lawDigest : Digest
  valDigest : Digest
  value     : ByteArray
  nonce     : UInt64
  allowance : UInt64
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

/-- Persistent binary Merkle tries for cells, journal entries, and replay window slots. -/
abbrev Cells      := Trie 64 Slot
abbrev Journal    := Trie 64 Entry
abbrev WindowSlot := Trie 16 ReplayRecord

/-- Closed 39-constructor kernel refusal sum type (Law 7: Refusals by name). -/
inductive Reject where
  | badSig | staleNonce | retiredCell | unknownCell | cellAlreadyExists
  | quotaExceeded | allowanceUnderflow | lawRejected (r : LawRefusal)
  | pinMismatch | rowPinMismatch | rangePinMismatch | windowExpired
  | duplicateCall | invalidGenesis | ... -- 39 named constructors total
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

In /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/Protocol/Rows.lean, the 52 conformance rows are first-class Lean data structures (Row) that both drive the live socket test runner and serve as the subjects of formal proofs in Protocol/Proofs/:

lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/Protocol/Rows.lean:18-76
inductive Expect where
  | admitted (height : Nat)
  | refused  (reason : RefusalName)
  | followed (height : Nat) (root : Digest)
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

inductive Fault where
  | none
  | truncateTail (bytes : Nat)
  | flipBit (offset : Nat)
  | dropFrame (index : Nat)
  | withholdPrefix (upTo : Nat)
  | staleReRun
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

Most remarkably, /Users/ember/dev/nanodregg/Spec/README.md defines four top-level end-to-end system security properties—OneHistory, Integrity, Consent, and Shown—and requires two paired theorems for every property:

  1. top_* (Soundness of deployed): Proves that the real deployed system satisfies the claim (top_oneHistory_member, top_oneHistory_pair, top_integrity_value, top_integrity_law, top_consent_okCommitted, top_consent_final, top_consent_cancelOk, top_consent_staleFinal, top_shown_onlyFollowers).
  2. floor_* (Non-Vacuity / Refutation of trivial): Proves that a lazy trivial implementation fails the exact same statement (floor_oneHistory_member, floor_integrity_law, floor_consent_okCommitted, etc.), mathematically guaranteeing that no specification predicate in Spec/ is vacuously true!

6. /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk & /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2 (2,583 Commits) & /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2-lanes (66 Lanes)

6.1 From delvetalk v1 to delvetalk2 (delve.town, Replay Edition 20)

/Users/ember/dev/delvetalk (21 commits, HEAD 9f70515) began as an early prototype of a Smalltalk/MUD-inspired object-capability environment backed by Lean. In /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2 (2,583 commits, HEAD fb2179968 on October 15, 2026), Ember built the full production system powering delve.town (https://gsb.fg-goose.online, replay edition 20) and its open feed-only sister world (https://obend.fg-goose.online).

As documented in /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/README.md, a town in delvetalk2 is an append-only DAG-CBOR journal of object turns. Every resident, room, bell, commonplace book, automaton, and rule is an Object with four components:

  1. Identity (id): A human-meaningful path such as garden, bell/1, avatar/ember, or kind/bell.
  2. Content-Addressed Objective Bend Closure (pin): Built from world/lib/, the 9 core object archetypes in world/objects/ (Record.obend, Thing.obend, Kind.obend, Avatar.obend, Library.obend, Mending.obend, Policy.obend, Tide.obend, Workshop.obend), and the 18 domain bodies in world/bodies/ (automatafl.obend, bell.obend, board.obend, clock.obend, commonplace.obend, counter.obend, deal.obend, door.obend, fountain.obend, garden.obend, guestbook.obend, lantern.obend, letter.obend, loom.obend, mirror.obend, oracle.obend, shelf.obend, weathervane.obend), composed via Faré-style prototype inheritance (fix / mix) and Spween declarative scene graphs (world/station.spween).
  3. Canonical DAG-CBOR State (stateCid): Conforming to the object's compiled Objective Bend state type and relational uniqueness keys.
  4. Modal Clause Text (law / rule / want): Parsed and denoted by a single unified grammar in spec/bend/Compiler/ObjectiveBendLaw.lean.

6.2 The Unified Modal Clause Language (ObjectiveBendLaw.lean)

In /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/bend/Compiler/ObjectiveBendLaw.lean, every object carries line-oriented clauses in three modalities:

  • law NAME "reading": EXPR — Refuses a write at commit time unless $\varphi$ holds ([write]φ).
  • rule NAME "reading": EXPR then SPELL — Fires at commit time under the rule owner's authority when $\varphi$ holds ([write]φ then σ), casting SPELL (delvetalk ... with interpolated {TERM} holes).
  • want NAME "reading": EXPR asks SPELL — Poses a standing desire evaluated at read time against the world clock (φ standing), surfacing SPELL on the object's card and the town's daily chronicle as an action wanting a hand.
lean
-- /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/bend/Compiler/ObjectiveBendLaw.lean:89-190
inductive LawFact where
  | subject | caller | height | turn | pin | kind | method | verb | clock | object | laws | refusedBy
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr, Inhabited

inductive LawSide where
  | new | old | cited (object : String)
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr, Inhabited

mutual
inductive LawTerm where
  | field (side : LawSide) (name : String)
  | fact (fact : LawFact)
  | count (side : LawSide) (field : String) (filter : LawFilter)
  | int (value : Int)
  | text (text : String)
  | sum (left right : LawTerm)
  | mod (term : LawTerm) (modulus : Nat)

inductive LawFilter where
  | all
  | eq (column : String) (value : LawTerm)
  | and (left right : LawFilter)
end

inductive LawRule where
  | monotone | writeOnce | appendOnly | unchanged | insertOnly
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr, Inhabited

inductive LawExpr where
  | eq (left right : LawTerm) | ne (left right : LawTerm)
  | lt (left right : LawTerm) | le (left right : LawTerm)
  | gt (left right : LawTerm) | ge (left right : LawTerm)
  | member (term : LawTerm) (place : LawPlace)
  | rule (rule : LawRule) (field : String)
  | changed (field column : String) (who : LawTerm)
  | changedTerm (term : LawTerm)
  | under (object : String)
  | wants (object name : String)
  | not (body : LawExpr) | and (left right : LawExpr)
  | or (left right : LawExpr) | implies (premise conclusion : LawExpr)
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr, Inhabited

6.3 Stroke-Verified LLM Enchantment (Host/Enchant.lean) and Cross-Edition World Carry (Host/Carry.lean)

Two signature subsystems in spec/Delvetalk/Host/ solve the twin problems of hallucination-free LLM narrative rendering and zero-loss live world upgrades:

  1. Stroke-Checked Enchantment (/Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/Delvetalk/Host/Enchant.lean):
  2. Every act on a thing leaves structured, host-assembled Strokes (Line, Mark, Quantity, Verb, Rule, Want, Door, tone, fields) and span-bounded Telling records (away since the reader's last departure or before covering the place's prior history).
  3. When the world's model paints a prose card (maxPaint = 400, maxDay = 600, maxAway = 300, maxBefore = 500), Enchant.verify checks the generated prose against Strokes: every number must match an authorized Quantity beside its unit, every quoted phrase or handle must come from Strokes, and the exact factual card always stands beneath the paint.
lean
   -- /Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/Delvetalk/Host/Enchant.lean:87-120
   structure Telling where
     form    : String
     since   : Nat
     upto    : Nat
     exact   : List String := []
     history : String := ""

   structure Strokes where
     object  : String
     version : Nat
     kind    : String := ""
     voice   : String := ""
     about   : String := ""
     head    : String := ""
     fields  : List (String × String) := []
     tone    : String := ""
     maker   : String := ""
  1. Cross-Edition World Carry (/Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/Delvetalk/Host/Carry.lean):
  2. When the delvetalk-obend binary advances to a new replay edition (e.g., edition 19 → edition 20), delvetalk-obend carry reads the snapshot taken at the old journal's head (readWorld), recompiles every object against the new world/lib and world/bodies (rebuilt), runs package migrate(old: State8) -> State functions when state schemas evolve, verifies that every suspended continuation checkpoint decodes under the new compiled packet (resumable), and preserves every historical receipt slug (Host/Slug.lean, e.g. nosap-hodaz) so old links resolve seamlessly as "entry $N$ of the world before the carry".
  1. Multi-World Serving & AT Protocol Federation (/Users/ember/dev/delvetalk2/spec/Delvetalk/Serve.lean):
  2. Serve.lean serves the main persistent world, on-demand scratch worlds (isolated ephemeral forks where residents or agents can rehearse spells and laws before casting them in town), and a complete AT Protocol PDS/Repository (did:web:gsb.fg-goose.online) with k256 repo signing, Merkle Search Trees (MST), CARv1 export, and 18 online.fg-goose.gsb.* lexicons.

7.1 drorb (618 Commits): Verified Post-Quantum Edge Server & Tailscale Control/Data Plane

/Users/ember/dev/drorb (618 commits, HEAD ec01e68, October 6, 2026; detailed in GOAL.md) is a formally verified reverse proxy, TLS 1.3 / HTTP/2 / HTTP/3 / WebSocket edge terminator, and complete Tailscale (ts2021) control and data plane written in Lean 4, lowered through HOL4 Pancake (datacake), and driven by a thin Rust io_uring / kqueue ring I/O wrapper.

Key verified subsystems in /Users/ember/dev/drorb/:

  1. Verified Post-Quantum & Classical Cryptography:
  2. All 6 NIST PQ operations—ML-KEM-768 (keygen, encaps, decaps) and ML-DSA-65 (keygen, sign, verify)—plus FIPS 202 Keccak-f[1600] (rc_lanes_eq_exec proved by Lean kernel decide with zero axioms), X25519, ChaCha20-Poly1305, AES-GCM, and HKDF-SHA256/384.
  3. Verified HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2 (H2Ingress), HTTP/3, and Braid Calculus:
  4. Reactor/BraidCalculus.lean proves compositional non-interference (braid_gate, braid_transform) across 25+ middleware gates (forward-auth 401, connection-cap 503, stick-table 429, slowloris 408, conditional 304, redirect 308, method-filter 405, body-size 413, Host-allowlist 421, response-splitting and bare-LF smuggling rejection, and H2.Conn.feed_congr).
  5. Complete Tailscale (ts2021) Control & Data Plane:
  6. Implements the Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s ts2021 control framing, DERP relay server/client over verified TLS, STUN binding responder, Disco ping/pong/call-me-maybe cross-NAT hole punching, IPAM (100.64.0.0/10), drorb-ctl CLI, and a verified HuJSON ACL compiler (servedMapFor_filter_is_acl_compile proving that the MapResponse packet filter sent to peers equals the compiled ACL policy).

7.3 /Users/ember/dev/DreggNet (520 Commits): Verified Pancake Reactor DSL & Execution Cloud

/Users/ember/dev/DreggNet (520 commits, HEAD 2c574b6; ARCHITECTURE.md) combines a 6-crate Rust execution cloud with a Lean 4 reactor compiler in Dsl/:

  1. Lean 4 Pancake Reactor Compiler (Dsl/Reactor.lean & Dsl/EmitPancake.lean):
  2. Models zero-copy io_uring / kqueue event loops as typed ReactorComponent state machines mapping RingEvent → RingSubmission, and compiles them to HOL4 Pancake ASTs (PExpr, PStmt, PFun, emitRegion):
lean
   -- /Users/ember/dev/DreggNet/Dsl/Reactor.lean:42-118
   /-- Copy-once buffer recycling law: every completed recv buffer slot is either
       recycled immediately into a send submission or returned to the free ring
       without double-free or leak. -/
   theorem reactor_prim_recycle
       (c : ReactorComponent) (s : RingState) (ev : RingEvent)
       (hwf : RingState.WF s) :
       let (s', sub) := c.step s ev
       BufferConserved s s' sub

   theorem reactor_prim_wf
       (c : ReactorComponent) (s : RingState) (ev : RingEvent)
       (hwf : RingState.WF s) :
       RingState.WF (c.step s ev).1
  1. The 6 Rust Cloud Crates:
  2. dreggnet-exec: Pure-Rust fuel-metered wasmi WebAssembly sandbox executing workload bundles.
  3. dreggnet-bridge: Watches Dregg journal cells for funded execution-lease requests, dispatches them to dreggnet-exec, and submits signed execution receipts back to the journal.
  4. dreggnet-control: Userspace boringtun WireGuard mesh controller and peer router.
  5. dreggnet-gateway: Fly.io-compatible HTTP/TLS edge gateway routing into mesh workers.
  6. dreggnet-provider & dreggnet-webapp: Provider daemon and operator dashboard.

7.4 /Users/ember/dev/DreggCloud (88 Commits): The House / World / Room Sovereign Firmament

/Users/ember/dev/DreggCloud (88 commits, HEAD e9ff17a, August 29, 2026; README.md, REORIENT.md, HORIZONLOG.md) is the sovereign hosting firmament organized around the House / World / Room ontology in crates/dreggcloud-house:

  • House: The operator's cryptographic custody root, append-only transition journal (Through House Schema v16), and EmitEvent commitment anchor. Every owner lifecycle command is signed with Cipherclerk hybrid Ed25519 + ML-DSA keys (with zero-domain owner signatures strictly refused as of 7a77652).
  • World: A mounted semantic world image (a Dregg store, a Universe dungeon world, or a prepaid service archive) admitted into House custody by exact content digest.
  • Room: An isolated capability workspace or live Sandstorm grain mounted inside a World, rendered to browsers via DEOS Shadow DOM view trees (dc-delivery-*).

Three major custody and serving welds landed at the top of DreggCloud:

  1. House Schema v14 — Live MPC-TLS Evidence (notary-selfhost & notary-live-client, 8ee7127):
  2. Mounts live TLSNotary MPC-TLS attestations (verifying GitHub repository ownership and AWS Bedrock model responses) directly into House custody transitions.
  3. House Schema v15 — Exact Universe World Custody (universe-house-custody/src/lib.rs, 2616663):
  4. Defines AdmitUniverseWorldV1 and ExactUniverseWorldArtifactV1, binding a deterministic dungeon-on-dregg Universe world snapshot (world_id, manifest_sha256, state_root, tick_height) into the House journal.
  5. House Schema v16 — Prepaid Service Archive Custody (service-house-custody/src/lib.rs, e9ff17a & 6aaee3b):
  6. Defines AdmitServiceArchiveV1 and ExactServiceArchiveArtifactV1, mounting prepaid compute/storage service archives into House schema 16 and deterministically reopening remaining prepaid capacity across daemon restarts via offline audit verification.
  7. grain-serve — The sandstorm-serve Capability Weld (grain-serve/src/lib.rs, 31079cb):
  8. Wraps the upstream sandstorm-serve grain launcher behind a House-governed BearerGate and AssuranceRung classifier, ensuring that every HTTP/WebSocket request entering a live Sandstorm grain carries an unforgeable House capability token and records its assurance rung.
